L49: ## 1 Executive Summary
L50: ## 1.1 Product Introduction
L51: Imlunestrant is an orally bioavailable, estrogen receptor (ER) antagonist that binds to ER\alpha. It is a new molecular entity. Imlunestrant currently has not been approved for any indication worldwide.
L52: The Applicant proposed the following indication for NDA 218881:
L53: *Imlunestrant is an estrogen receptor antagonist indicated for treatment of adults with ER-positive, HER2-negative, ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer, previously treated with an endocrine-based regimen.
L54: The recommended indication for approval is:
L55: *Imlunestrant is indicated for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor-1 (ESR1*)-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.
L56: The recommended dosage for imlunestrant is 400 mg orally once daily, without food (at least 2 hours before food, or 1 hour after food).
L57: ## 1.2 Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L58: The review team recommends granting regular approval for imlunestrant for the treatment of adults with ER-positive, HER2-negative, *ESR1*-mutated (*ESR1m*) advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy, in accordance with 21 Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) 314.126(a)(b).
L59: Substantial evidence of effectiveness for this application is based on efficacy and safety results from the EMBER-3 trial, a multicenter, open label, active-control, 3-arm trial in 874 patients (323 with *ESR1m* disease) randomized to imlunestrant monotherapy, a treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm of endocrine therapy of either fulvestrant or exemestane, or a third arm of imlunestrant plus abemaciclib, in patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer previously treated with an AI, with or without a CDK4/6 inhibitor. Patients were excluded if they were eligible to receive a PARP inhibitor. The review for this NDA application was focused on the imlunestrant monotherapy arm vs. the TPC arm as these were the relevant arms for the proposed indication.
L60: The primary endpoints were investigator assessed progression free survival (PFS) in the *ESR1m* population and the ITT population (*ESR1m* and *ESR1m*-not detected). The secondary endpoints were overall survival (OS) in the *ESR1m* and ITT populations. Randomization was stratified by previous treatment with a CDK4/6 inhibitor (yes vs no), presence of visceral metastasis (yes vs no), and region (East Asia vs North America/Western Europe vs Others). *ESR1* mutational status was determined by blood circulating tumor deoxyribonucleic acid (ctDNA) using the Guardant360 CDx assay and was limited to specific *ESR1* mutations in the ligand binding domain.
L61: EMBER-3 demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS by investigator in the *ESR1m* population in this replacement trial. Median PFS was 5.5 months versus 3.8 months (HR 0.62, 95% CI: 0.46-0.82; p-value 0.0008). Results from the secondary endpoints of PFS by blinded independent review committee (BIRC), objective response rate (ORR) and duration of response (DoR) in the *ESR1m* population appeared to be supportive of the primary endpoint results. The PFS endpoint in the ITT population did not cross the prespecified significance alpha level and therefore was not statistically significant. The secondary OS endpoint in the *ESR1m* population was immature (31% maturity), and the interim analysis of OS at the time of final PFS analysis was not statistically significant. However, there was no evidence of detriment in the *ESR1m* population (HR 0.91, 95% CI: 0.56-1.47). OS could not be tested in the ITT population since PFS in the overall population failed to meet the prespecified threshold.
L62: The FDA review team conducted an exploratory analysis of investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-not detected (*ESR1m*-nd) population and excluded 20 patients whose *ESR1* mutation status was failed or missing (n=385). The HR was 1.04 (95% CI: 0.82, 1.33). The observed median PFS was 5.6 (95% CI: 3.8, 7.3) months in the imlunestrant arm and 5.6 (95% CI: 5.5, 7.4) months in the TPC arm.
L63: The Applicant removed (b) (4) before the trial started enrolling. (b) (4) patient demographics and baseline disease characteristics were generally well balanced between treatment arms in the *ESR1m* patient population. Additionally, prespecified sensitivity analysis using a multivariate Cox regression model adjusted for baseline covariates appeared to show treatment benefit consistent with the primary analysis results.
L64: While exploratory analyses by individual *ESR1* mutations may suggest some differential efficacy for imlunestrant, the subgroups were small and exploratory, and the evidence supports a consistent treatment benefit for patients with specific *ESR1* mutations.
L65: The majority of adverse reactions associated with imlunestrant in EMBER-3 were Grade 1-2 in severity, as would be anticipated with an endocrine therapy. However, notably GI toxicities were higher for imlunestrant compared to TPC, including diarrhea (22% vs. 12%), nausea (17% vs. 13%), constipation (10% vs. 6%), and abdominal pain (10% vs. 6%). Fatigue was also higher for imlunestrant compared to TPC (23% vs. 14%). The FDA review team also reviewed dyslipidemia as an adverse event of special interest, as this has been noted with another approved oral SERD. While the incidence was similar for increased triglycerides and increased cholesterol between the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm, it was still notable to include these incidences in Section 6 of the US Prescribing information (USPI) for providers and patients to be aware to monitor for these during treatment.
L66: Based on an overall favorable benefit:risk analysis, the review team recommends granting regular approval to imlunestrant in the *ESR1m* population. This indication was modified by FDA from the Applicant proposed indication to accurately reflect the patient population enrolled in the EMBER-3 trial. The indication for approval is:
L67: *Imlunestrant is indicated for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor-1 (ESR1)-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.*
L68: A supplemental Pre-Market Approval (PMA) Application (sPMA P200010/S021) was submitted to CDRH for the Guardant360 CDx assay. The sPMA will receive contemporaneous approval with this NDA as a companion diagnostic device for imlunestrant.
L69: Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with one adequate and well-controlled clinical investigation and confirmatory evidence.
L70: *Provide a description of the SEE that supported the regulatory action. If an application/ submission is a CR due to lack of established SEE, below description is omitted.*
L71: Refer to Section 8.1 and 8.4.1 of this review for details.
L72: Confirmatory evidence considered supportive for this NDA review included nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology studies, primary pharmacology data of binding affinity for *ESR1m*, PK/PD data for imlunestrant, and the first in human EMBER trial that evaluated initial dose finding for imlunestrant as a monotherapy.
L73: ## 1.3 Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L74: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L75: Breast cancer is the most common cancer in women worldwide. In the U.S., there are 316,950 new cases of breast cancer and 42,170 deaths from breast cancer estimated for 2025. Although rare (&lt;1%), male patients can also develop breast cancer and may present at a higher stage than female patients.
L76: Histopathological subtypes of breast cancer are defined by expression of the estrogen receptor (ER), progesterone receptor (PR), and/or human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2). Approximately 70% of patients with breast cancer will have ER-positive, HER2-negative disease. Although associated with a better prognosis than other breast cancer subtypes, if ER-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer is advanced and not surgically resectable, or metastatic, it is incurable and associated with a limited life expectancy.
L77: The preferred first-line treatment for patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer in the U.S. is a CDK4/6 inhibitor in combination with endocrine therapy (an AI or fulvestrant). At disease progression, FDA-approved options for second and later-line treatment options may include endocrine monotherapy with fulvestrant, an AI, or tamoxifen (if not received as first-line treatment); everolimus in combination with endocrine therapy; targeted therapy such alpelisib in combination with fulvestrant in *PIK3CA*-mutated tumors, or capivasertib plus fulvestrant for *PIK3CA/AKT1/PTEN*-altered tumors, or PARP inhibitors for *BRCA*-mutated tumors; and chemotherapy such as capecitabine. Inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant was approved in 2024 for adults with endocrine-resistant, *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy.
L78: Acquired resistance to endocrine therapy, particularly AIs, can occur due to activating missense mutations and in-frame deletions in
L79: the ligand-binding domain (LBD) of *ESR1*. It is estimated that approximately 10-50% of patients exposed to an AI for treatment of metastatic breast cancer will acquire tumor *ESR1* mutation(s). Elacestrant, on oral SERD, was approved in January 2023 for the treatment of postmenopausal women or adult men with ER-positive, HER2-negative, *ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy. There remains an unmet need for additional therapies that may offer an additional option to improve clinical outcomes in all patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer, including patients with *ESR1* mutations.
L80: The efficacy and safety assessment for imlunestrant is primarily based on data from the EMBER-3 trial, a multicenter, active-controlled, open-label 3-arm trial in 874 patients (323 with *ESR1m* disease) randomized to imlunestrant monotherapy, a treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm of endocrine therapy with either fulvestrant or exemestane, or a third arm with an additional investigational combination regimen, in patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer previously treated with an AI, with or without a CDK4/6 inhibitor. The results from the combination arm are not part of this NDA review. Patients continued treatment until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoints were investigator assessed PFS in the *ESR1m* population and the ITT population (*ESR1m* and *ESR1m*-not detected). The secondary endpoints were OS in the *ESR1m* and ITT populations. Approximately 67% of patients in the imlunestrant arm received prior CDK4/6 inhibitor in any setting vs. 72% of patients in the TPC arm in the *ESR1m* population.
L81: EMBER-3 demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS by investigator assessment in the *ESR1m* population. Median PFS was 5.5 months versus 3.8 months (HR 0.62, 95% CI: 0.46-0.82; p-value 0.0008). The PFS endpoint in the ITT population did not cross the prespecified significance alpha level and therefore was not statistically significant. The secondary OS endpoint in the *ESR1m* population was immature (31% maturity) and not statistically significant. However, there was no detriment in the *ESR1m* population (HR 0.55, 95% CI: 0.35-0.86). A postmarketing commitment (PMC) has been issued by the FDA and agreed to by the Applicant to obtain the next prespecified interim and final OS results for the *ESR1m* population. OS could not be tested in the ITT population since PFS in the overall population failed to meet the prespecified threshold.
L82: The safety profile of imlunestrant was generally reflective of an endocrine therapy, with mostly Grade 1-2 ARs. Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 1.8% of patients who received imlunestrant, including cardiac arrest, acute myocardial infarction, right ventricular failure, hypovolemic shock, and upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage (each 0.3%), plus an additional death thought to be unrelated (dengue fever). Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 0.9% of patients who received TPC, including cardiac arrest (0.6%) and abdominal infection (0.3%). Serious adverse reactions (SARs) occurred in 10% of patients who received imlunestrant and in 11% of patient who received TPC. SARs in &gt; 1% of patients receiving imlunestrant included pleural effusion (1.2%) and in TPC included fracture (1.5%). The most common (incidence \ge10%) ARs, including laboratory abnormalities, were decreased hemoglobin, musculoskeletal pain, decreased calcium, decreased neutrophils, increased AST, fatigue, diarrhea, increased ALT, increased triglycerides, nausea, decreased platelets, constipation, increased cholesterol, and abdominal pain. Imlunestrant was associated with increased GI toxicity compared to TPC, including diarrhea (22% vs. 12%), nausea (17% vs. 13%), constipation (10% vs. 6%), and abdominal pain (10% vs. 6%). Dyslipidemia incidence was similar for increased triglycerides (21% vs. 22%) and increased cholesterol (10% vs. 12%) between the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm, and given the trial was an active controlled trial, it was deemed reasonable to include these incidences in labeling for providers and patients to be aware of the need to monitor these during treatment with imlunestrant.
L83: Male patients were eligible for enrollment on EMBER-3; however, none were enrolled in the *ESR1m* population. The FDA included male patients in the approved indication based on extrapolation from female patient data, supported by both biological rationale and regulatory precedent. The mechanism of action of imlunestrant, estrogen receptor antagonist, supports that it should work regardless of patient sex. The FDA's guidance on male breast cancer specifically supports this extrapolation approach, recognizing the practical impossibility of conducting adequately powered studies in this rare population while ensuring that male patients have access to effective treatments (FDA Guidance: Male Breast Cancer, 2020).
L84: The FDA review team concluded that for patients in the *ESR1m* population, the benefit-risk assessment for imlunestrant was favorable given the statistically significant improvement in PFS, which was robust to multiple sensitivity analyses and supported by a positive OS HR trend (despite OS being immature). Although the magnitude of improvement in median PFS was small, it should be interpreted in the context of a replacement design and the fact that the HR result was robust. The toxicities were considered
L85: acceptable given that clinical benefit was demonstrated.
L86: At the time of the EMBER-3 trial design (early 2021), single-agent endocrine therapy represented an acceptable standard of care option for second line therapy. Elacestrant, another SERD, was approved in U.S. for ER-positive, HER2-negative, *ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer while this trial was enrolling; however, elacestrant was not available in most countries when the trial was enrolling. Subsequently, it has been noted in trials that fulvestrant monotherapy underperforms after progression on a CDK4/6i therapy. More than half of patients on this trial had prior CDK4/6i therapy. There is still an unmet medical need in this patient population despite the recent approvals and the FDA does not require comparative effectiveness studies against all available therapies for regular approval, and the chosen comparator was appropriate when the trial was initiated.
L87: FDA also revised the Applicant proposed indication to reflect the patient population enrolled in EMBER-3 (i.e., disease progression following at least 1 line of endocrine therapy). Based on the overall benefit-risk assessment, the review team recommends regular approval for the following indication:
L88: *Imlunestrant is indicated for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor-1 (ESR1)-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.*
L89: Patients should be selected for treatment using the Guardant360 CDx assay. The Guardant360 CDx assay is a qualitative next generation sequencing (NGS)-based in vitro diagnostic device which uses targeted high throughput hybridization-based capture technology for the detection of single nucleotide variants (SNVs) and insertions/deletions (indels) in 55 genes, copy number amplifications (CNAs) in 2 genes, and fusions in 4 genes. The sPMA P200010/S021 will receive contemporaneous approval with this NDA as a companion diagnostic device for imlunestrant. The specific *ESR1* mutations detected by Guardant360 CDx for the imlunestrant indication include: E380 mutations, V422 deletion, S463P/F, L469V, L536 mutations, Y537 mutations, and D538
L90: mutations.
L91: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Breast cancer is the most common cancer in women, with 316,950 new cases estimated and 42,170 deaths estimated from breast cancer in the U.S. in 2025. Although rare (<1%), breast cancer can also occur in males.
Approximately 70% of patients with breast cancer will have ER+HER2- disease.
Acquired ESR1-mutations are estimated to range anywhere from 10-50% in the advanced and metastatic setting after prior exposure to prior endocrine therapy, especially aromatase inhibitors.
Advanced or metastatic breast cancer is incurable.
ER-positive, HER2-negative (ESR1)-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer is a serious and life-threatening condition.
Current Treatment Options	Advanced or metastatic breast cancer remains incurable. Treatment is palliative, with the aims of reducing cancer-related symptoms, delaying disease progression, and prolonging survival.
Following treatment with a CDK4/6 inhibitor in combination with endocrine therapy, treatment options may include endocrine monotherapy with fulvestrant, an AI, or tamoxifen; everolimus in combination with endocrine therapy; targeted therapy such alpelisib in combination with fulvestrant in
All treatment options are palliative. There is an unmet medical need to improve outcomes in patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer with ESR1-mutated tumors.
L92: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
PIK3CA-mutated tumors, or capivasertib plus fulvestrant for PIK3CA/AKT1/PTEN-altered tumors, or PARP inhibitors for BRCA-mutated tumors; and chemotherapy such as capecitabine.
Elaestrant, on oral SERD, was approved January 2023 for treatment of postmenopausal women or adult men with ER-positive, HER2-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.

Benefit	The EMBER-3 trial enrolled 874 patients randomized to imlunestrant monotherapy, a treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm of endocrine therapy of either fulvestrant or exemestane, or a third arm of an additional investigational combination regimen, in patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer previously treated with an AI, with or without a CDK4/6 inhibitor.
There were 323 patients enrolled with ESR1m disease across all 3 arms, with 256 patients with ESR1m in total enrolled in the imlunestrant monotherapy arm and TPC arm.
In the ESR1m population, median PFS by investigator was 5.5 months versus 3.8 months (HR 0.62, 95% CI: 0.46-0.82; p-value 0.0008).
PFS by BICR results were supportive of the primary endpoint (HR 0.66, 95% CI: 0.47, 0.92).
For patients in the ESR1-mutated population, imlunestrant demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS by investigator, which was supported by a favorable trend in OS and secondary endpoints such as PFS by BICR.
L93: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
At the planned interim analysis for OS, the OS did not reach statistical significance (HR 0.55, 95% CI: 0.35-0.86).

Risk and Risk Management	Serious adverse reactions (SARs) occurred in 10% of patients who received imlunestrant and 11% in patients who received TPC. SARs in > 1% of patients receiving imlunestrant included pleural effusion (1.2%).
Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 1.8% of patients who received imlunestrant (cardiac arrest, acute myocardial infarction, right ventricular failure, hypovolemic shock, and upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage) and 0.9% of those receiving TPC (cardiac arrest and abdominal infection).
Imlunestrant was associated with increased GI toxicity compared to TPC, including diarrhea (22% vs. 12%), nausea (17% vs. 13%), constipation (10% vs. 6%), and abdominal pain (10% vs. 6%).
The most common (incidence \ge10%) adverse reactions, including laboratory abnormalities were hemoglobin decreased, musculoskeletal pain, calcium decreased, neutrophils decreased, AST increased, fatigue, diarrhea, ALT increased, triglycerides increased, nausea, platelets decreased, constipation, cholesterol increased, and abdominal pain.
Embryo-Fetal Toxicity was included under Warnings and Precautions.
Safe use of imlunestrant in this population can be managed with labeling.
There is no indication for a REMS.

L103: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L104: ## 2.1 Analysis of Condition
L105: ## The Applicant's Position:
L106: It is estimated that 2.3 million new cases of breast cancer occurred worldwide in women in 2022 (Bray et al. 2024). Treatment options for patients with breast cancer are largely determined by ER and HER2 status (Waks and Winer 2019; Cardoso et al. 2024; NCCN 2024). ER-driven breast cancers remain the most frequent cancers among women and are a major cause of cancer-related deaths worldwide (Bray et al. 2024).
L107: ER is expressed in more than two thirds of breast cancers, and advances in ET targeting the ER have reduced the risk of recurrence in EBC and prolonged survival in advanced or MBC, henceforth referred to as ABC. However, ER+ ABC is incurable with a median OS of 3 to 5 years (Hortobagyi et al. 2022; Meegdes et al. 2023; Cardoso et al. 2024), representing an unmet medical need to optimize ET options as monotherapy and in combination with targeted therapy.
L108: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L109: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's characterization of advanced and metastatic ER-positive (ER+), HER2-negative (HER2-) breast cancer. Advanced and metastatic ER+ breast cancer is incurable, and the goal of therapy is palliative.
L110: ## 2.2 Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L111: ## The Applicant's Position:
L112: Medicines that target ER, through inhibition of ligand (estrogen) synthesis, direct receptor antagonism, or receptor degradation, are the most crucial component of treatment (Patel et al. 2023; Jhaveri et al. 2024). For patients with ER+, HER2- ABC, standard of care (Cardoso et al. 2024; NCCN 2024) includes giving
L113: - single-agent ET to inhibit ER signaling, such asan AI (anastrozole, letrozole, exemestane)
- a SERM (tamoxifen), or
- a SERD (fulvestrant or elacestrant [for tumors harboring *ESR1* mutations]), or
- combinations of ET (with the exception of elacestrant) witha CDK4/6i (abemaciclib, palbociclib, or ribociclib)
- an mTOR inhibitor (everolimus)
L114: - a PI3K inhibitor (alpelisib, for tumors harboring *PIK3CA*-activating mutations)
- an AKT inhibitor (capivasertib, for tumors harboring *PIK3CA*- or *AKT1*-activating mutations or *PTEN* alterations), or
- a PARP inhibitor (olaparib or talazoparib, for tumors harboring germline *BRCA1* or *BRCA2* mutations).
L115: Most patients receive an AI combined with a CDK4/6i or single-agent ET in the first-line setting. Subsequent treatment involves changing the ET backbone generally to IM SERD (fulvestrant) given alone or combined with targeted agents (alpelisib or capivasertib) based on presence of targetable tumor mutations or with everolimus in a biomarker-unselected population. However, per NCCN guidelines and current U.S. practice, some patients may receive ET monotherapy in the ABC setting (NCCN 2024).
L116: Some patients may receive chemotherapy (Cardoso et al. 2024; NCCN 2024)
L117: - as initial therapy based on (disease burden and clinical signs and symptoms suggesting visceral crisis) aggressiveness at initial presentation
- for endocrine refractory disease, involving the use of antibody-drug conjugates, or
- as salvage therapy following failure of ET-based interventions.
L118: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L119: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the current treatment landscape for advanced and metastatic ER+, HER2-, breast cancer. Endocrine therapy [an aromatase inhibitor (AI) or fulvestrant] in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor is the preferred first-line therapy with some combinations demonstrating an overall survival benefit versus placebo (Hortobaygi et al. 2022). CDK4/6 inhibitors are approved with fulvestrant following disease progression on endocrine therapy. Together this suggests that patients should receive a CDK4/6i with ET with or without a PI3K inhibitor in the first or second-line of therapy. The choice of therapy in later lines of ET depends on prior therapy and may include ET combinations (e.g., everolimus in combination with exemestane), and targeted therapy alone or in combinations (*ESR1* mutated, *PIK3CA* mutated, *AKT1* mutated, *PTEN* alteration, or *BRCA1* and *BRCA2* mutated), and endocrine monotherapy. The evidence for ET monotherapy is weaker but is indicated for a non-biomarker selected population.
L120: Elacestrant was approved in the US on January 27, 2023, for patients with *ESR1*m ABC based on the results of the EMERALD trial which had a control arm of fulvestrant or an aromatase inhibitor. The pivotal study for imlunestrant, EMBER-3, was still enrolling and therefore elacestrant would have been available therapy for patients in the US (Shah, et al. 2024). The ideal sequencing of therapies for patients eligible for multiple therapies is currently unknown.
L121: While BRCA testing was not required for study eligibility, patients with a known or suspected deleterious BRCA mutation were excluded if they were enrolled in a region where PARP inhibitors were approved and available. Patients who were assessed to have visceral crisis or inflammatory breast cancer and therefore eligible for chemotherapy were similarly excluded.

L127: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L137: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L138: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's review of key regulatory interactions with the following clarifications.
L139: The Applicant originally proposed including (b) (4) in their pre-IND meeting package submitted December 23, 2020, to FDA. After receiving FDA preliminary responses, which included standard comments regarding the possible need for a companion diagnostic (CDx), the Applicant conveyed (b) (4)
L140: At the Type B, EOP1 teleconference held on February 11, 2021, the FDA reiterated that the need for a CDx would depend on the results of the trial and encouraged the Applicant to make provisions if this were needed. (b) (4) FDA recommended prespecifying *ESR1* missense mutations that were considered activating, consideration in the trial design that not all *ESR1m* may be clinically equivalent, that multiple co-occurring *ESR1m* may influence
L141: efficacy, and that the effect of alternative splicing of *ESR1* should be considered. Despite this advice, (b) (4) when the protocol was submitted. The Applicant ultimately submitted a proposed indication for an *ESR1m* population when the NDA was submitted in October 2024.
L142: On December 20, 2024, the FDA recommended against adding an interim analysis of OS after data unblinding as it would be considered exploratory only and may be data driven. The Applicant chose to add this interim OS analysis despite FDA's comments. See details in Section 8.1.1.
L143: Regulatory feedback regarding Arm C was not reviewed as it is beyond the scope of the proposed indication for this NDA review.
L144: ## 4 Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L145: ## 4.1 Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L146: For clinical inspections, 3 clinical sites were selected in conjunction with OSI for FDA inspection based on being high enrolling sites for the EMBER-3 trial. In addition, the Applicant (Eli Lilly and Company), was also selected for inspection. The full report was completed by Dr. Leigh Marcus from FDA OSI and is summarized below. Overall, the inspection findings were acceptable and did not alter the conclusions from the NDA review.
L147: ## **1. Dr. Monica Lis Casalnuovo (Site #33334)**
L148: Buenos Aires, Argentina
L149: ***Inspection Dates: 4/1/2025, 4/3/2025 – 4/4/2025, 4/7/2025***
L150: This was the first FDA inspection for this clinical investigator. At this site, 15 patients were screened and 9 were enrolled to the trial (5 with tumors that were *ESR1-mut*). During the inspection, the primary efficacy endpoint data were verifiable. There was no underreporting of adverse events (AEs) or serious AEs (SAEs). It was noted that Informed consent Documents (ICDs) did not contain the required statement related to ClinicalTrials.gov in any of their versions of the main ICD document. There was a discussion to ensure the site has the required statement related to clinicaltrials.gov in all versions of the main informed consent form going forward.
L151: *Clinical Reviewer: The findings from this site did not alter the benefit: risk of this application.*
L152: ## **2. Dr. Jose Angel Garcia Saenz (Site #74489)**
L153: Madrid, Spain
L154: ***Inspection Dates: 4/1/2025, 4/3/2025 – 4/4/2025, 4/7/2025***
L155: This was the first FDA inspection for this clinical investigator. At this site, 15 patients were screened and 12 were enrolled to the trial (4 with tumors that were *ESR1-mut*). The primary efficacy endpoint data were verifiable. All AEs and SAEs were reported from the site. However, there were SAE's that were not reported within the 24-hour time frame during the follow-up period (Subject # (b)(6) [emesis, and COVID-19] and Subject # (b)(6) ["pharmacological pneumonitis" defined as drug-induced pneumonitis, a type of
L156: Inluriyo (imlunestrant)
L157: interstitial lung disease). The site team had training to make sure that even in the follow-up phase that SAEs must be reported to the sponsor within the 24-hour timeframe.
L158: *Clinical Reviewer: The findings from this site did not alter the benefit: risk of this application. Late reported AEs were still captured and reviewed in the NDA with appropriate labeling.*
L159: ## **3. Dr. Komal Jhaveri, (Site #99301)**
L160: New Jersey, United States
L161: *Inspection Dates: 3/10/2025 – 3/14/2025*
L162: This investigator has been inspected previously for a different application in July 2024. Prior FDA inspection in showed an outdated protocol version being used, 2 minor AEs and a concomitant medication was not reported for 1 subject, and a discrepancy in eligibility status was observed in one patient. For the current inspection, 21 patients were screened and 19 were enrolled to the trial (5 with tumors that were *ESR1-mut*). The primary efficacy endpoint data were verifiable. There was underreporting of one AE in 1 patient (Subject # (b)(6) “hip pain” missing from the sponsor data listing. There was no evidence of under-reporting of SAEs. An outdated ICD was used for one patient (Subject # (b)(6)), and the site did not properly calculate the proper window of dates to obtain scans which resulted 2 patients having multiple “out of window (OOW)” +/- 3-day scans, 1 of whom with the *ESR1-mutation* (Subject # (b)(6)) in the indicated population. The underreported AE in Subject # (b)(6) was added into electronic data capture (EDC) by the site after data cut-off and the AE (hip pain) was likely due to underlying cancer. The OOW scans were off by 1-3 days, therefore no clinical significance was seen. Protocol deviations were filed for all OOW assessments. Subject # (b)(6) was reconsented with the current ICD after the inspection. The site agreed to no longer share documents on an open electronic platform, and in the future, will only be performing e-consent via their EPIC electronic medical record (EMR) software.
L163: *Clinical Reviewer: The issues found regarding underreporting of an AE in one patient and use of outdated informed consent document did not alter the FDA review teams conclusion of the benefit: risk for this NDA review.*
L164: ## **4. Eli Lilly and Company (Applicant)**
L165: Lilly Corporate Center
Indianapolis, Indiana United States
L166: *Inspection Dates: 3/24/2025 – 3/27/2025*
L167: This inspection covered the Sponsor's study conduct related to the EMBER-3 trial. The previous FDA inspection of this Applicant in September 2022 had no significant regulatory violations. The Applicant had agreements with a CROs to assist in monitoring sites. Four sites: Site #11738, Site #14248, Site #29299, and Site #96527, were reviewed for monitoring reports and other documents during this inspection. Site #29299 was put on enrollment hold due to noncompliance, and the site of Dr. Satheesh Thungappa was terminated due to noncompliance by the Applicant due to prior noncompliance on a different trial not related to this NDA application.

L169: *Clinical Reviewer: Concur with FDA OSI findings that the Applicant monitoring for the EMBER-3 trial was adequate and did not alter the conclusions for this NDA review.*
L170: ## 4.2 Product Quality
L171: The FDA Product Quality Team recommended approval for imlunestrant. The review team assessed the drug substance, drug product, quality labeling, manufacturing, and biopharmaceutics for imlunestrant and concluded that the Applicant provided sufficient information to assure the identity, strength, purity, and quality of the proposed product. The review team also concluded that all associated manufacturing, testing, and packaging facilities were acceptable based on prior inspection history. The review team determined that approval of this application is not expected to have significant effects on the quality of the human environment and issued a finding of no significant impact (FONSI). There were no product quality PMRs or PMCs issued. For further detail see the Product Quality review.

L174: ## 4.4 Devices and Companion Diagnostic Issues
L175: CDRH received a supplemental PMA Application (sPMA P200010/S021) from Guardant Health, Inc. for the Guardant360 CDx assay. CDRH considers this sPMA approvable as a companion diagnostic device to aid in detecting specific *ESR1* mutations in patients with breast cancer for treatment with INLURIYO (imlunestrant).
L176: The Guardant360 CDx assay is a qualitative next generation sequencing (NGS)-based in vitro diagnostic device which uses targeted high throughput hybridization-based capture technology for the detection of single nucleotide variants (SNVs) and insertions/deletions (indels) in 55 genes, copy number amplifications (CNAs) in 2 genes, and fusions in 4 genes. The assay uses circulating cell-free DNA (cfDNA) from plasma of peripheral whole blood collected in Streck Cell-Free DNA Blood Collection Tubes (BCTs). This assay is already approved as a companion diagnostic for 5 other drugs:
L177: - TAGRISSO (osimertinib) for NSCLC patients with EGFR exon 19 deletions, L858R, and T790M mutations
- RYBREVANT (amivantamab-vmjw) for NSCLC patients with EGFR exon 20 insertions
- ENHERTU (fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki) for NSCLC patients with *ERBB2/HER2* activating mutations
- LUMAKRAS (sotorasib) for NSCLC patients with KRAS G12C mutations
- ORSERDU (elacestrant) for breast cancer patients with *ESR1* missense mutations between codons 310 and 547
L178: The clinical validity of the Guardant360 CDx assay as a companion diagnostic device for INLURIYO (imlunestrant) is supported by a prospective analysis of plasma samples and clinical outcome data from the EMBER-3 clinical study (NCT04975308). Pre-treatment plasma samples from 630 of the primary EMBER-3 registration population subjects were tested with Guardant360 CDx. Of these, 254 patients (40.3%) had one or more *ESR1* mutation(s) detected. The specific *ESR1* mutations detected by Guardant360 CDx for the imlunestrant indication include: E380 mutations, V422 deletion, S463P/F, L469V, L536 mutations, Y537 mutations, and D538 mutations. The analytical performance of Guardant360 CDx for the detection of specific *ESR1* mutation(s) were assessed and deemed acceptable.
L179: The CDRH review team concluded that both analytical and clinical validation data submitted supported the reasonable assurance of safety and effectiveness of Guardant360 CDx assay as a companion diagnostic device to aid in the selection of patients with breast cancer with detectable *ESR1* mutations in plasma for treatment with INLURIYO (imlunestrant). For details, refer to the Summary of Safety and Effectiveness Data (SSED) for the Guardant360 CDx device.
L180: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L181: ## 5.1 Executive Summary
L182: Imlunestrant (Inluriyo) is an estrogen receptor (ER) antagonist indicated for the treatment of adult patients with ER-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, *ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer, with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy. The recommended dose of Inluriyo is 400 mg orally once daily. The nonclinical development program for imlunestrant consisted of studies in mice, rats, and monkeys to evaluate the pharmacology (primary, secondary, safety), metabolism, general toxicity, embryo-fetal development, and genotoxicity. During the nonclinical development, imlunestrant was referred to as LY3484356 tosylate, LSN3484356, and LSN3513322.
L183: Estrogen receptors (ERs) consist of nuclear ERs, extra-nuclear ERs, and G protein-coupled ERs. Estrogen receptor-alpha (ER\alpha) and estrogen receptor-beta (ER\beta) are located in the nucleus and are encoded by the *ESR1* and *ESR2* genes, respectively. ER\alpha is expressed in breast, prostate, uterus, liver, and bone tissues. Mutations in the *ESR1* gene, such as Y537S/N and D538G, contribute to ER^+HER2^- breast cancer resistance to endocrine therapy.
L184: In primary pharmacology studies, imlunestrant bound to wild-type ER\alpha, mutant (Y537S) ER\alpha, and wild-type ER\beta with Ki values of 0.6 nM, 2.8 nM, and 0.1 nM, respectively. Imlunestrant induced degradation of ER\alpha in breast cancer cell lines with wild-type and *ESR1*-mutated (Y537S/N) ER\alpha and led to inhibition of ER-dependent gene transcription. In vitro, imlunestrant inhibited the proliferation of both ER\alpha wild-type and *ESR1*-mutated (Y537S/N) breast cancer cell lines (IC_{50s} 1.67 to 7 nM). In vivo, daily oral administration of imlunestrant (3 to 30 mg/kg, 28 days) to mice bearing ER\alpha wild-type and *ESR1*-mutated (Y537S) breast cancer xenografts led to \le 83\% tumor growth inhibition (TGI) compared to control.
L185: In secondary pharmacology studies, the binding affinity of imlunestrant to ER was at least 300-fold higher than other nuclear hormone receptors such as human glucocorticoid receptor, androgen receptor, mineralocorticoid receptor, or progesterone receptor (PR) suggesting unlikely off-target activity at clinical concentrations. The Applicant conducted a non-GLP study in immature female rats to evaluate the potential agonistic effects of imlunestrant on ER\alpha. Oral administration of imlunestrant at doses up to 200 mg/kg for 4 days did not result in remarkable changes to uterine weight or the histopathology of uterus, cervix, and vagina when compared to control. On the other hand, positive controls estradiol and tamoxifen led to severely increased uterine weights and histopathology findings in female reproductive organs. The data suggests that imlunestrant does not have agonistic effects on ER\alpha.
L186: Imlunestrant had no remarkable effects on the central nervous and respiratory systems when administered orally once daily to rats up to 150 mg/kg/day for up to 6 months or on the
L187: cardiovascular, central nervous, and respiratory systems when administered orally once daily to cynomolgus monkeys up to 600 mg/kg/day for up to 3 months.
L188: In vitro, imlunestrant demonstrated high plasma protein binding (\ge 99\%) in rat, dog, mouse, monkey, and human plasma. There were no unique human metabolites identified. In repeat dose toxicology studies, exposures (C_{max} and AUC_{0-24}) generally increased with increasing dose in a less than dose proportional manner in rats and approximately dose proportionally in monkeys. Animal to human exposure margins were calculated using the mean steady state clinical C_{max} (141 ng/mL) and AUC_{0-24} (2400 ng*h/mL) at the recommended dose.
L189: Repeat-dose toxicity studies were conducted with imlunestrant in rats (\le 150 mg/kg/day) up to 6-months duration and monkeys (\le 600 mg/kg/day) up to 3-months duration. Recovery groups were only included in the chronic rat study to assess the reversibility of effects following long-term imlunestrant administration. Of note, a 6-month repeat-dose toxicity study is not warranted to support an NDA submission for an advanced cancer indication. In the 6-month repeat-dose toxicity study in rats, mortality occurred at 150 mg/kg/day in 2 females due to urinary obstruction with correlated histopathology findings in the urinary bladder that was likely imlunestrant-related. In addition, early drug-induced mortalities occurred during the 1-month study in male rats at 300 mg/kg/day due to hepatocellular and/or muscular damage with accompanied increase in transaminase and creatine kinase levels. There were no early mortalities in the monkey studies. The major target organs of toxicity in the general rat and monkey toxicology studies were male and female reproductive organs, hematopoietic/lymphatic system, kidney, urinary bladder, and eye and were generally consistent across the short and long-term studies.
L190: Hematologic changes were generally more pronounced in rats than monkeys and included increases in red and white blood cell parameters with microscopic correlates of increased hematopoietic cells in the bone marrow (rat) and macrophage vacuolation in various tissues including gastrointestinal organs. Increased liver enzymes were noted without adverse microscopic correlates in the liver. In rats, kidney tubular degeneration, transitional cell hyperplasia in the urinary bladder, granulosa cell hyperplasia in the ovary, and hyperplasia in the mammary gland were observed at all tested dose levels (\ge 4 times the human AUC at the recommended dose). Findings of hyperplasia were included in Section 13.2 of the label. Additionally, in the 6-month rat study, an increased trend in lens degeneration and corneal opacity was noted at 150 mg/kg/day; however, these findings with similar intensity but lower frequency were noted in the control group and were not observed at higher doses in shorter term studies. Adverse ocular effects were also not observed in patients. Toxicology findings were either fully or partially reversible where recovery groups were assessed.
L191: A dedicated fertility study was not conducted with imlunestrant in accordance with ICH S9 guidance and the intended advanced cancer indication. The Applicant assessed the effects of
L192: imlunestrant on reproductive organs in rats and monkeys as part of the GLP-compliant repeat dose toxicology studies. Daily oral administration of imlunestrant resulted in follicular cysts in the ovary and atrophy in the vagina, cervix, and uterus at doses \ge 10 mg/kg/day in female rats (\ge 4 times the human AUC at the recommended dose) and \ge 15 mg/kg/day in female cynomolgus monkeys (\ge 1 times the human AUC at the recommended dose). Decreased sperm and cellular debris in the epididymis and spermatic retention in the testis were observed in male rats at \ge 10 mg/kg/day. The effects of imlunestrant on male and female reproductive organs were reversible in rats following a 3-month recovery period. Reversibility was not assessed in cynomolgus monkeys.
L193: The Applicant conducted several GLP-compliant phototoxicity studies in BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts, and imlunestrant was considered phototoxic. An additional study was conducted to determine the potential phototoxic effects of imlunestrant on the eyes and skin of Long-Evans pigmented female rats, when administered imlunestrant orally at doses up to 1000 mg/kg for 4 days. The results showed no ocular findings indicative of phototoxicity, but imlunestrant caused grade 1 erythema of skin (up to 4/5 rats) following administration at 1000 mg/kg with UV light exposure (47 times human AUC at the recommended dose). There were no clinical adverse events reported suggestive of phototoxicity.
L194: Imlunestrant was not mutagenic in an in vitro bacterial reverse mutation (Ames) assay. Imlunestrant produced chromosomal damage (increase in the percent micronuclei) in the in vitro micronucleus assay in lymphoblastoid TK6 cells, but it did not cause chromosomal damage in the rat bone marrow micronucleus test or DNA strand breaks in the rat liver and duodenum comet assays in vivo when administered up to 2000 mg/kg. Thus, overall imlunestrant is not considered genotoxic. Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted with imlunestrant in accordance with ICH S9 guidance.
L195: In the embryo-fetal developmental (EFD) study, daily oral administration of imlunestrant (0.3, 3, 30 mg/kg) to pregnant rats during the period of organogenesis from gestation days 6 to 17 resulted in embryo-fetal mortality at \ge 0.3 mg/kg and structural abnormalities at \ge 3 mg/kg at maternal exposures that were below the human AUC0-24 at the recommended dose.
L196: The label advises females of reproductive potential and males with female partners of reproductive potential to use effective contraception during treatment and for 1 week after the last dose of imlunestrant based on the recommendations in the FDA guidance, “Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling Recommendations”. The guidance recommends a contraception period of 5 half-lives (imlunestrant half-life = 30.1 hours after repeated dosing) after the last dose for nongenotoxic small molecules that cause teratogenicity or embryo-fetal lethality. Additionally, the label advises lactating women not to breastfeed during treatment and for 1 week (5 half-lives) after the last dose due to the potential for severe adverse reactions in a breastfed child.
L197: ## **Recommendation:**
L198: The nonclinical data submitted to this NDA are adequate to support approval of Inluriyo for the proposed indication. The nonclinical recommendation is approval.
L199: ## 5.2 Referenced NDAs, BLAs, DMFs
L200: ## The Applicant's Position:
L201: There are no other referenced NDAs, BLAs, or DMFs applicable to the nonclinical pharmacology or toxicology of imlunestrant.
L202: To maintain consistency with the submitted modules and reports, doses of nonclinical pharmacology/toxicology studies are expressed as imlunestrant-free base.
L203: ## 5.3 Pharmacology
L204: ## Primary pharmacology
L205: Imlunestrant is a next-generation orally bioavailable, noncovalent, SERD and antagonist. Imlunestrant binds with high affinity to wild-type and mutant (Y537S) ER\alpha and wild-type ER\beta ligand-binding domains with K_i values of 0.64 nM, 2.8 nM, and 0.11 nM, respectively.
L206: Imlunestrant is a potent degrader of ER\alpha wild-type and mutant (Y537N/S) proteins in ER+ breast cancer cell lines, with an IC_{50} value of 3 nM in MCF7 *ESR1* wild-type cells. In MCF7 *ESR1* wild-type and Y537N mutant cells, imlunestrant potently inhibited ER-mediated PGR gene transcription (IC_{50} 41 nM and 13 nM, respectively). Imlunestrant is a pure antagonist and has not shown agonist activity in MCF7 cells as measured by the absence of PGR expression induction. Imlunestrant potently inhibits cell proliferation in MCF7 and T47D *ESR1* wild-type and Y537N mutant breast cancer cell lines as well as an *ESR1*-Y537S mutant PDX-derived cell line. In a panel of breast cancer cell lines, 11 out of 12 ER+ breast cancer cell lines were sensitive to imlunestrant (IC_{50} &lt; 100 nM), while all ER- cell lines tested were insensitive (IC_{50} &gt; 2 \muM). In vitro combination of imlunestrant with abemaciclib displayed synergistic anti-proliferative activity in ER+ breast cancer cell lines.
L207: Imlunestrant demonstrated dose- and time-dependent sustained in vivo target inhibition (&gt;75% PGR gene expression inhibition up to 96 hours) in *ESR1* wild-type (MCF7) and *ESR1*-Y537S mutant (ST941/C) xenograft tumors in mice. Imlunestrant was well tolerated and induced significant tumor growth inhibition and tumor regressions in multiple ER+ breast cancer xenograft models, including an *ESR1*-Y537S mutant PDX model. Combination of imlunestrant with abemaciclib was well tolerated and exhibited enhanced antitumor activity in a T47D breast cancer xenograft model. In clinically relevant ER+ breast cancer PDX models, including models with *ESR1* mutations and palbociclib resistance, imlunestrant exhibited single-agent efficacy and
L208: enhanced combination activity with abemaciclib. Additionally, imlunestrant crosses the blood-brain barrier in mice and showed antitumor activity and improved OS in an intracranial ER+ breast cancer model. Consistent with its profile as a pure antagonist, imlunestrant displayed no uterotrophic effects or adverse microscopic findings in the uterus, cervix, or vagina of immature female rats.
L209: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L210: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The data in combination with the CDK4/6 inhibitor were not reviewed for this NDA application since the proposed indication is for imlunestrant monotherapy.
L211: The established pharmacologic class (EPC) for imlunestrant is estrogen receptor antagonist. In vitro, imlunestrant induced degradation of ER\alpha, leading to inhibition of ER-dependent gene (i.e., PGR, PDZK1, AREG, RASGRP1, WISP2, and GREB1) transcription and cellular proliferation in ER^+ breast cancer cells.
L212: The Applicant states that imlunestrant showed anti-tumor activity in ER^+ breast cancer PDX models including those that were resistant to palbociclib (b) (4)
L213: Applicant did not provide direct evidence from in vitro or in vivo studies demonstrating anti-tumor activity following imlunestrant treatment in either palbociclib or other CDK4/6 inhibitor (abemaciclib) resistant models.
L214: In an orthotopic brain model, daily oral administration of imlunestrant (\ge 10 mg/kg, 28 days) to mice implanted with luciferase expressing breast cancer cells (MCF7-fLuc) led to reduced tumor growth and increased animal life span compared to control. Exposure to imlunestrant was observed in the brain following oral administration to mice bearing ER\alpha wild-type flank breast cancer xenografts. Although the nonclinical data suggests the ability of imlunestrant to penetrate the brain resulting in anti-tumor activity, there is insufficient clinical data to support anti-tumor activity on brain metastases from primary breast tumors.
L215: A summary of the uterotrophic effects of imlunestrant are included in the Secondary Pharmacology section of the review.
L216: Additional data (presented by the FDA in **Figure 5.1**)
L217: #ONCO167: The in vivo anti-tumor activity of imlunestrant was examined using MCF7-fLuc breast cancer cells implanted orthotopically in mouse brain. Luciferase labelled MCF7 (*ESR1* wild type) ER^+ breast cancer cells were implanted in the brain and tumor growth was followed using live imaging. Daily administration of 10 to 60 mg/kg imlunestrant orally once daily for 28 days increased animal life span compared to the control group (&gt; 75\% VS. 0\% on Day 49). Additionally, imaging data showed that imlunestrant inhibited tumor cell growth in the brain
L218: (decreased tumor cell luciferase luminescence) suggesting imlunestrant penetrated the brain. Of note, based on current available clinical data there is insufficient clinical evidence of imlunestrant induced anti-tumor activity on brain metastases of patients with *ESR1*-mutated breast cancer.
L219: **Figure 5.1 Anti-tumor activity of imlunestrant in MCF-fluc brain orthotopic model.**
L220: (Excerpted from the Applicant's submission.)
L221: ## Secondary pharmacology
L222: ## The Applicant's Position:
L223: Imlunestrant demonstrated high selectivity as an ER\alpha antagonist with &gt;1734-fold selectivity over other nuclear hormone receptors such as androgen receptor, glucocorticoid receptor, mineralocorticoid receptor, and PGR. Imlunestrant was highly selective against 33 kinases and 8 human G-protein coupled receptors relevant for human safety when tested in enzymatic assays in vitro. Imlunestrant displayed no uterotrophic effects or adverse microscopic findings in the uterus, cervix, or vagina of immature female rats, indicating no agonist activity in these tissues.
L224: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L225: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Additional details from the uterine study are summarized below.
L226: Data (presented by the FDA):
L227: #8393708: This non-GLP study assessed the potential agonistic effects of imlunestrant on ER\alpha through effects on the uterus, cervix, and vagina. Immature female rats (19-21 days old) were administered either vehicle or 10, 30, 75, or 200 mg/kg imlunestrant orally once daily for 4 days (n = 5 females/group). Positive controls were estradiol (0.01 mg/kg) and tamoxifen (10 mg/kg) administered orally once daily for 4 days. The toxicity parameters included organ weight and histopathology changes in uterus (endometrium, endometrial glands, and stroma), cervix (epithelium), and vagina (epithelium). When compared to the control group, there were no remarkable changes to the histopathology findings in uterus, cervix, and vagina of rats treated with imlunestrant at doses up to 200 mg/kg. Findings were limited to a decrease in uterus weight relative to body weight when imlunestrant was administered at 30 mg/kg (11%) and 75 mg/kg (32%) without remarkable change at 200 mg/kg compared to control. Both positive controls caused minimal to marked microscopic changes in endometrium (karyomegaly, hypertrophy, hyperplasia, increased mitotic figure), endometrial gland (severe hypertrophy), stroma (minimal to severe eosinophils infiltrates), and myometrium of uterus (severe hypertrophy), and showed histopathology changes in cervix (slight to moderate keratinization and hyperplasia epithelium) and vagina (minimal to severe keratinization and hyperplasia epithelium). A statistically significant increase in uterus weight was observed in rats administered estradiol (452%) and tamoxifen (247%) compared to control. Overall, the results indicate that imlunestrant does not exhibit agonistic activity on ER\alpha, while the positive controls demonstrated the expected agonistic effects.
L228: ## **Safety pharmacology**
L229: Imlunestrant had no effect on the cardiovascular, central nervous, and respiratory systems of monkeys in 4-week or 3-month toxicology repeat-dose toxicology studies. Additionally, imlunestrant did not demonstrate inhibitory activity, defined as 50% inhibition or greater, in a human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) cardiac potassium channel assay. Safety pharmacology assessments were performed in compliance with good laboratory practice regulations and according to ICH S7 and ICH S9 guidelines.
L230: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L231: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Additionally, imlunestrant had no remarkable effects on the central nervous and respiratory systems of rats in the repeat dose toxicology studies based on clinical observations.
L232: Data (presented by the FDA):
L233: # 190112.FMF: The in vitro data showed that imlunestrant inhibited hERG current by 4% at 0.3 \muM and 28% at 1 \muM versus 3% in the control group. No data was obtained at the 3 \muM tested concentration due to disruption of the giga-ohm seal required for successful patch clamp recordings. Therefore, the IC50 for inhibitory effect of imlunestrant on hERG potassium current was not calculated.
L234: ## 5.4 Absorption, Distribution, Metabolism, Excretion/Pharmacokinetics
L235: ## The Applicant's Position:
L236: In single-dose PK and oral bioavailability studies in CD-1 mice and cynomolgus monkeys, imlunestrant oral bioavailability ranged from approximately 8% to 44%. In all species after single and multiple dose, imlunestrant was slowly absorbed. Following repeated dosing of imlunestrant in rats and in monkeys, systemic exposure (Cmax and AUC) generally increased with increasing dose, but the increase was not consistently dose proportional. In both species, generally no accumulation and no sex difference in exposure were observed.
L237: Across mouse, rat, dog, and monkey, imlunestrant is highly bound (greater than 99.8%) to plasma proteins. In mice, imlunestrant was found to cross the blood-brain barrier. Imlunestrant was widely distributed across tissues in rats.
L238: The metabolism of imlunestrant was primarily mediated by direct phenol glucuronidation and sulfation of the parent, with minor contribution from oxidation. The major human metabolite (M1, direct glucuronide conjugate) is present in rat and monkey, with appropriate steady-state coverage.
L239: The route of excretion of [14C]-imlunestrant-derived radioactivity was determined in rats and indicated that the major route of elimination was in feces (greater than 50% of administered dose).
L240: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L241: The FDA generally agrees with Applicant's position. See the clinical pharmacology Section 6 for information regarding human data and drug interaction studies.
L242: ## Data (presented by the FDA):

L252: ## 5.5 Toxicology
L253: ## 5.5.1 General Toxicology
L254: ## The Applicant's Position:
L255: The general toxicology profile across repeat-dose toxicity studies in rats and monkeys demonstrates that imlunestrant was well tolerated at doses and exposures that exceeded the anticipated therapeutic levels in humans.
L256: ## Single-dose toxicity studies
L257: No single-dose toxicity studies of imlunestrant were conducted. Single-dose studies are typically not conducted for modern drug development, as short pilot and range-finding studies generally provide equivalent or more information. In pilot and range-finding studies, single doses were well tolerated with no mortality or clinical observations indicating acute toxicity from Day 1 to Day 2.
L258: ## Repeat-dose toxicity studies
L259: Imlunestrant has been administered by QD oral gavage to rats for up to 6 months and to monkeys for up to 3 months. Consistent with imlunestrant pharmacology, the primary target organs in rat and monkey repeat-dose studies were female reproductive tissues. Microscopic findings included
L260: - cysts in the ovaries of rats and monkeys
- hyperplasia of ovarian granulosa cells in rats
- atrophy of the epithelium of the uterus, cervix, and vagina of rats, as well as
- hypertrophy/hyperplasia of the female mammary glands.
L261: Except for the mammary gland effects, these findings were generally correlated with organ weight changes, macroscopic changes, or both. Effects on male reproductive tissues were also apparent in longer-term rat studies. Reproductive tissue effects were collectively considered adverse in both sexes but their relevance for the intended patient population of imlunestrant is minimal because men and pre/perimenopausal women treated with imlunestrant will receive GnRH agonist and because the effects in animals were generally reversible.
L262: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L263: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Additional target organs of toxicity included kidney and urinary bladder.
L264: The 7-day dose range finding study in monkeys and 14-day dose range finding toxicity study in rats were not included in the review since the Applicant submitted GLP-compliant repeat dose toxicity studies up to 6 months in rats and up to 3 months in monkeys.
L265: Data (presented by the FDA):
L266: **Study title:** A 6-month toxicity and toxicokinetic study of LY3484356 tosylate (compound 3513322) by oral gavage daily to rats with a 3-month recovery period
L267: Study no.:	8003353
Date of study initiation:	May 26, 2021
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	Imlunestrant (LY3484356 tosylate), lot# CR-C18071754-J21001, and 67.6% (free base) and 89.8% (salt) for potency/content
L268: ## **Key Study Findings**
L269: - Early mortality occurred at 150 mg/kg attributed to imlunestrant-related urinary obstruction.
- The major target organs were male and female reproductive organs, kidney, urinary bladder, and hematopoietic/lymphatic systems. Changes in histopathology findings were either reversible or trending towards recovery.
L270: ## Methods
L271: Doses:	0, 10, 30, 60, 150 mg/kg
Frequency of dosing:	Once daily
Route of administration:	Oral gavage
Dose volume:	10 mL/kg
Formulation/Vehicle:	(b) (4) in ultra-pure water
Species/Strain:	Sprague Dawley rats
Number/Sex/Group:	15/sex/group (main study); 5/sex/group (recovery group for the VH, 60 mg/kg and 150 mg/kg)
Age:	6-8 weeks
Weight:	160 to 210 g (males)/ 140 to 180 g (females)
Satellite groups:	9/sex/group (toxicokinetic group, except VH was 3/sex/group); 5/sex/group (T-dependent antibody response [TDAR] testing group)
Unique study design:	None
Deviation from study protocol:	All deviations that occurred during the study did not impact the overall integrity of the study or the interpretation of the data.
L272: ## Observations and Results
L273: Parameters	Major findings
Mortality	-At 150 mg/kg, three rats were early moribund sacrificed between Days 82 and 176 of dosing phase. These animals showed decreases in body weight and activity, hunched posture, decreased vocalization, cold to touch, and shallow breathing. The cause of early moribund sacrifice was due to urinary obstruction (2 females at 150 mg/kg euthanized on Days 82 and 106) or a mass observed in the thymus (1 male at 150 mg/kg on Day 176). Urinary obstruction correlated with histopathology findings of marked bacterial pyelonephritis in the kidney (inflammation, pelvis) and moderate cystitis (inflammation) with hyperplasia of epithelium, dilation, and hemorrhage in the urinary bladder. Changes in the thymus may not be related to the administration of imlunestrant based on lack of correlating histopathology findings in the surviving main study animals.
Clinical Signs	Unremarkable
Body Weights	Terminal Sacrifices (compared to VH control)
L274: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 218881
L275: Inluriyo (imlunestrant)
L276: (Excerpted from the Applicant's submission)
L277: Recovery (compared to the VH control)
L278: Recovery (Week 39; compared to VH control)
Animals dosed with imlunestrant at 60 mg/kg (1/4 males; 3/5 females) and 150 mg/kg (4/5 males; 4/5 females) still showed slight corneal opacity suggesting no recovery. However, both male (4/5) and female (3/5) control animals also showed very slight corneal opacity at the end of the recovery period.
Although an increased incidence in ophthalmoscopy finding was noted at 150 mg/kg, the relationship to imlunestrant treatment is unclear considering findings with the same severity were noted in recovery control animals and ocular findings were absent in the 1- and 3-month studies including higher doses of imlunestrant.

Immunophenotyping analysis	On Day 183 (end of dosing), the Applicant evaluated changes for total lymphocytes for all treatment groups in blood, spleen, and thymus. Other parameters, including total T lymphocytes, helper T lymphocytes, cytotoxic T lymphocyte, B lymphocyte, NK cells,
L315: and helper T cells: cytotoxic T cells ratio, were assessed at \le 30 mg/kg (blood) and \le 60 mg/kg (spleen, thymus). Reversibility was assessed on Day 274 at \ge 60 mg/kg.
Overall imlunestrant showed dose-dependent increases in various lymphocyte populations (especially T lymphocytes), with effects being more pronounced in females during the end of dosing period and in males during the recovery period only at the highest dose level. Changes in the immunophenotyping analysis in blood and spleen of females were reversible at the end of the recovery period.
Terminal Sacrifices (Day 183)
At the end of the dosing period in females, imlunestrant showed dose-dependent increases in various cell types including total lymphocytes (39-77% in blood and 18-84% in spleen from 10-150 mg/kg), absolute cytotoxic T lymphocyte (19-61% in thymus from 10-60 mg/kg), absolute T lymphocytes in spleen (18-43% from 10-60 mg/kg), absolute NK cells (29-43% from 10-60 mg/kg) when compared to the VH control. These changes were reversible in females at the end of the recovery period.
Recovery period (Day 274)
For immunophenotyping findings in thymus of females compared to VH control at the end of Day 274, changes were primarily observed at the 150 mg/kg including total lymphocyte count (33%), absolute total T lymphocytes (45%), absolute helper T lymphocytes (39%), absolute cytotoxic T lymphocytes (54%), total lymphocyte count/thymus (33%), absolute thymocyte subsets at different maturation stages (CD3^+/CD4^+/CD8a^+; 54%), absolute thymocyte subsets at different stages (CD3^-CD4CD8a^+; 30%).
For immunophenotyping in the spleen of males, changes observed were primarily in males at the 150 mg/kg at the end of Day 274 including total lymphocytes (61% in spleen and 122% in thymus), absolute T lymphocytes (63% in spleen), Total lymphocytes (126% in thymus), absolute helper T lymphocyte (77% in spleen and 135% in thymus), and cytotoxicity T lymphocytes (45% in spleen and 117% in thymus), total spleen cell count (61%), and absolute B lymphocytes (91%), total lymphocyte count/thymus (122%), absolute thymocyte subsets at different maturation stages (CD3^+/CD4^+/CD8a^+; 115%), absolute thymocyte subsets at different stages (CD3^-CD4CD8a^+; 123%).

T-cell dependent antibody response
L316: ## **T-cell dependent antibody response (anti-KLH IgM)**
L317: - Compared to the vehicle control, changes were not dose-dependent, except a dose-dependent increase in anti-KLH IgM in the second challenge on Days 176 and 183 in males administered 30 to 150 mg/kg imlunestrant. Although it was not dose-dependent, a decrease in the primary anti-KLH IgM response was noted 7 to 14 days after the first KLH injection, but increased during Day 21 post-KLH administration. However, the magnitude of changes in T-cell dependent antibody response did not correlate with histopathology findings. Overall, imlunestrant had no effect on the primary immune response to KLH challenge.
L318: ## **T-cell dependent antibody response (anti-KLH IgG)**
L319: - Changes were not dose-dependent when compared to the VH control; except a dose-dependent increase in anti-KLH IgG in the second challenge on Days 176 and 183 in males administered 10-150 mg/kg imlunestrant. Changes in anti-KLH IgG responses also decreased at 7 to 14 days after the initial KLH injection, though this effect was not dose-dependent. However, the magnitude of changes in T-cell dependent antibody response did not correlate with histopathology findings. Overall, imlunestrant had no effect on the humoral immune response to KLH challenge.
L320: ## **Dosing solution analysis**
L321: Formulations met the concentration acceptance criteria (\pm (b)/(a))% of the nominal concentration). Formulation met the homogeneity acceptance criteria (the mean relative standard deviation (RSD) of concentration was \le (b)/(a))%.
L322: ## **General toxicology, additional studies in rats:**
L323: GLP-compliant 4-week and 3-month daily oral repeat dose toxicology studies were conducted in rats. These studies were reviewed under IND 145311. The main findings including target organs of toxicity generally reflected those observed in the GLP-compliant 6-month study. Additional findings are noted below.
L324: **3-month study (#8003221/GLP compliant):** Rats were administered daily oral imlunestrant (LY3484356; lot# CR-C18071754-J21001, 89.8% purity) at doses of 0, 10, 30, and 150 mg/kg/day for 3 months followed by a 6-week recovery period (n = 10/sex/group [main], 5/sex/group [recovery for only control and high dose groups]).
L325: - No early deaths. Clinical observations of salivation and abnormal liquid/soft/mucoid feces were observed at \ge 10 mg/kg in both males and females, and minimal nerve fiber degeneration in the optic nerve in a female administered 150 mg/kg.
L326: **4-week study (#8002568/GLP compliant):** Rats were administered daily oral imlunestrant (lot# JR-C18071754-J19001, 92.7% purity) at doses of 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg for 4 weeks followed by a 2-week recovery period (n = 10/sex/group [main], 5/sex/group [recovery for only control and high dose groups]).
L327: - Early deaths (n=3 males) occurred at 300 mg/kg due to imlunestrant related hepatocellular and/or muscular damage, toxicities that were not observed in the 3- and 6-month studies up to 150 mg/kg.
L328: **Study title/number:** A 3-month toxicity and toxicokinetic study of LY3484356 tosylate (compound 3513322) given daily by oral gavage to Cynomolgus monkeys/ 8003147
L329: **Methods:** Monkeys received daily oral imlunestrant (LY3484356; lot# CR-C18071754-J21001, 89.8% purity) at 0, 15, 100, and 600 mg/kg for 3 months (n = 4/sex/group).
L330: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L331: - The major target organs were the hematopoietic/lymphatic systems, female reproductive organs, gastrointestinal tract, liver, and kidneys with similar severity to those observed in the one-month repeat-dose toxicity study.
L332: ## **Observations and Results**
L333: Parameters	Major findings
Mortality	All animals survived to the end of the study.
Clinical Signs	At 600 mg/kg, imlunestrant-related clinical signs included vomitus during Days 5-102 in both males and females, salivation in males between Days 95-105, abdominal distension in females during Days 30-35. Abnormal feces (pale, soft, liquid) were observed at 600 mg/kg in both males and females during Days 4-106.

L346: LD: low dose; MD: mid dose; HD: high dose; M: male; F: female
L347: ## General toxicology, additional studies in monkeys:
L348: A GLP-compliant 4-week daily oral repeat dose toxicology study was conducted in monkeys. This study was reviewed under IND145311. The main findings including target organs of toxicity generally reflected those observed in the GLP-compliant 3-month study. Additional findings are noted below.
L349: **4-week study (#8002569/GLP compliant):** Monkeys were administered daily oral imlunestrant (LY3484356; lot# CR-C18071754-J19001, 89.8% purity) at doses of 0, 30, 150, and 600 mg/kg (n = 3/sex/group).
L350: - No deaths or significant weight loss occurred at the high dose of 600 mg/kg during the 4-week toxicity study. Treatment-related clinical chemistry changes at doses \ge 30 mg/kg included increases in alanine aminotransferase (up to 2X above control), creatine kinase (up to 3X above control), total bilirubin (up to 41%) and a dose-dependent decrease in globulin (11-18%).
L351: ## 5.5.2 Genetic Toxicology
L352: ## The Applicant's Position:
L353: Imlunestrant does not pose a biologically relevant genotoxic risk. Imlunestrant was not mutagenic in the Ames bacterial reverse mutation assay. Imlunestrant produced chromosomal damage in the in vitro micronucleus assay but did not cause chromosomal damage or DNA strand breaks in vivo when tested up to 2000 mg/kg in the rat comet and bone marrow micronucleus assays.
L354: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L355: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that imlunestrant is not considered genotoxic.
L356: ## 5.5.3 Carcinogenicity
L357: ## The Applicant's Position:
L358: Carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support marketing for therapeutics intended to treat patients with advanced cancer. Carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted to support the advanced cancer indication for imlunestrant but are ongoing to support other indications.
L359: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L360: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L361: ## 5.5.4 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L362: ## The Applicant's Position:
L363: Based on its intended pharmacologic mechanism of action as a SERD and findings in animal studies, imlunestrant could cause harm to a fetus and may impair fertility.
L364: In repeat-dose toxicity studies, imlunestrant was associated with reversible cessation of estrous cycling and generally reversible anatomic effects on the female reproductive tract. Anatomic effects on the male reproductive tract were observed in rats and were reversible.
L365: In an embryo-fetal development study of pregnant rats administered 0, 0.3, 3, or 30 mg/kg/day oral imlunestrant during the period of organogenesis from Gestation Days 6 to 17, imlunestrant caused gross pathological changes in
L366: - maternal reproductive tissues
- embryo lethality
- fetal malformations
- variations, and
- effects on fetal growth.
L367: ## Inluriyo (imlunestrant)
L368: Adverse maternal and embryo-fetal effects were observed at doses based on body surface area and exposures less than or equal to those in clinical trials, and a no-observed-adverse-effect level for maternal and embryo-fetal toxicity was not established.
L369: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L370: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Imlunestrant caused embryo-fetal mortality at \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, approximately 0.1 times the human AUC at the recommended dose. Imlunestrant related fetal malformations and fetal variations were observed at \ge 3 mg/kg (1 times the human AUC at the recommended dose of 400 mg once daily).

L401: ## Ovarian and uterine findings
L402: - Pre-implantation loss (16.9%) at 30 mg/kg/day was statistically significantly increased compared to controls; however, according to the Applicant, this was within the Test Facility's historical control data (HCD) range (3.3-19.2%).
- Increased number of total resorptions, early resorptions, % post-implantation loss, and decreased number of live fetuses were observed at \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day. To note, these differences from controls remained similar whether the data from 1 female (No. 3506) with total resorption of its litter (10 early and 1 late resorptions at 11 implantation sites; 100% post implantation loss) were included or excluded from the analysis. Table below summarizes the data with all the animals included.
- Although not observed at 30 mg/kg, early delivery (GD 21) occurred in 40% at 3 mg/kg, which was out of the HCD range (6-12%).
- Overall, the findings suggest imlunestrant treatment leads to embryo-fetal mortality.

L411: ## Fetal malformations
L412: Statistically significant increase in fetuses with malformations including small jaw (mandible, maxilla), protruding tongue, malrotated and hyperextended hindlimb were observed at ≥ 3 mg/kg when compared to litters in the control group.

L427: ## 5.5.5 Other Toxicology Studies
L428: ## The Applicant's Position:
L429: ## Impurities
L430: Acceptance criteria for impurities in imlunestrant drug substance that are specified (b) (4) the ICH Q3A qualification threshold ((b) (4)) are toxicologically supported by the principles of ICH M7 and ICH S9, qualification in the 1-month rat qualification study or the 3-month repeat-dose monkey toxicology study, and genetic toxicity assessments consisting of an Ames assay, an in vitro micronucleus assay, or both.
L431: Limits for ICH M7 Class 2 or 3 impurities are conservatively set based on an exposure of (b) (4) µg/day ((b) (4) ppm) and a dosing duration of (b) (4) years, which maintains an excess cancer incidence of no more than 1 in 100,000 for the indicated advanced cancer population.
L432: Consistent with the recommendations of ICH S9 and ICH M7, a limit of up to (b) (4)% of an impurity in drug substance may be justified based on the advanced cancer patient population as well as manufacturing considerations. The Ames-positive (b) (4) impurity (b) (4) can form at trace levels above (b) (4) of imlunestrant but has been consistently measured at below (b) (4) ppm ((b) (4)%) in imlunestrant tosylate. Due to manufacturing considerations, a limit of (b) (4)% is proposed for (b) (4).
L433: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L434: The FDA agrees that the proposed specification of (b) (4)% for (b) (4) is consistent with the recommendations of ICH S9 and ICH Q3B and is acceptable from a nonclinical perspective.
L435: Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
L436: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L437: The Applicant conducted genetic toxicology studies, bacterial reverse mutation assays (Ames) and/or in vitro micronucleus assays using human lymphoblastoid TK6 cells, on several impurities. The proposed specification limits of the following impurities (Table below) are the ICH Q3A/B guidelines, and some showed genotoxic potential. The proposed specification limits of the listed impurities in the drug substance and drug product were qualified by the GLP compliant 28-day or the 3-month repeat-dose toxicity study in rats. There are no concerns from a nonclinical perspective with these impurity specifications for the proposed advanced cancer indication.
