L440: ## **Data (presented by FDA):**
L441: In the 28-day toxicity study, rats were given imlunestrant lot# ALL-D00218-026 at 100 mg/kg (600 mg/m2 Human Equivalent Dose [HED]) orally once daily for 28 days without any early mortalities. In the 3-month toxicity study, rats were given imlunestrant lot # CR-C18071754-J21001 at doses up to 150 mg/kg (900 mg/m2 HED) without any early mortalities. Based on body surface area (mg/m2) in rats at the dose level that did not result in severe toxicities, these impurities were administered at higher levels than would be administered to patients receiving the recommended clinical dose of 400 mg (two 200 mg tablets) taken orally, once daily (400 mg/60 kg x (b)(4) mg/m2) at the proposed specification limits for these impurities in the imlunestrant drug substance and drug product and are considered qualified.
L442: ## Phototoxicity
L443: Imlunestrant was positive for phototoxic potential in an in vitro study using BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts. In a rat phototoxicity study, imlunestrant was mildly phototoxic to the skin, producing barely perceptible dermal redness following UV radiation exposure, with no evidence of ocular phototoxicity. The risk of clinically relevant phototoxicity is low because the dermal effects observed in rats were minimal at C_{max} exposures exceeding those at the human therapeutic dose by 36-fold.
L444: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L445: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that imlunestrant showed phototoxic potential in vitro and was phototoxic to the skin in vivo.
L446: The in vitro phototoxicity study and other toxicity studies to determine the potential ocular and skin irritancy were reviewed under IND 145311 and are summarized below.
L447: # 20183318: The Applicant conducted a GLP-compliant study to evaluate the phototoxic potential of imlunestrant as measured by the relative reduction in viability of BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts exposed to imlunestrant (0.032-100 µg/mL) in the presence or absence of ultraviolet radiation. Imlunestrant showed mean photo effect (MPE) &gt; 0.15% (0.495-0.823%); thus, it was considered phototoxic in the in vitro phototoxicity study in BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts.
L448: # 19AD29.3500055: The bovine corneal opacity and permeability assay was used to assess the potential ocular irritancy of imlunestrant to isolated bovine corneas. Three corneas were incubated in the presence of imlunestrant (20% w/v) at 32°C for 4 hours. An in vitro score was determined for the test article based on the induction of opacity and permeability (to fluorescein) in the isolated bovine corneas. Based on in vitro score, imlunestrant was not predicted to be an irritant.
L449: #20183102: The Applicant conducted a GLP-compliant study to evaluate acute toxicity and irritant potential of imlunestrant when administered by dermal route to Sprague Dawley rats as a single dose of 2000 mg/kg. The test article was administered dermally to approximately 10% of the body surface area. Rats were monitored for mortality, clinical signs, dermal observation, and body weight for 14 days post-dose. No mortality occurred during the study and no imlunestrant-related clinical signs. No effects on body weight or gross pathology findings. Minimal imlunestrant-related dermal irritation was noted in 1 male (Grade 1 erythema on Days 1 and 2) and all 5 females (Grade 1 erythema, flaking skin, and eschar on Days 1 to 12).
L450: Additional in vivo phototoxicity study in female rats was reviewed and summarized below.
L451: **Study title/number: A GLP repeated dosage phototoxicity study to determine the effects of**
L452: ## **oral administration of LY3484356 tosylate (imlunestrant) on eyes and skin in pigmented rats/ 20405711**
L453: **Methods:** The objective of this GLP-compliant study was to determine the potential phototoxic effects of imlunestrant on the eyes and skin of Long-Evans pigmented female rats when administered by oral gavage. Rats were administered 0, 5, 60, and 1000 mg/kg orally once daily for 4 days, followed by exposure to ultraviolet B, ultraviolet A, and visible light from a xenon lamp. This study also included rats administered imlunestrant at 1000 mg/kg dose level without UV light exposure. The following parameters and endpoints were evaluated in this study; viability, clinical observations, dermal scoring, body weights and body weight gains, ophthalmology, bioanalysis, and toxicokinetic evaluation, and macroscopic and microscopic examinations.
L454: ## **Results:**
L455: - Clinical signs of toxicity related to imlunestrant administration were observed during Days 4-7 in 4-5 out of 5 rats at 1000 mg/kg including hunched posture and thin appearance.
- At high dose of 1000 mg/kg following UV exposure, grade 1 erythema (light and dark pigment and red color skin) was observed in 3/5 rats on Days 5-7 at site 1 and 4/5 rats on Days 5-6 at site 2. Based on these results, imlunestrant was phototoxic at a dose level of 1000 mg/kg.
- Compared to the VH control, there were no imlunestrant-related ocular findings indicative of phototoxicity.
- A decrease in body weight was only observed in rats administered the highest dose of 1000 mg/kg during Days 5-7 (17-19% with UV light exposure and 13-16% without UV light exposure).
- Systemic exposure to imlunestrant increased with increasing dose on Day 4. The increase in mean C_{max} and AUC_{0-8h} was less than dose proportional at 5, 60, and 1000 mg/kg on Day 4.
L456: **Table 5.1 Summary of toxicokinetic data on Day 4 in female rats**
L457: Dose (mg/kg)	C_{max} (ng/mL)	T_{max} (hours)	AUC_{0-8h} (h*ng/mL)
5	151	2	901
60	1540	2	9620
1000	5100	4	37600
L458: At the clinical recommended dose of 400 mg once daily, the steady state C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} were 141 ng/mL (45% CV) and 2400 ng*h/mL (46% CV), respectively. Based on the toxicokinetic data from the in vivo phototoxicity study in female pigmented rats administered 1000 mg/kg
L459: imlunestrant orally once daily for 4 days, the exposure (AUC_{0-8hr}) was 37600 h \cdot ng/mL (or 112800 h \cdot ng/mL for AUC_{0-24hr}). Thus, exposure to 1000 mg/kg imlunestrant caused phototoxicity in rats at doses that were approximately 47 times the human AUC_{0-24}.

L464: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L465: ## 6.1 Executive Summary
L466: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L467: The Applicant is seeking approval of imlunestrant for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor-1 (ESR1)-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.
L468: The clinical pharmacology review focused on the following key review issues:
L469: - Recommended dosage for the general population: 400 mg taken orally (PO), once daily (QD) taken on empty stomach, at least 2 hours before or 1 hour after food. The recommended dosage was selected based on safety, activity, pharmacokinetic (PK), and pharmacodynamic (PD) data from Studies EMBER and EMBER-2 and supportive exposure-response (E-R) analyses for safety and efficacy. Safety and efficacy of the recommended dosage in the intended patient population was demonstrated in EMBER-3. The recommendation for administration under fasting conditions is based on the positive food effect observed when imlunestrant was administered with a low-fat meal and the food conditions used in all three studies (i.e., imlunestrant at least 2 hours before or 1 hour after food).
- Recommended dosage for patients with hepatic impairment (HI): 200 mg QD for patients with moderate (Child-Pugh B) or severe (Child-Pugh C) hepatic impairment. No dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A). Imlunestrant is hepatically metabolized, and data were available from population PK analysis (PopPK) and a dedicated HI study to support dosage recommendations for patients with HI.
- Dosage modifications for patients receiving concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors or inducers with imlunestrant (a substrate of CYP3A): Avoid concomitant use of imlunestrant with strong CYP3A inhibitors or inducers. However, if concomitant use cannot be avoided, reduce imlunestrant dosage to 200 mg QD when used with strong CYP3A inhibitors, or increase imlunestrant dosage to 600 mg QD when used with strong CYP3A inducers.
L470: ## Recommendations
L471: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in NDA 218881. This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations and/or comments are summarized in **Table 6.1**.
L472: Table 6.1: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues, Recommendations, and Comments
L473: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of efficacy and safety comes from the randomized Phase 3 Study EMBER-3. In patients with ER+, HER2-, ESR1-mutated locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, imlunestrant 400 mg QD resulted in an improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) as compared to the control arm (fulvestrant or exemestane) with hazard ratio for PFS of 0.62 and median PFS of 5.5 months in the imlunestrant arm compared to 3.8 months in the control arm. See Section 8 for details.
General dosing instructions	The recommended dosage is 400 mg PO QD, on an empty stomach (at least 2 hrs before or 1 hr after food).
Dosing in patient subgroups	HI: The recommended dosage of imlunestrant for patients with moderate (Child-Pugh Class B) or severe (Child-Pugh Class C) HI is 200 mg QD. No dosage modification is recommended in patients with mild (Child-Pugh Class A) HI.
Renal impairment (RI): No dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild to moderate RI. The effect of severe renal impairment is unknown; however, renal clearance of imlunestrant is low.
ESR1 Mutations: No dosage modification is recommended based on ESR1 mutations.
Other Intrinsic Factors: No dosage modification is recommended based on age, sex, body weight, race, ethnicity, or UGT1A1 polymorphisms (e.g., UGT1A1*1/*28 or UGT1A1*28/*28).

Drug-Drug Interactions	Imlunestrant as an Object: Imlunestrant is a CYP3A substrate.Avoid concomitant use with strong CYP3A inhibitors. If concomitant use cannot be avoided, decrease the imlunestrant dosage to 200 mg QD.
Avoid concomitant use with strong CYP3A inducers. If concomitant use cannot be avoided, increase the imlunestrant dosage to 600 mg QD.

Imlunestrant as a Precipitant:Imlunestrant inhibits P-gp and BCRP. Avoid concomitant use unless otherwise recommended in the Prescribing Information for P-gp or BCRP substrates where minimal concentration changes may lead to serious adverse reactions.


Labeling	Proposed labeling language was reviewed, corrected, and updated according to applicable FDA guidances. Overall, the proposed labeling language in the clinical pharmacology pertinent sections is generally acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement to the FDA revisions.
L474: Post-Marketing Requirements (PMRs) and Commitments (PMCs)	No clinical pharmacology PMRs or PMCs will be issued.
L475: ## 6.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L476: ## 6.2.1 Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L477: ## Data:
L478: Imlunestrant has been administered orally at doses ranging from
L479: - 200 to 1200 mg QD in patients with ER+ locally advanced or MBC and ER+ recurrent, persistent, or metastatic EEC, and
- 200 to 800 mg QD in healthy participants.
L480: Exposures of imlunestrant increased proportionally across the 200 mg to 1200-mg dose range.
L481: Patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or MBC in the EMBER-3 Phase 3 pivotal study were administered 400 mg QD imlunestrant. The C_{\text{max,ss}}, C_{\text{min,ss}}, and AUC_{0-24,ss} were 141 ng/mL (45% CV), 71.2 ng/mL (54% CV), and 2400 ng*h/mL (46% CV), respectively.
L482: ## Absorption
L483: - The absolute oral bioavailability of imlunestrant was 10.5%.
- Maximal plasma concentrations of imlunestrant were reached approximately 4 hours postdose with an elimination half-life of approximately 30 hours.
L484: ## Distribution
L485: - The mean apparent oral volume of distribution for imlunestrant was (b) (4) L in patients with locally advanced or MBC.
- The percent of imlunestrant bound in human plasma was greater than 99.9%.
L486: ## Metabolism
L487: - The primary clearance pathways involved in imlunestrant metabolism are sulfation, glucuronidation, and CYP3A4-mediated oxidation.
L488: ## Elimination
L489: - Following a single 400 mg (100 \mu Ci) oral dose of [^{14}C]-imlunestrant, the primary elimination route of imlunestrant-related radioactivity was via the feces with a mean
L490: recovery of 97.3%. Negligible amounts were found in urine with a mean recovery of 0.3%.
L491: - Imlunestrant was the predominant component in feces, accounting for a mean of 61.8% of the radioactive dose.
- The mean apparent oral clearance of imlunestrant was 166 L/h, and the mean elimination t_{1/2} was estimated to be 30 hours in patients with locally advanced or MBC.
L492: ## Exposure-response relationships
L493: - Imlunestrant exposure was a significant predictor of PFS. In the monotherapy setting, compared to the mean average exposure at 400 mg QD, patients with 2-fold higher exposures had approximately 5 months longer median PFS and patients with 2-fold lower exposures had approximately 2 months shorter median PFS.
- Increase in imlunestrant exposures were not associated with increases in treatment-emergent AEs (SAEs, nausea, diarrhea, and fatigue).
- In the Phase 1 EMBER (Study J2J-MC-JZLA), imlunestrant concentrations across the 200 mg to 1200 mg dose range had no clinically meaningful effect on QTc interval, with the upper bound of the 90% CI of the mean delta QTc at C_{max} of 400 mg equal to 3.87 msec (90% CI: -0.43, 3.87).
L494: ## The Applicant's Position:
L495: Overall, the clinical pharmacology profile of imlunestrant is considered supportive of the proposed 400 mg QD dose.
L496: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L497: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the pharmacology and clinical pharmacokinetics of imlunestrant with the following exception:
L498: FDA disagrees with the exposure-PFS relationship conclusions that imlunestrant exposure was a significant predictor of PFS.
L499: During the review, the Applicant identified an error in the description of the exposure-PFS analysis data presented in the EMBER-3 Population PK/PD report. The dataset for the exposure-PFS analysis used an incorrect unit conversion. Correction of this error resulted in a change in interpretation of the exposure-PFS analysis results. Using the corrected dataset, there is no exposure-PFS relationship based on data from EMBER-3. Variations in imlunestrant exposure among patients enrolled in the EMBER-3 trial who received imlunestrant 400 mg QD do not affect PFS.
L500: In addition, the exposure-PFS relationship was deemed not fully characterized as the E-R analysis for efficacy only includes data from one dose level.
L501: The E-R analyses for safety were not impacted by the error. See **Section 19.4.2** for details.
L502: ## 6.2.2 General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L503: ## 6.2.2.1 General Dosing
L504: ## Data:
L505: Please see Section 6.2.1.
L506: ## The Applicant's Position:
L507: The recommended Phase 2 dose of 400 mg QD was established in the Phase 1 EMBER study based on PK, safety, and antitumor activity data. This study did not reach the maximum tolerated dose at the highest dose level of 1200 mg. An optimum biologically active dose of 400 mg was chosen based on PK data and EMBER-2 PD data for Phase 3 studies.
L508: Based on the popPK analysis, after 400 mg QD doses, 87% of patients are predicted to exceed TEC80 over the entire dosing interval at steady state, indicating that biologically efficacious concentrations are achieved and sustained at this dose in the majority of patients. After 200 mg QD doses, 45% of patients are predicted to exceed TEC80 over the entire dosing interval at steady state.
L509: The 400 mg QD dose in EMBER demonstrated a manageable safety profile with a lower incidence of nausea and diarrhea compared with higher doses. Finally, among the imlunestrant monotherapy cohorts, 4 out of 6 partial responses were observed at the 400-mg dose level indicating clinical activity.
L510: In parallel with EMBER-3, the EMBER-2 window-of-opportunity study (Neven et al. 2024) evaluated the on-target PD effect of imlunestrant at 3 dose levels (200 to 800 mg QD) in postmenopausal women with early-stage breast cancer. Though similar on-target effects with substantial ER degradation were observed across all doses, the 200 mg QD dose had the lowest rate of complete cell cycle arrest and the 800 mg QD dose had the highest incidence of nausea and diarrhea. Thus, the EMBER-2 data supported the ongoing enrollment in EMBER-3 with 400 mg QD and the dose reduction strategy of 200 mg QD.
L511: Exposure-response analyses identified a wide therapeutic window for imlunestrant in patients with ABC. Exposures up to 2.5-fold above and 2-fold below those achieved with 400 mg QD retain both safety and efficacy. The 400-mg dose allows
L512: - flexibility to accommodate a wide range of intrinsic or extrinsic patient factors, such as mild hepatic impairment, and
- dose reductions for safety while still providing therapeutic benefit.
L513: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L514: FDA agrees that the proposed recommended imlunestrant dosage of 400 mg PO QD on an empty stomach is appropriate for the indicated population.
L515: In EMBER-3, the proposed recommended dosage demonstrated a favorable benefit-risk profile compared to the control arm (fulvestrant or exemestane) in patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative, *ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer. Imlunestrant improved median PFS by 1.7 months (5.5 months for imlunestrant vs 3.9 months for control arm with HR of 0.62) in the proposed patient population with an acceptable safety profile. In EMBER-3, permanent discontinuation, dosage interruption, and dosage reduction of imlunestrant due to an AE occurred in 4.6%, 10%, and 2.4% of patients, respectively. Refer to **Section 1.3** for additional assessment of the benefit-risk.
L516: With regard to safety, the MTD was not reached at up to 1,200 mg QD in EMBER and there were no apparent E-R relationships for safety of imlunestrant. Higher rates of nausea and diarrhea were observed for 800 mg QD compared to 200 and 400 mg QD in EMBER-2, despite the lack of E-R relationships for safety, which contributed to the selection of 400 mg QD for further evaluation.
L517: With regard to activity, there was no apparent dose-response relationship for ORR in patients treated with imlunestrant monotherapy in EMBER (**Table 6.2**), similar ER degradation was observed across 200 mg to 800 mg QD dosages in EMBER-2, and there was no apparent E-R relationship for PFS among patients treated with 400 mg QD in EMBER-3.

L521: The proposed administration of imlunestrant without food is appropriate given that administration of imlunestrant 400 mg with a low-fat meal increased Cmax 3.6-fold and AUC0-inf 2-fold as compared to administration under fasting conditions (Refer to **Section 6.3.2.4** for details). In addition, all three studies were conducted using the proposed food conditions (i.e., imlunestrant at least 2 hrs before or 1 hr after food); therefore, the observed safety and efficacy in EMBER-3 is representative of the recommended dosage and administration instructions.
L522: ## 6.2.2.2 Therapeutic Individualization
L523: Data:
L524: Intrinsic factors
L525: In a hepatic impairment (determined by Child-Pugh classification) clinical pharmacology study (Study J2J-MC-JZLG), mild hepatic impairment did not have a significant effect on imlunestrant exposures. Moderate hepatic impairment increased the AUC_{(0-\infty)} by 122% and AUC_{(0-\infty),u} by 82% with no significant difference on C_{max}. Severe hepatic impairment increased the dose-normalized AUC_{(0-\infty)} by 206% and dose-normalized AUC_{(0-\infty),u} by 133% with no significant difference on dose-normalized C_{max}. Dose reductions to 200 mg QD in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment are recommended, since these chronic conditions may lead to increased accumulation of imlunestrant. Moreover, the exposure increase in severe hepatic patients is beyond the range of exposures over which the exposure-safety analyses were conducted.
L526: Following a single radiolabeled 400-mg dose of imlunestrant to healthy participants, approximately 97.3% of the dose was recovered in feces (19.8% unchanged) and 0.3% in urine, suggesting imlunestrant is not cleared renally. Based on a popPK analysis, mild and moderate renal impairment does not affect imlunestrant PK. There were insufficient patient numbers to draw clear conclusions regarding the effect of severe renal impairment and moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the PK of imlunestrant.
L527: Based on a popPK analysis, imlunestrant disposition is not affected by
L528: - serum albumin (range: 22.0 to 55.0 g/L)
- age (range: 28 to 95 years)
- ethnicity (180 Hispanic [17%], 777 non-Hispanic [74%])
- race (667 White [64%], 50 Black or African American [5%], 240 Asian [23%], 93 others/missing [9%])
- mild and moderate renal impairment (448 normal [43%], 482 mild [46%] 108 moderate [10%], 2 severe [&lt;1\%]) classified by estimated glomerular filtration rate, and
- mild hepatic impairment (842 normal [81%], 201 mild [19%]).
L529: Based on a popPK analysis, imlunestrant disposition is statistically significantly affected by
L530: - body weight (range: 36 to 145 kg), and
- study population (MBC: N = 914 [87%], EEC: N = 72 [6.9%], healthy participants: N = 64 [6.1%]).
L531: However, PK simulations performed for a range of body weights and between patients and healthy participants showed a large degree of overlap between the PK profiles, illustrating the minimal impact body weight or study population have on the PK profile when considering the degree of natural PK variability (CL/F interindividual variability = 51%) in the analysis population.
L532: ## Inluriyo (imlunestrant)
L533: In a retrospective analysis examining the effect of UGT1A1 polymorphism on imlunestrant PK, results showed that UGT1A1 genetic polymorphisms with reduced function did not affect imlunestrant exposures.
L534: The primary clearance pathways involved in imlunestrant metabolism are sulfation, glucuronidation, and CYP3A4-mediated oxidation. There are no clinically relevant genetic polymorphisms of cytosolic sulfotransferases or CYP3A4.
L535: ## Extrinsic factors
L536: Based on a popPK analysis, imlunestrant disposition is not affected by formulation or the presence of abemaciclib or omeprazole.
L537: In Study J2J-MC-JZLD, a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor (itraconazole) increased imlunestrant AUC(0-\infty) by 111%, and a strong CYP3A4 inducer (carbamazepine) decreased imlunestrant AUC(0-\infty) by 42.2%. In Study J2J-MC-JZLI, a statistically significant decrease in the AUC(0-\infty) of imlunestrant of 13.1% was observed when imlunestrant was dosed in the presence of quinidine (P-gp inhibitor). This decrease in imlunestrant exposure was not considered clinically meaningful.
L538: Statistically significant increases in AUC(0-tlast), AUC(0-\infty), and Cmax of imlunestrant of 99%, 104%, and 255%, respectively, were observed following a single oral dose of 400 mg imlunestrant in the fed state (low-fat meal) compared to in the fasted state. The increase in Cmax observed in the presence of a low-fat meal was beyond the exposure range studied in the clinical development of imlunestrant. Therefore, imlunestrant should be dosed in the fasted state.
L539: ## The Applicant's Position:
L540: ## Intrinsic factors requiring dose modification or adjustment
L541: Imlunestrant dose reduction by 200 mg is recommended in moderate and severe hepatic impairment.
L542: ## Extrinsic factors requiring dose modification or adjustment
L543: Strong CYP3A inhibitors should be avoided (b) (4). Strong CYP3A inducers should be avoided, and alternative agents considered when coadministered with imlunestrant. If concomitant use is unavoidable, imlunestrant dose should be increased by 200 mg QD since there was a positive exposure-efficacy relationship.
L544: Imlunestrant should be dosed in the fasted state. Patients should not eat at least (b) (4) before and at least (b) (4) after taking imlunestrant, which are the conditions under which EMBER, EMBER-2, and EMBER-3 were carried out.
L545: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L546: FDA agrees with the Applicant regarding therapeutic individualization with the following exceptions:
L547: Avoid concomitant use with strong CYP3A inhibitors. If concomitant use cannot be avoided, decrease the imlunestrant dosage to 200 mg QD.
L548: Imlunestrant inhibits both P-gp and BCRP which may result in increased exposure and increased adverse reactions of the substrates of these transporters. Avoid concomitant use of P-gp and BCRP substrates unless prescribing information for P-gp and BCRP substrates recommends specific dosage modifications.
L549: Additional drug-drug interaction (DDI) studies supported lack of clinically significant interaction for imlunestrant as a precipitant with substrates of CYP2C8, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A.
L550: No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of imlunestrant were observed when used concomitantly with omeprazole (gastric acid-reducing agent) or quinidine (P-gp inhibitor).
L551: For additional information, refer to **Section 6.3.2.3** for intrinsic patient factors, **Section 6.3.2.4** for food effect and DDIs, and **Section 19.4.1** for PopPK analysis.
L552: ## 6.2.2.3 Outstanding Issues
L553: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L554: There are no known outstanding issues.
L555: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L556: FDA agrees with the Applicant that there are no outstanding clinical pharmacology issues.
L557: ## 6.3 Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L558: ## 6.3.1 General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L559: ## **Data:**
L560: Please see Section 6.2.1.
L561: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L562: Imlunestrant clinical pharmacology has been well characterized in a series of clinical studies in healthy participants and in patients with HR+, HER2- ABC or MBC to support the appropriateness of the proposed imlunestrant dose regimen.
L563: Exposure-response analyses to evaluate the range of safe and efficacious exposures of imlunestrant in patients with locally advanced or MBC identified a wide therapeutic window for
L564: imlunestrant. Changes in exposure from 400 mg QD that increase imlunestrant exposure by 2.5-fold or decrease exposure by 2-fold are not expected to have a major impact on safety or efficacy. This allows flexibility for the physician/patient in accommodating a wide range of intrinsic/extrinsic patient factors as well as allowing the proposed dose reductions for safety while still allowing some treatment benefit.
L565: Imlunestrant can be coadministered with weak and moderate CYP3A inhibitors, weak and moderate CYP3A inducers, P-gp inhibitors and agents that modify gastric pH (for example, proton-pump inhibitors) without dose modification. Imlunestrant can also be coadministered with CYP2C8, CYP2C19, and CYP3A substrates. Intrinsic and extrinsic factors requiring imlunestrant dose adjustments or modifications are discussed in Section 6.2.2.2. (b) (4) BCRP, and P-gp substrates for which a small increase in concentration could lead to significant AEs.
L566: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L567: FDA agrees that imlunestrant clinical pharmacology has been well characterized. As described in Section 6.2.1, no significant E-R relationships for efficacy were identified in the indicated patient population; however, these results should be interpreted with caution.
L568: (b) (4)
L569: ## **FDA disagrees with the recommendation that**
L570: Imlunestrant inhibits P-gp and BCRP which may result in increased exposure and increased adverse reactions of the substrates of these transporters. Avoid concomitant use of P-gp and BCRP substrates with imlunestrant unless prescribing information for these substrates recommends specific dosage modifications.
L571: (b) (4)
L572: ## **The FDA recommended**
L573: A small increase in dextromethorphan exposure (a sensitive CYP2D6 substrate) was observed in a clinical DDI study. However, given the magnitude of dextromethorphan exposure change and the higher sensitivity of dextromethorphan compared to other CYP2D6 substrates, inhibition of CYP2D6 is unlikely to be clinically significant for other CYP2D6 substrates.
L574: See Section 6.3.2 and Section 19.4 for additional information.
L575: ## 6.3.2 Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L576: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L577: ## **Data:**
L578: The evidence of effectiveness for EMBER-3 is also discussed in Section 8.
L579: Inluriyo (imlunestrant)
L580: ## The Applicant's Position:
L581: Yes.
L582: Results from popPK and exposure-response analyses showed that the proposed dose and dose intensity of imlunestrant lead to an efficacy benefit in the proposed label population. AEs can be managed through dose interruptions and dose reductions as outlined in the proposed labeling. Therefore, imlunestrant has a positive benefit-risk ratio at the proposed daily dose regimen.
L583: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L584: Refer to Section 8.1 for assessment of the evidence of effectiveness for imlunestrant. FDA agrees that the totality of data support the proposed dosage of imlunestrant 400 mg QD for the indicated patient population. As described in **Section 19.4.2**, the corrected E-R analysis for efficacy did not demonstrate a relationship between imlunestrant exposure and PFS.
L585: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L586: ## Data:
L587: Please see Section 6.2.2 and Section 6.2.2.1.
L588: ## The Applicant's Position:
L589: Yes.
L590: The proposed 400 mg QD dose is effective and generally well tolerated in the proposed patient population with no dosing difference based on demographic characteristics. No new safety findings were identified in Phase 3 clinical development with AEs being effectively managed through dose interruption or reduction, or standard clinical practice. Safety data were consistent across clinical trials and with the mechanism of action. AEs were transient and reversible upon imlunestrant discontinuation. See Section 8.2.
L591: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L592: FDA agrees that imlunestrant 400 mg PO QD without food (taking imlunestrant at least 2 hours before food, or 1 hour after food) is an acceptable dosage for the indicated patient population. See **Section 6.2.2.1** for additional information.
L593: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?
L594: ## Data:
L595: Please see Section 6.2.2, Section 6.2.2.1, and Section 8.
L596: ## The Applicant's Position:
L597: Yes.
L598: Dose adjustment of imlunestrant to 200 mg QD is recommended in moderate and severe hepatic impairment.
L599: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L600: ## I. Age, Race/Ethnicity, Body Weight
L601: FDA agrees that no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of imlunestrant were observed based on age (28 to 95 years), race (64% White, 23% Asian, and 5% Black or African American), ethnicity (74% non-Hispanic or Latino, 17% Hispanic or Latino), body weight (36 to 145 kg), or UGT1A1 genetic polymorphisms (e.g., UGT1A1*1/*28 or UGT1A1*28/*28; see below for additional information).
L602: Based on a popPK analysis, body weight (range 36 to 145 kg) showed statistically significant impact on clearance and volume parameters through allometric relationships. Patients at the 95th percentile weight (100.4 kg) had 40% higher clearance versus median weight (68.2 kg), while 5th percentile patients (48.5 kg) had 26% lower clearance. However, this impact is not considered clinically significant given the flat E-R relationships for efficacy and safety. See the FDA's Assessment in **Section 19.4.1** for details.
L603: ## II. Patients with Renal Impairment
L604: Based on PopPK analysis, there are no clinically significant differences in pharmacokinetics of imlunestrant in patients with mild to moderate (eGFR 30 – 89 mL/min calculated based on CKD-EPI equation) RI as compared to patients with normal renal function (eGFR \ge90 mL/min). The effect of severe (eGFR 15- 29 mL/min) RI and patients on dialysis on imlunestrant pharmacokinetics is unknown. However, urinary elimination of imlunestrant was 0.3% of the total radioactivity excreted indicating limited renal clearance. Therefore, a dedicated study in subjects with severe RI is not warranted.
L605: ## III. Patients with Hepatic Impairment
L606: There were no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of imlunestrant in subjects with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A) based on the popPK analysis.
L607: Imlunestrant AUC0-inf increased 2.2-fold in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) and dose-normalized AUC increased 3.1-fold in subjects with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh C) compared to subjects with normal hepatic function; see **Table 6.3**.
L608: FDA agrees that the recommended imlunestrant dosage of 200 mg QD is appropriate for patients with moderate or severe HI and that no dosage modification is recommended for patients with mild HI. Based on the observed increase in imlunestrant exposure, the recommended dosage of 200 mg QD is likely to result in higher imlunestrant exposure in patients with severe HI
L609: compared to patients with normal hepatic function who receive 400 mg QD. Based on the available safety data from imlunestrant dosages up to 1,200 mg QD and the E-R safety analyses, the increase in exposure in patients with severe HI is still expected to remain within the safety margins; however, FDA recommends monitoring for increased adverse reactions in patients with severe HI given the increased rates of GI toxicities (nausea and diarrhea) observed at imlunestrant 800 mg QD compared to imlunestrant 400 mg QD in EMBER-2.

L613: ## IV. ESRI Mutations
L614: In EMBER-3, circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) was analyzed using the FDA-approved Guardant360 CDx to detect the presence of *ESRI* mutations at baseline prior to initiating study treatment. A total of 37.9% (323/853) of patients had an *ESRI* mutation detected. Among these patients, the four most common *ESRI* mutations detected were D538G (21.8%), Y537S (12.9%), Y537N (9.5%), and E380Q (7.7%) (Table 6.4). Some patients had more than one *ESRI* mutation
L615: detected. The prevalence of *ESR1* mutation detected and the type of mutations were generally balanced across treatment arms.
L616: FDA conducted exploratory analyses assessing the impact of *ESR1* mutations on imlunestrant efficacy in the *ESR1* mutation-detected (*ESR1*m-detected) population. Hazard ratios for PFS of imlunestrant vs control treatment were estimated from Cox proportional hazards model. Participants carrying single *ESR1* mutations demonstrated greater treatment effects for imlunestrant compared to control than those carrying multiple *ESR1* mutations. Participants carrying the D538G and Y537N mutations showed the largest treatment effects [(D538G: HR=0.52, 95% CI: 0.31-0.90, p-value=0.0181) and (Y537N: HR=0.40, 95% CI: 0.09-1.73, p-value = 0.2213)], while participants carrying only the Y537S mutation showed the smallest treatment effect (HR=0.79, 95% CI: 0.40-1.56, p-value=0.4954). These results suggest that while the HR point estimates were &lt; 1 across the evaluated *ESR1* mutations, the magnitude of benefit of imlunestrant compared to standard of care (SOC) may differ depending on the *ESR1* mutation profile (Table 6.5). There are several limitations to these subgroup analyses. First, the study was not designed to assess treatment effect within these subgroups, and these exploratory analyses were not stratified for other factors such as prior treatments or visceral metastasis. Second, the number of participants were small in some of these subgroups. Therefore, although these results suggest variable response based on *ESR1* mutations, a broad indication across different *ESR1* mutations is justified. Further exploration of these relationships with other *ESR1*-targeting drugs is warranted.

L624: ## V. Impact of UGT1A1 polymorphisms
L625: In vitro studies demonstrated that uridine 5'-diphospho-glucuronosyltransferase (UGT)1A1 is involved in the clearance of imlunestrant, however, the relative contribution of various UGT enzymes toward the overall clearance of imlunestrant remains undetermined. The sponsor performed a retrospective exploratory analysis to explore the association of *UGT1A1* polymorphisms with imlunestrant exposure using a subset of participants enrolled in EMBER-3 and healthy women of childbearing potential enrolled in Study J2J-MC-JZLD. Paired genetic and PK data were available for a total of 137 subjects. The following genotypes were observed: UGT1A1*1/*1, UGT1A1*1/*28, UGT1A1*28/*28, UGT1A1*36/*28, UGT1A1*1/*37, and
L626: UGT1A1*28/*37. Only reduced function haplotypes (*6, *28, *37) were included in the analysis. Individuals presenting with UGT1A1*36/*28 genotype were excluded from analysis because *36 is an increased function allele. Individuals with one reduced function haplotype were grouped together (UGT1A1*1/*37 was grouped with UGT1A1*1/*28) as intermediate metabolizers, and individuals with two reduced function haplotypes were grouped together (UGT1A1*28/*37 was grouped with UGT1A1*28/*28) as poor metabolizers.
L627: Results showed that *UGT1A1* genotype does not have an apparent effect on dose-normalized exposure (i.e., C_{max} and AUC) (Table 6.6). Safety data suggested that the safety profiles for participants who were *UGT1A1* extensive metabolizers were comparable to participants who were intermediate and poor metabolizers, consistent with the findings that *UGT1A1* polymorphisms did not affect imlunestrant exposure.

L631: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L632: ## Data:
L633: Please see Section 6.2.2.2.
L634: ## The Applicant's Position:
L635: ## Inluriyo (imlunestrant)
L636: Yes.
L637: Strong CYP3A4 inducers should be avoided, and alternative agents considered when coadministered with imlunestrant. If concomitant use is unavoidable, imlunestrant dose should be increased by 200 mg QD since there was a positive exposure-efficacy relationship.
L638: Imlunestrant should be dosed in the fasted state. Patients should not eat at least (b) (4) before and at least (b) (4) after taking imlunestrant.
L639: (b) (4)
L640: Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors should be
L641: BCRP, and P-gp substrates for which a small increase in concentration could lead to significant AEs.
L642: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L643: ## I. Food effect:
L644: Administration of a single dose of imlunestrant 400 mg with a low-fat meal (approximately 475 calories with 13% fat, 16% protein, and 71% carbohydrates) increased imlunestrant C_{max} 3.6-fold and AUC_{0-inf} 2-fold as compared to administration under fasting conditions. Therefore, FDA agrees with the Applicant that imlunestrant should be administered in the fasted state (at least 2 hours before and at least 1 hour after food). In EMBER-3, patients were instructed to take imlunestrant at least 2 hours before or 1 hour after food (i.e., the recommended administration instructions) therefore, EMBER-3 evaluated safety of imlunestrant under the recommended administration instructions with regard to food.
L645: ## II. Drug-Drug Interactions:
L646: ## 1. Effects of Other Drugs on Imlunestrant
L647: a) **Strong CYP3A Inhibitors:** Co-administration of imlunestrant with itraconazole 200 mg (strong CYP3A inhibitor) for multiple days, increased imlunestrant AUC_{0-inf} 2.1-fold and increased C_{max} 1.9-fold (**Table 6.7**). Therefore, FDA disagrees with the Applicant's proposal of (b) (4) when imlunestrant is administered with a strong CYP3A inhibitor. Dosage modification for concomitant use with strong CYP3A inhibitors should be included in the labeling with the recommendation to avoid concomitant use of strong CYP3A inhibitors and if concomitant use is unavoidable, decrease the imlunestrant dosage to 200 mg once daily.

L657: moderate CYP3A inhibitors or inducers are not warranted as the magnitude of exposure change is expected to be less than that observed with strong CYP3A inhibitors/inducers and not clinically significant.
L658: ## 2. Effect of Imlunestrant on Other Drugs:
L659: a) **P-gp substrates:** Digoxin (P-gp substrate) AUC_{0-inf} increased 1.4-fold and C_{max} increased 1.6-fold following concomitant use of single dose imlunestrant 400 mg (Table 6.9).

b) **BCRP substrates:** Rosuvastatin (BCRP substrate) AUC_{0-inf} increased 1.5-fold and C_{max} increased 1.7-fold following concomitant use of single dose imlunestrant 400 mg (Table 6.10).
L660: (b) (4)
L661: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's proposal
L662: Concomitant use of P-gp and BCRP substrates should be avoided unless otherwise recommended in the Prescribing Information of these substrates given that concomitant use with imlunestrant leads to an increase in exposure of these substrates which may increase the risk of adverse reactions related to substrate exposure.

L670: c) **CYP2D6 substrates:** Dextromethorphan (sensitive CYP2D6 substrate) AUC_{0-\infty} increased 1.3-fold and C_{\max} increased 1.4-fold following concomitant use of single dose of imlunestrant 800 mg. Based on evaluation of DDI studies using dextromethorphan and other CYP2D6 substrates, dextromethorphan is a more sensitive substrate of CYP2D6 than other CYP2D6 substrates. Therefore, based on the observed increase in dextromethorphan exposure following concomitant use of imlunestrant, imlunestrant is unlikely to result in a clinically significant increase in exposure for CYP2D6 substrates. FDA recommended (b) (4)
L671: d) **Other Drugs:** There were no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of midazolam (CYP3A substrate) or repaglinide (CYP2C8 substrate) observed when used concomitantly with imlunestrant 400 mg QD.
L672: ## 3. In vitro study findings:
L673: a) **CYP Enzymes:** Although imlunestrant is an inhibitor of CYP2B6 and CYP2C9 in vitro, the extent of inhibition is not considered clinically significant given that the R values for CYP2B6 (1.042) and CYP2C9 (1.026) were similar to the R value for CYP2C8 (1.027) which demonstrated a lack of clinically significant in vivo inhibition. Imlunestrant is not an inhibitor of CYP1A2. Imlunestrant is not an inducer of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP2C9.
L674: b) **Transporter Systems:** Imlunestrant is not a substrate of BCRP, OCT1, OATP1B1, or OATP1B3.

L697: ## The Applicant's Position:
L698: The primary study supporting the evaluation of safety and efficacy in this NDA is EMBER-3. A detailed description of the results is provided in Section 8.
L699: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L700: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the trials for imlunestrant listed in Table 7.1. The FDA's evaluation of efficacy and safety is mainly based on results from Study J2J-OX-JZLC (EMBER-3), hereafter EMBER-3.
L701: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L702: ## 8.1 Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L703: EMBER-3 is a global, open-label, Phase 3, randomized study in patients with ER+, HER2- locally ABC or MBC after disease progression on AI with or without a CDK4/6i. The design of this study was discussed with the FDA at the End-of-Phase 2 meeting, and the final SAP was reviewed by the Agency prior to locking the database.
L704: ## 8.1.1 EMBER-3: A Phase 3, Randomized, Open-Label Study of Imlunestrant, Investigator's Choice of Endocrine Therapy, and Imlunestrant plus Abemaciclib in Patients with ER+, HER2- Locally Advanced or Metastatic Breast Cancer Previously Treated with Endocrine Therapy
L705: ## Trial Design
L706: ## The Applicant's Description:
L707: ## *Design and methodology*
L708: Study J2J-OX-JZLC (EMBER-3) was a global, open-label, Phase 3, randomized study in patients with ER+, HER2- locally ABC or MBC after disease progression on AI with or without a CDK4/6i. Prior CDK4/6i treatment was expected if it was approved and reimbursed.
L709: EMBER-3 had 3 treatment arms:
L710: - Arm A: Imlunestrant (hereinafter referred to as the **imlunestrant** arm)
- Arm B: Investigator's Choice of ET (fulvestrant or exemestane, **SOC** arm)
- Arm C: Imlunestrant plus abemaciclib (**imlun+abema** arm).
L711: EMBER-3 enrolled 874 patients from October 2021 to November 2023. Initially, patients were randomly assigned 1:1 between the **imlunestrant** arm and the **SOC** arm. **imlun+abema** arm was added in Amendment (a), which was approved in October 2021 prior to the first patient entering treatment. Enrollment to the **imlun+abema** arm began in March 2022 at which point 122 patients had been randomly assigned across the **imlunestrant** and **SOC** arms. All 3 arms remained open until the target enrollment for the **imlunestrant** arm and the **SOC** arm was met. At the end of enrollment, 426 patients had been concurrently assigned at random between the **imlunestrant** arm and the **imlun+abema** arm.
L712: This pivotal study evaluated 3 comparisons for investigator-assessed PFS (primary endpoint):
L713: - In the ITT population, **imlunestrant** (Arm A) versus **SOC** (Arm B)
L714: - In the *ESR1m* subpopulation, **imlunestrant** (Arm A) versus **SOC** (Arm B)
- In the ITT population, **imlun+abema** (Arm C) versus **imlunestrant** (Arm A).
L715: **Note:** The efficacy analyses include only patients concurrently enrolled in Arms A and C. Safety analyses include all treated patients, whether concurrent or not.
L716: **Note:** The analysis of **imlun+abema** arm versus **imlunestrant** arm was only conducted if the primary endpoints for PFS in the ITT population or in the *ESR1m* subpopulation were significant.
L717: Participants were randomly assigned using the following stratification factors (Figure 8.1):
L718: - previous treatment with any CDK4/6i (yes versus no)
- presence of visceral metastases (yes versus no); visceral includes lung, liver, brain, pleural, and peritoneal involvement, and
- region (East Asia versus North America/Western Europe versus Others).
L719: Abbreviations: AI = aromatase inhibitor; BID = twice daily; CDK = cyclin-dependent kinase; ER = estrogen receptor; HER2 = human epidermal growth factor receptor 2; mTOR = mammalian target of rapamycin; PI3K = phosphoinositide 3-kinase; PO = orally; QD = once daily; R = randomization; SERD = selective estrogen receptor degrader.
L720: ## **Figure 8.1.** **EMBER-3 study schema.**
L721: ## ***Eligibility criteria***
L722: Eligible patients were required to be 18 years or older with a diagnosis of ER+, HER2- breast cancer per ASCO-CAP guidelines per local testing and either locally advanced or metastatic disease. In addition to postmenopausal women, men and premenopausal women with concomitant use of a GnRH agonist were also eligible. Patients were required to have 1 of the 3 scenarios given below:
L723: - relapsed while on or within 12 months of completion of (neo)adjuvant AI, alone or in combination with a CDK4/6i, with no treatment for advanced disease
- relapsed after 12 months from completion of (neo)adjuvant ET (ET, tamoxifen, or AI), with subsequent progression on or after only 1 line of therapy with an AI, alone or in combination with a CDK4/6i, or
- presented with de novo metastatic disease, with subsequent progression on or after only 1 line of therapy with an AI, alone or in combination with a CDK4/6i.
L724: Additionally, per RECIST v1.1, patients were required to have either measurable disease or non-measurable bone-only disease.
L725: No chemotherapy for ABC was permitted. In addition, patients with prior use of fulvestrant, investigational SERDs, or other targeted agents except CDK4/6i were not allowed. Patients with inflammatory breast cancer, visceral crisis, symptomatic or untreated central nervous system metastasis, or carcinomatous meningitis were excluded.
L726: ## ***Study treatment***
L727: Table 8.1 outlines the study interventions.

L731: Abbreviations: BID = twice daily; C1D1 = Cycle 1 Day 1; C1D15 = Cycle 1 Day 15; QD = once every day.
L732: a Per local label.
L733: b Patients were instructed not to consume any food at least 1 hour before and at least 2 hours after administration.
L734: Source: Table JZLC.3.2
L735: ## Planned measurements and timing of assessments
L736: The protocol Schedule of Activities outlines the schedule, measurements, and collection methods of the efficacy, PK, safety, and biomarker assessments. Radiological imaging occurred at baseline and then at regular intervals thereafter until radiographic disease progression, death, start of a new anticancer therapy or study completion, whichever occurred first.
L737: Patients who discontinued from the protocol treatment were required to enter a follow-up period. Following a short-term follow-up of approximately 30 days in which safety assessments were completed, patients continued in long-term follow-up for assessment of SAEs and OS (assessed every 12 weeks).
L738: The protocol details the collection and assessment of safety information (evaluation, definitions, recording, and reporting of AEs, SAEs, and other reportable safety events).
L739: ## Dose selection discussion
L740: In the Phase 1 EMBER study, imlunestrant was administered at 5 dose levels (200 to 1200 mg QD). At the 200-mg dose, the trough concentrations of the majority of patients did not exceed the TEC80 range (therapeutic effective concentration required for 80% target inhibition) and thereby did not have sustained biologically relevant concentrations over the entire dosing interval, which was also supported by the popPK study. This threshold has been associated with tumor regression in xenograft models, and therefore, considered a relevant benchmark. With doses at and above 400 mg, the TEC80 range was exceeded throughout the dosing interval; 400 mg being the lowest dose that satisfied the desired PK requirements. In addition, there were notable differences in the frequency of low grade side effects (such as nausea) between 400 mg and higher doses, which could have potentially impacted long-term adherence to imlunestrant. Moreover, evidence of clinical activity was observed at the 400-mg dose, with 4 of 6 imlunestrant monotherapy partial responses observed across the study seen at the 400-mg dose level. Therefore, this was chosen as the registration dose for the EMBER-3 study.
L741: At the same time, the EMBER-2 window-of-opportunity study evaluated 3 dose levels (200 mg, 400 mg, and 800 mg) to assess for effects on key biologically relevant PD biomarkers in
L742: postmenopausal women with EBC. Though similar on-target effects with substantial ER degradation were observed across all doses, the 200 mg QD dose had the lowest rate of complete cell cycle arrest and the 800 mg QD dose had the highest incidence of nausea and diarrhea. This further supported the ongoing EMBER-3 registration dose at 400 mg QD and a dose adjustment to 200 mg QD as needed.
L743: Overall, the PK, safety, and initial clinical activity in EMBER supported the imlunestrant monotherapy starting registration dose at 400 mg QD in EMBER-3.
L744: ## Choice of comparator discussion
L745: ## ***Imlunestrant (Arm A) versus SOC (Arm B)***
L746: For the patients enrolled in EMBER-3, investigator's choice of SOC (Arm B), fulvestrant or exemestane, was an appropriate comparator for **imlunestrant** monotherapy (Arm A). Investigator's choice of **SOC** reflects the alternatives commonly used for ET-pretreated patients, is consistent with recommended options in treatment guidelines globally, and allows for physician and patient preferences between both agents.
L747: ## Open-label design
L748: EMBER-3 is an open-label study. Each participant in this study was aware of his or her own assigned treatment group. However, the open-label design was inevitable due to the route of administration for the study treatments:
L749: - oral for imlunestrant and exemestane, and
- intramuscular for fulvestrant.
L750: Although the participants and staff involved in treating and caring for participants were aware of participants' treatment assignments, the sponsor remained strictly blinded to the aggregate data prior to database lock.
L751: The primary and secondary endpoints rely on objective measures, helping to control potential bias. Notably, the primary endpoint of investigator-assessed PFS was repeated by BIRC, both using RECIST v1.1, to
L752: - assess any potential bias introduced through investigator assessments, and
- show consistency of treatment effect regardless of investigator or BIRC assessment.
L753: Sensitivity analyses were prespecified to evaluate the robustness of the primary analysis.
L754: ## Stratification factors discussion
L755: Stratification factors are known prognostic factors with the potential to influence the primary PFS objective and were included to reduce potential bias.
L756: *ESR1m* status was not a stratification factor. With the sample size of this EMBER-3 study and the known prevalence, up to 50% Bhave et al. 2024), of *ESR1m* incidence among patients
L757: receiving prior AI-based treatments, it was expected that the treatment arms would be balanced for *ESR1m*.
L758: ## *Prior CDK4/6i discussion*
L759: To reflect real-world clinical practice and global differences in CDK4/6i access in the advanced and metastatic setting, EMBER-3 also included patients who did not have previous CDK4/6i treatment.
L760: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L761: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of the EMBER-3 trial with the following comments and clarifications. Of note, throughout this review, FDA refers to the control arm in this trial as treatment of physician's choice (TPC), as the FDA believes this is more accurately reflective of the options in the EMBER-3 trial. The Applicant refers to this arm as standard of care (SOC).
L762: ## **Eligibility criteria**
L763: - Patients were required to have an ECOG performance status of 0 or 1 to be eligible for this trial. The FDA notes that men and women with an ECOG performance status of greater than 1 and ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer are routinely treated with endocrine monotherapy. Thus, the enrolled population is not representative of the whole US population that may receive imlunestrant.
- Patients who were eligible for PARPi therapy were excluded. It is not clear that these patients would not benefit from later lines of endocrine therapy; however, this was not adequately assessed in EMBER-3.
L764: ## **Prior CDK4/6i therapy**
L765: - The FDA cautioned the Applicant February 11, 2021, at a Type B, EOP1 meeting that most patients should have received prior CDK4/6i therapy to be representative of a US population. The Applicant submitted amendment (a) of the protocol to IND 145311 on October 11, 2021, that required prior CDK4/6i where available and reimbursed. On September 1, 2022, amendment (b) of the protocol was submitted which also allowed the Applicant to limit the number of patients enrolled that had not received prior CDK4/6i therapy.
- Although the study included both patients who did and did not receive prior CDK4/6i therapy, the prespecified analyses were not powered and are exploratory only.
- Practice patterns regarding the choice of CDK4/6i appear to be changing based on recent data that supports an overall survival benefit for some CDK4/6i given in a metastatic setting.
L766: ## **Stratification factors**
L767: - The original design of the EMBER-3 trial included (b) (4), the prevalence of *ESR1m* was considered large enough (b) (4). See also FDA's review of Presubmission Regulatory Activities in Section 3.2 of this Assessment Aid review.
- The FDA also cautioned the Applicant that if differential activity was observed based on specific *ESR1* mutations (e.g., Y537S), the lack of stratification by specific mutations would be a review issue.
L768: ## Detection of mutations, including *ESR1*
L769: - Patients were required to provide blood samples of circulating tumor DNA analysis. Samples were analyzed using Oncoscreen520 Plus (Burning Rock - China) and Guardant360 (Guardant Health – rest of the world). Results were not required before randomization since this was not a stratification factor. As a result, some patients on EMBER-3 had an unknown *ESR1m* status. According to the Applicant's analyses patients were designated "*ESR1m*-detected" if either test showed a ligand binding domain mutation or "*ESR1m*-not detected" if no *ESR1* mutation(s) was detected, if the test did not detect ctDNA, or test results were missing.
- No tissue testing for *ESR1m* was performed.
- The Applicant clarified in an IR response on February 28, 2025, that patients and investigators were blinded to *ESR1m* status except for one patient (Subject ID: (b) (6)) who requested this information about 9 months after disease progression on treatment of physician's choice (TPC) treatment (fulvestrant). The Applicant does not have any data to show whether patients may have obtained information on *ESR1m* status outside of EMBER-3. Five patients with *ESR1* mutations received subsequent therapy with an oral SERD. Three were assigned to the TPC arm (Subject IDs: (b) (6)) and two were assigned to the imlunestrant arm (Subject IDs: (b) (6)).
- One patient (Subject ID: (b) (6)) enrolled in South Korea and randomized to the TPC arm had a *BRCA2* R2494* nonsense mutation with a VAF of 51%, indicating a germline mutation. This patient received saruparib after progression.
L770: ## Open-label design
L771: - The FDA agrees that the open-label design benefited patients on the trial by allowing them to forego a placebo injection. A prospectively planned sensitivity analysis of PFS by BIRC was also performed on all patients.
L772: ## Control arm
L773: At the time of that the EMBER-3 trial was designed, ET monotherapy was an acceptable comparator. However, since that time it has become clear that fulvestrant monotherapy underperforms as a standard of care therapy after prior CDK4/6i therapy. Additionally,
L774: elacestrant was approved in the US for patients with ER+, HER2- metastatic breast cancer and an *ESR1*m during the conduct of EMBER-3.
L775: ## Concomitant therapy
L776: - Patients were allowed to receive palliative radiotherapy on the trial if no other systemic or local therapy was effective to manage painful metastases. The Applicant provided sensitivity analyses to assess the impact of concomitant palliative radiotherapy on investigator assessed PFS.
L777: The FDA did not review any study elements related to the combination (Arm C) as it is out of the scope of the proposed indication under this NDA.
L778: ## Study Endpoints
L779: ## The Applicant's Description:
L780: ## Choice of investigator-assessed PFS as the primary endpoint
L781: Investigator-assessed PFS is an appropriate endpoint in MBC as demonstrated by recent pivotal studies supporting approvals in this setting (FDA 2019). Meta-analyses indicate that investigator-assessed PFS provides a reliable estimate of treatment effect (Amit et al. 2011; Dello Russo and Navarra 2022). Importantly, PFS as assessed by BIRC was a secondary endpoint to assess potential for bias in investigator assessments (Amit et al. 2011).
L782: Table 8.2 details the key primary and secondary objectives.

L790: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L791: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of study endpoints for the comparison of imlunestrant vs TPC in the overall population and in the *ESR1*-m-detected population. In general, PFS of appropriate magnitude is an acceptable endpoint to support traditional approval, when supported by other endpoints—such as blinded independent central review (BICR) and objective response rate (ORR)—and when results are consistent across subgroups.
L792: The PRO-based time-to-event endpoint of time to sustained worsening of the “worst pain” as measured by Worst Pain NRS was challenging to interpret due to factors such as defining deterioration, accounting for intercurrent events, and potential missing data, as described in the literature.
L793: The FDA did not review any study elements related to the combination (imlun+abema) as it is out of the scope of the proposed indication under this NDA.
L794: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L795: ## The Applicant's Description:
L796: ## *Analysis of sample size*
L797: For **imlunestrant** treatment arm compared to patients enrolled in the **SOC** treatment arm in the ITT population, the sample size calculation assumed the median PFS of **SOC** arm to be 4.3 months, and the hazard ratio of 0.74 amounts to an approximate 1.5-month improvement in median PFS under the assumption of exponential survival distribution. The assumed median PFS of **SOC** arm was estimated based on an unpublished meta-analysis of historical controls. A total of 480 events yields at least 76% power to detect superiority of **imlunestrant** treatment arm over **SOC** treatment arm with the 1-sided log-rank test at the initial significance level of 0.005.
L798: For **imlunestrant** treatment arm compared to patients enrolled in the **SOC** treatment arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, the sample size calculation assumed the median PFS of **SOC** arm in the subset is 3.6 months, the hazard ratio of 0.57 amounts to an approximate 2.7-month improvement in median PFS under the assumption of exponential survival distribution. A total of 192 events yields approximately 97% power to detect superiority of **imlunestrant** treatment arm over **SOC** treatment arm with the 1-sided log-rank test at the significance level of 0.02.
L799: For **imlun+abema** treatment arm compared to **imlunestrant** treatment arm in the concurrent ITT population, if the median PFS of **imlunestrant** treatment arm is assumed to be 5.8 months (assuming the target HR for **imlunestrant** treatment arm compared to patients enrolled in the **SOC** treatment arm in the ITT population is met), the hazard ratio of 0.7 then amounts to an approximate 2.5-month improvement in median PFS under the assumption of exponential survival distribution. A total of 248 events yields at least 80% power to detect superiority of **imlun+abema** treatment arm over **imlunestrant** treatment arm with the 1-sided log-rank test at the significance level of 0.025.
L800: Initially, patients were randomly assigned 1:1 between the **imlunestrant** arm and the **SOC** arm. Amendment (a) of the protocol, which added the **imlun+abema** arm, was approved before first patient visit, and the enrollment of this arm started approximately 6 months after recruitment into EMBER-3 began. After the addition of the **imlun+abema** arm, patients were randomly assigned 1:1:1 into each treatment arm. Under the enrollment assumptions, it is estimated that a total number of 860 participants were to be enrolled in this study. Specifically, approximately 320 participants were to be enrolled in the **imlunestrant** arm or the **SOC** treatment arms, and approximately 220 participants in the **imlun+abema** treatment arm.
L801: EMBER-3 was appropriately powered for the 3 PFS comparisons.
L802: ## *Statistical analysis*
L803: The primary endpoints of PFS were evaluated in 3 comparisons:
L804: - compare **imlunestrant** treatment arm and the **SOC** treatment arm in the ITT population
- compare **imlunestrant** treatment arm and **SOC** treatment arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, and
- compare **imlun+abema** treatment arm and **imlunestrant** treatment arm in the concurrent ITT population.
L805: To adjust for multiplicity and control the overall type I error rate at 0.025 (1-sided), a graphical approach (Bretz et al. 2009; Mauer and Bretz 2013) was used to test the 3 PFS primary endpoints and the 3 key secondary OS endpoints. Figure 8.2 represents the graph with initially allocated significance levels at each node and the associated weights for each directed edge from these respective nodes.
L806: The graphical testing scheme ensured that H3 (PFS between the **imlun+abema** arm and **imlunestrant** arm in the concurrent ITT population) would only be tested if at least 1 of the
L807: monotherapy comparisons (H_1 or H_2) was rejected, and the key secondary OS hypotheses (H_4, H_5, and H_6) would be tested only if the corresponding primary PFS hypotheses were rejected.
L808: Abbreviations: *ESR1* = estrogen receptor 1; H_1 = PFS between Arm A and Arm B in the ITT population; H_2 = PFS between Arm A and Arm B in the *ESR1*-mutation-detected population; H_3 = PFS between Arm C and Arm A in the concurrent ITT population; H_4 = OS between Arm A and Arm B in the ITT population; H_5 = OS between Arm A and Arm B in the *ESR1*-mutation-detected population; H_6 = OS between Arm C and Arm A in the concurrent ITT population; imlun+abema = imlunestrant in combination with abemaciclib; ITT = intention to treat; OS = overall survival; PFS = progression-free survival; SOC = standard of care investigator's choice endocrine therapy of fulvestrant or exemestane.
L809: Arm A = imlunestrant; Arm B = SOC; Arm C = imlun+abema.
L810: Small edge weights are represented by dotted lines. \epsilon denotes an infinitesimally small number (\epsilon = 10^{-4}).
L811: **Figure 8.2.** Initial graphical representation of the testing scheme.
L812: ## *Analysis of the primary endpoints*
L813: The primary endpoint was investigator-assessed PFS. The Kaplan-Meier method (Kaplan and Meier 1958) was used to estimate the PFS curves. Median PFS and PFS rates at various time points with 95% CIs were estimated for each arm. The comparison of PFS curves between treatment arms was conducted by a stratified log-rank test as the primary analysis, stratified by the randomization strata (previous treatment with CDK4/6i, presence of visceral metastases, region). The treatment effect was estimated by hazard ratio with its corresponding 95% CIs using the stratified Cox proportional hazard model with treatment as the only covariate, stratified by the randomization strata.
L814: PFS comparing **imlunestrant** treatment arm and **SOC** treatment arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population was analyzed in the same way, except that region was excluded in the stratified analysis to reduce the number of strata.
L815: Multiple sensitivity analyses were conducted to evaluate the robustness of the primary analysis.
L816: ## *Analysis of the key secondary endpoints*
L817: Overall survival between **imlunestrant** treatment arm and the **SOC** treatment arm in the ITT population, OS between **imlunestrant** treatment arm and **SOC** treatment arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, and OS between **imlun+abema** treatment arm and **imlunestrant** treatment arm in the concurrent ITT population are the key secondary endpoints.
L818: OS curves, median OS, and OS rates at 1 year and 2 years with 95% CI for each treatment arm were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method. OS was compared between treatment arms using a log-rank test, stratified by the same factors as PFS. The corresponding hazard ratio between treatment arms was estimated using a stratified Cox regression model. The inferential analysis of OS was based on the stratified analyses.
L819: ## *Pre-specified interim analyses*
L820: For the primary endpoint of PFS between imlunestrant treatment arm and the SOC treatment arm in the ITT population, one interim futility analysis was to be conducted when approximately 192 (40% information fraction) investigator-assessed events had occurred. No interim analysis was conducted for PFS comparing **imlunestrant** treatment arm and the **SOC** treatment arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L821: For the key secondary endpoint of OS for **imlunestrant** treatment arm and the **SOC** treatment arm in the ITT population, the interim and final analyses were planned at the number of events summarized in Table 8.3.
L822: **Table 8.3.** Timing of OS Analyses in the ITT Population
L823: OS look 1	At the time of final analysis of PFS in the ITT population
OS look 2	Approximately 255 OS events
OS look 3	Approximately 330 OS events
Final OS	Approximately 390 events
L824: Abbreviations: ITT = intent-to-treat population; OS = overall survival; PFS = progression-free survival.
L825: For the OS comparing **imlunestrant** treatment arm and the **SOC** treatment in the *ESR1*-detected population, the interim and final analyses are summarized in Table 8.4.
L826: **Table 8.4.** Timing of OS Analyses in the *ESR1m*-Detected Population
L827: OS look 1	At the time of final analysis of PFS in the ITT population
OS look 2	At the time of OS look 2 in the ITT population
OS look 3	At the time of OS look 3 in the ITT population
Final OS	Approximately 155 events
L828: Abbreviations: ITT = intent-to-treat population; OS = overall survival; PFS = progression-free survival.
L829: ## *Analysis of prespecified subgroups*
L830: Prespecified subgroup analyses of PFS were conducted for each PFS comparison. Results are displayed in forest plots in the CSR.
L831: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L832: FDA generally agrees with the description of the statistical analysis plan for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population and in the *ESR1m*-detected population except the description of the interim analysis of PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L833: Per SAP Version 5 (the latest version prior to data unblinding), the final analysis of PFS between the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population was planned to be conducted when 480 PFS events were observed. At the time of final PFS analysis between the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population, if approximately 192 PFS events had been observed for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, the final PFS analysis for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population was planned to be conducted at the 1-sided alpha level of 0.02. However, if the number of PFS events for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population had not reached the target number of events (192 PFS events), one interim analysis for efficacy could be conducted if deemed appropriate, and the Lan-DeMets spending function (O'Brien-Fleming type) was planned to be used to determine the boundaries that the interim and final analyses for PFS in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L834: Of note, Applicant's description of stratified analyses (stratified log-rank test and stratified Cox proportional hazard model) stratified by the randomization strata (previous treatment with CDK4/6i, presence of visceral metastases, region) are for the primary analysis of PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population. In the primary analysis of PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, the stratification factor of region was excluded in the stratified analyses to reduce the number of strata. In a response to FDA's Information Request dated February 6, 2025, the Applicant clarified that region was removed from all stratified analyses in the *ESR1m*-detected population to reduce the number of strata. This included the analyses of PFS by investigator
L835: assessment, OS, and PFS by BIRC in the *ESR1m*-detected population for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm and the comparison of imlun+abema arm and imlunestrant arm. Similarly, region was also removed from the exploratory stratified PFS and OS analyses in the *ESR1m*-nd population.
L836: Per SAP Version 5, the first interim OS analysis for all comparisons was planned to be conducted at the time of the final PFS analysis for the comparison of the imlunestrant and TPC in the ITT population. For the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, the timing of the second and third interim OS analyses was anchored to the OS endpoint for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population (i.e., event-driven based on the number of OS events for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population), and the timing of the final OS analysis was event-driven when 155 OS events were observed from the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population. For each OS comparison, the Lan-DeMets spending function (O'Brien-Fleming type) based on the actual observed number of events was used to determine the boundaries for the interim and final analyses of OS.
L837: Per SAP Version 5, the alpha of 0.025 (1-sided) was initially split between the PFS comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population (0.02 alpha) and the PFS comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population (0.005 alpha). The PFS comparison of imlun+abema arm and imlunestrant arm in the concurrent ITT population was planned to be tested only if any comparison of PFS for imlunestrant arm and TPC arm was statistically significant. The key secondary endpoint OS would be tested only if the corresponding primary PFS endpoint was statistically significant. No multiplicity adjustments were made for any efficacy endpoints in the *ESR1m*-nd population.
L838: The proposed indication for the current submission is the imlunestrant monotherapy in the *ESR1m*-detected population. This review is focused on the benefit/risk assessment in the comparison of imlunestrant monotherapy and TPC in the *ESR1m*-detected population only. The comparison of the imlun+abema arm and imlunestrant arm is not relevant to support the efficacy of imlunestrant monotherapy for the proposed indication under this NDA; therefore, details of efficacy analyses for the comparison of the imlun+abema arm and imlunestrant arm were not included in this review.
L839: ## Protocol Amendments

L841: The Sponsor implemented all changes in study conduct via 4 protocol amendments, labeled protocol Amendments (a) to (d).
L842: Table 8.5 outlines the approval dates of each protocol amendment.

inclusion Criterion #3, where patients were expected to have received prior treatment with a CDK4/6 inhibitor if approved and reimbursed.
