added more PFS sensitivity analyses
Approved and submitted to IND before the final primary outcome database lock but after futility interim analyses (unblinded interim data were only accessible by the DMC).
L852: Abbreviations: DMC = data monitoring committee; *ESR1* = estrogen receptor 1; IND = investigational new drug application; ITT = intention to treat; OS = overall survival PFS = progression-free survival; SAP = statistical analysis plan.
L853: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L854: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the protocol and SAP amendments with the following addition. SAP Version 5 was the latest version of the SAP finalized before data unblinding. At the pre-NDA meeting held on September 23, 2024, the Applicant asked if FDA would consider an SAP amendment for an updated analysis of OS. The FDA advised the Applicant to submit a proposal for review. The Applicant submitted proposed SAP Addendum v1 in a meeting request dated November 14, 2024 (FDA DARRTs Sequence Number 0165) under IND 145311. Note that SAP Addendum v1 was amended after data unblinding. In SAP Addendum v1, the Applicant proposed to remove the anchoring of the timing for interim OS analyses for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected
L855: population and the comparison of the imlun+abema arm and imlunestrant arm in the ITT population on the OS endpoint for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population. Instead, the Applicant proposed time-driven interims for the following OS endpoints: OS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1*m-detected population, and OS for the comparison of the imlun+abema arm and imlunestrant arm in the ITT population. After review, in the WRO meeting minutes dated December 20, 2024, the FDA advised the Applicant not to change the remaining formal OS tests between imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1*m population as they were made after data unblinding and could be data driven. Additionally, the FDA stated that changing from event-driven to the proposed calendar-based analyses would be likely to lead to inadequately powered analyses. FDA warned the Applicant that analyses conducted under the SAP Amendment v1 would be considered post-hoc and descriptive only.
L856: Against FDA advice, the Applicant proceeded with SAP Addendum 1 and conducted updated OS analyses with DCO date of January 30, 2025. Topline results of updated OS analyses based on the DCO date of January 30, 2025, were provided and discussed by the Applicant (b) (4) meeting held on May 23, 2025. The
L857: FDA considered these OS analyses post-hoc and descriptive only. Per meeting minutes, “The FDA agreed with the proposal to return to the plan of the future OS interim and final analyses as pre-specified initially in SAP Version 5 before data unblinding, .... The FDA reiterated that the Sponsor should only keep and perform the preplanned, event-driven OS analyses and revise the SAP back to Version 5. Sponsor acknowledged.”
L858: Per FDA’s Information Request dated February 6, 2025, the Applicant stated that the interim futility analysis of PFS for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population was conducted based on a data cutoff date of February 27, 2023, under the guidance of an independent DMC, who reviewed unblinded data prepared by the independent Statistical Analysis Center.
L859: The first oral selective estrogen receptor degraders (SERD), elacestrant, was approved by the FDA on January 27, 2023 in the ER-positive HER2-negative and *ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer in postmenopausal women and adult men who have progressed after at least one line of endocrine therapy. In addition, the designs of several registrational intent trials for other oral estrogen receptor antagonists, including camizestrant and vepdegestrant, which prespecified assessment in an *ESR1*m population were public before July 2023. The EMBER-3 protocol was amended (Protocol Amendment [d]) in July 2023. In Protocol Amendment [d], the initial alpha allocations for PFS for the comparisons of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population and in the *ESR1*m-detected population were changed from 1-sided 0.02 in the ITT population and 1-sided 0.005 in the *ESR1*m-detected population to 1-sided 0.005 in the ITT population and 1-sided 0.02 in the *ESR1*m-detected population. The Applicant stated that the modification of initial alpha allocations for PFS for the comparisons of the imlunestrant arm and
L860: TPC arm in the ITT population and in the *ESR1m*-detected population was based on the external data of oral estrogen receptor antagonists where clinically and statistically meaningful improvement in PFS has been primarily driven by the *ESR1m* population.
L861: ## 8.1.2 Study Results
L862: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L863: ## The Applicant's Position:
L864: The EMBER-3 study was conducted in accordance with the protocol and
L865: - consensus ethical principles derived from international guidelines, including the Declaration of Helsinki and Council for International Organizations of Medical Sciences International Ethical Guidelines
- applicable ICH GCP Guidelines, and
- applicable laws and regulations.
L866: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L867: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the EMBER-3 trial was conducted in compliance with the principals of Good Clinical Practice.
L868: ## Financial Disclosure
L869: ## Data:
L870: Eli Lilly and Company (Lilly) submitted a financial disclosure certification document in Module 1.3.4. This documentation provides a listing of all principal investigators and sub-investigators who participated at sites that enrolled patients in EMBER-3, indicating whether the investigators have provided a Certification (Form 3454) or a Disclosure statement (Form 3455).
L871: ## The Applicant's Position:
L872: Financial Disclosure information was collected from investigators across this multi-site, multinational study. Patients were randomly assigned across 195 sites in 21 countries (Argentina, Australia, Austria, Belgium, Brazil, China, France, Germany, Greece, India, Italy, Japan, Mexico, the Netherlands, Russia, South Korea, Spain, Taiwan, Turkey, Ukraine, and the United States). See Appendix 19.2 for details.
L873: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L874: The FDA reviewed Form 3454 and agrees with the Applicant's assessment that financial interests were unlikely to impact the results of the EMBER-3 trial. For more details regarding the FDA's assessment of financial disclosures see Section 19.2 of this review.

L880: ## ITT population
L881: The ITT population consisted of all participants randomly assigned to study treatment, regardless of whether any doses of study treatment were taken, or if the correct treatment was administered. Participants were analyzed according to the treatment group to which they were assigned. Table 8.7 provides the disposition in the ITT population, including those who remain on study treatment, who entered posttreatment discontinuation follow-up, and who remained on posttreatment discontinuation follow-up at the time of data cut.
L882: The most common reason for treatment discontinuation was PD, with very few patients discontinuing treatment due to AEs.

L889: ## *ESR1m-detected population*
L890: Determination of baseline *ESR1m* status was important to the primary objective, but not required prior to randomization as *ESR1m* status was not a stratification factor. There were 853 (97.6% of ITT) patients who had a baseline pretreatment ctDNA sample tested and 323 (37%) patients were positive for an *ESR1m*, including 4 patients from China.
L891: A total of 551 (63%) patients were designated *ESR1m*-not-detected, which included those whose blood sample had no *ESR1* mutation or detectable ctDNA present, or an unknown result (analytical failures or missing samples).
L892: Patients with *ESR1m*-detected population were generally balanced across the treatment arms.
L893: Table 8.8 provides the disposition of the *ESR1m* population.

L898: ## The Applicant's Position:
L899: Disease progression was the most common cause of study treatment discontinuation, with very few patients discontinuing treatment due to AEs. The frequency of treatment discontinuations due to AEs was low across all arms. Withdrawal after randomization and before treatment started were equal across all 3 arms. In EMBER-3 study, on treatment withdrawals for reasons other than disease progression were minimal and similar across treatment arms.
L900: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L901: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment of patient disposition in the ITT and ESR1m populations with the following additions:
L902: ## **Early discontinuations:**
L903: The FDA performed an analysis of patients in the intention-to-treat population who withdrew consent or were withdrawn by their treating physician between randomization and 56 days (2 cycles) of therapy. Given the open-label design of the EMBER-3 trial, patients may have considered not starting therapy or discontinuing from the study early if they were assigned to the TPC arm because of the added requirements on the trial to receive open-label approved therapies.
L904: Five patients (1.5%) assigned to the TPC arm and two patients (0.6%) assigned to the imlunestrant arm withdrew from the study before starting therapy. An additional patient (Subject ID: (b) (6)) assigned to the TPC arm withdrew after two doses of fulvestrant based on their physician's decision that "other treatment may be more beneficial." Finally, three patients (0.9%) withdrew consent on the TPC arm versus two patients (0.6%) on the imlunestrant arm within 56 days. One patient (Subject ID: (b) (6)) on the TPC arm was diagnosed with a germline BRCA mutation by Guardant360 testing and withdrew consent; however, no information on subsequent therapy was provided. Overall, discontinuations between randomization and the first two doses of study medication were rare but were numerically higher on the TPC arm. It is unlikely that these small differences led to imbalances between the two arms.
L905: The FDA did not evaluate patient dispositions on Arm C since this was out of scope for the proposed indication under this NDA.
L906: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L907: ## Data:
L908: Protocol deviations were identified, evaluated, and categorized as important or nonimportant prior to unblinding. A nonimportant deviation was one that did not significantly affect data reliability or patient's rights, safety, or well-being. An important deviation was one that could have significantly affected the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of data or potentially affected a patient's rights, safety, or well-being.
L909: Table 8.9 summarizes important protocol deviations for **imlunestrant** treatment arm (Arm A) versus the SOC arm (Arm B) in the ITT and the ESR1m-detected populations. These were similar across populations.

L916: ## The Applicant's Position:
L917: Lilly reviewed the protocol deviations and considered them unlikely to have substantially affected patient safety or the results and conclusions in this report.
L918: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L919: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's characterization of important protocol deviations in the ITT and ESR1m populations. The FDA also performed additional analyses of important protocol deviations by subcategories for the ITT population.
L920: The most common protocol deviations under visit schedule criteria were follow up imaging out of window or not performed (9.4% vs 9.0%) and imaging for suspected progression not performed within 14 days (1.5% vs 0.9%).
L921: The most common protocol deviations under inclusion and exclusion criteria were timing and prior lines of therapy (Inclusion Criteria #3: 5.1% vs 3.0%), measurable disease by RECIST v1.1 or non-measurable bone-only disease (Inclusion Criteria #8: 3.3% vs 1.5%), and presence of symptomatic or untreated brain metastasis (Exclusion Criteria #20: 2.7% vs 2.4%). The FDA requested further detail on the patients that did not meet eligibility criteria 3 (Table 8.10).

L925: The most common protocol deviation for inclusion criteria 3 was for patients who progressed less than 12 months after adjuvant ET and received therapy for advanced disease. Only one patient on the Imlunestrant arm had an *ESR1* mutation without prior AI therapy. The most common protocol deviations under treatment assignment were incorrect stratification factor (5.8% vs 5.2%) and incorrect characterization of visceral metastatic disease (4.8% vs 3.6%). Sensitivity analyses, including an unstratified analysis and a stratified analysis using stratification factors based on CRF data, were consistent with the primary analysis (Table 8.18).
L926: The FDA agrees that the protocol deviations were unlikely to have substantially impacted the efficacy results and did not alter the overall conclusions for the benefit: risk analysis for the intended indication.

L935: ## The Applicant's Position:
L936: Demographic and baseline characteristics of patients were adequately reflective of ER+, HER2-ABC, and were generally balanced across treatment arms. Baseline characteristics for age, sex, ECOG, region, menopausal status, presence of visceral metastases, bone-only disease, endocrine resistance, and prior CDK 4/6i use were balanced across treatment arms for ITT and ESR1m subgroups (Table 8.11).
L937: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L938: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment that patient demographics were well balanced between treatment arms in the ESR1m patient population, even though ESR1 mutation status was not a stratification factor at the randomization. Patients had a better performance status than would be expected for a US population receiving second-line endocrine therapy. No male patients with an ESR1m were enrolled. Few Black or African-American patients with ESR1m breast cancer were enrolled relative to the prevalence of ER-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer in the US. Collectively, these limit the interpretation of the results for some of the
L939: US population that are likely to receive imlunestrant; however, did not alter the overall conclusions for approval for the intended indication.

L948: ## The Applicant's Position:
L949: The baseline disease burden observed in the ESR1m-detected population was representative of patients with ER+, HER2-, locally advanced or MBC previously treated with ET, with or without a CDK4/6i and balanced across the treatment arms. This was also consistent for the broader ITT population.
L950: All EMBER-3 stratification factors (previous treatment with any CDK4/6i, presence of visceral metastasis, and region) were evenly distributed within the ESR1m-detected subpopulation.
L951: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L952: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment that baseline disease characteristics were well balanced between treatment arms in the ESR1m patient population. However, FDA performed an analysis of disease characteristics in ESR1m patients from the United States (US) and Western Europe (WE) that showed more bone-only disease and less visceral disease in patients on the TPC arm (**Table 8.13**). This may explain why the magnitude of PFS benefit was lower for the TPC arm in the US/WE region with a HR of 0.77 (95% CI 0.51, 1.17).

L955: *PIK3CA* mutations and AKT/mTOR pathway alterations were higher in the *ESR1m* population, consistent with co-occurrence in this patient population.
L956: While the trial excluded patients that were HER2-positive by ASCO/CAP Guidelines, the data to perform an analysis of patients that may have had HER2-low or HER2-ultralow breast cancer was substantially missing or not collected, respectively. The therapeutic landscape for HER-low and -ultralow was evolving during the conduct of EMBER-3, impacting available subsequent therapies based on timing and region. Therefore, no further analysis could be conducted for these populations due to this limitation in the data.

L959: ## *Treatment compliance*
L960: Treatment compliance for imlunestrant and exemestane was assessed through pill counts at each cycle.
L961: Compliance for fulvestrant was assured as the drug was administered at the investigator site.

L963: Any medication or vaccine (including over-the-counter or prescription medicines, vitamins, and/or herbal supplements) received while on study and during the short-term follow-up was recorded. Anticancer therapies (including, chemotherapy, immunotherapy) were not permitted while on study treatment.
L964: The frequency of patients who received GnRH and bone-modifying agents while on treatment was similar across treatment arms within both the overall safety population and the *ESR1m*-detected safety population.

L973: ## The Applicant's Position:
L974: Treatment compliance in this study was high across all treatment arms. The therapeutic classes of concomitant medication used were appropriate and were not considered to have impacted study results.
L975: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L976: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment of concomitant medications.
L977: The FDA also performed an analysis of subsequent therapies (Table 8.15). More patients with *ESR1m* disease on the TPC arm received their first CDK4/6 inhibitor after discontinuing EMBER-3: 8 (7%) versus 2 (1.4%) on the imlunestrant arm. Six patients with *ESR1m* on each arm received bevacizumab (anti-VEGF antibody) which is not widely used in the US. Trastuzumab deruxtecan was only given to 3 (2.2%) patients on the imlunestrant arm and none on the TPC arm. Very few patients (&lt;5%) on both arms received a subsequent *PIK3CA* or *AKT* inhibitor relative to the incidence of these alterations (Table 8.12) in the *ESR1m* population.

L981: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L982: ## Data:
L983: ## *Progression-free survival by investigator assessment (primary endpoint)*
L984: EMBER-3 met its primary endpoint in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L985: Imlunestrant demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in investigator-assessed PFS compared with SOC. Treatment with imlunestrant reduced the hazard of disease progression or death by 38% (stratified HR = 0.617 [95% CI: 0.464, 0.821]). The median PFS was 5.49 months versus 3.84 months (**imlunestrant** versus **SOC**), representing a 1.64-month longer median PFS in the **imlunestrant** arm. A sustained PFS benefit was suggested with separation of PFS curves beyond the median and enhanced benefit at 6- and 12-month landmark analyses favoring **imlunestrant** (Table 8.16).

L1017: ## Progression-free survival subgroup analyses
L1018: Investigator-assessed PFS analyses were performed across prespecified subgroups. These subgroups included
L1019: - age (&lt;65 and ≥65 years)
- race (Asian, Caucasian, Other)
- geographic region (Asia, Europe, North America, Other)
- investigator choice of SOC (fulvestrant or exemestane)
- presence of visceral metastasis (yes/no),
- bone-only metastasis, and
- prior CDK4/6i (yes/no and specific CDK4/6i).
L1020: Consistent treatment effect was observed across all prespecified subgroups in the ITT and ESR1m-detected population.
L1021: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1022: EMBER-3 met its primary endpoint in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L1023: Treatment with **imlunestrant** demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in investigator-assessed PFS in the *ESR1m*-detected population compared with **SOC**. Imlunestrant led to a 38% reduction in tumor progression compared with **SOC**. The benefit of imlunestrant was sustained throughout with an enhanced PFS advantage seen at 6 and 12 months. These results were also supported by BIRC-assessed PFS, a predefined secondary endpoint, which also showed that there was no evidence of investigator bias favoring imlunestrant in this open-label study.
L1024: The observed PFS benefit of imlunestrant was consistent across subgroups with no significant difference observed for age, sex, race/ethnicity, region, visceral metastases, liver metastases, bone-only metastases, prior CDK4/6i treatment, or whether it was administered as first-line or second-line treatment for ABC.
L1025: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1026: This application is based on the results of final PFS analysis and the first interim OS analysis for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population with DCO date of June 24, 2024 in EMBER-3. All p-values and boundaries presented in Section 8.1.2 are 2-sided. Of note, the significance levels presented in Section 8.1.1 as well as the initial alpha allocated to each hypothesis testing shown in **Figure 8.2** are 1-sided.
L1027: The final PFS analysis for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the ITT population was planned to be conducted when 480 PFS events were observed from the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the ITT population. At the DCO date of June 24, 2024, 490 PFS events were observed from the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the ITT population. At this DCO date, the number of PFS events observed for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population was 211, which was greater than the pre-specified 192 PFS events for the final PFS analysis in this population, therefore, no interim PFS analysis for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population was conducted and the PFS analysis conducted with DCO date of June 24, 2024 was the final PFS analysis for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L1028: Landmark analyses for time-to-event endpoints are considered exploratory since specific time points are used as opposed to assessing the entire survival distribution. Therefore, any differences between treatment arms at these landmarks must be interpreted with caution.
L1029: As *ESR1m* status was not a stratification factor at randomization, this may increase the risk of imbalance in baseline characteristics. To address this concern, the sponsor performed the prespecified sensitivity analysis using a multivariate Cox regression model adjusted for baseline
L1030: covariates, and the results appeared to show treatment benefit consistent with the primary analysis results (Table 8.18).
L1031: The primary endpoint of investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population crossed the initially allocated alpha of 0.04 (2-sided) and was statistically significant. The graphical representation of testing schema was updated with alpha levels recalculated, and the investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm vs TPC arm in the ITT population was formally tested at 0.038 (2-sided) alpha level. The HR for the investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm vs TPC arm in the ITT population was 0.87 (95% CI: 0.72, 1.04), the observed median PFS was 5.6 (95% CI: 5.3, 7.3) months in the imlunestrant arm and 5.5 (95% CI: 4.6, 5.6) months in the TPC arm. The 2-sided stratified log-rank test p-value was 0.1158, which didn't cross the significance alpha level of 0.038 (2-sided).
L1032: ## - Lack of PFS benefit of imlunestrant over TPC in the *ESR1m*-nd population
L1033: The Applicant conducted an exploratory analysis of investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in patients without *ESR1* mutation (n=405, 61.3% of the ITT population). The HR was 1.00 (95% CI: 0.79, 1.27). The observed median PFS was 5.6 (95% CI: 4.4, 9.1) months in the imlunestrant arm and 5.7 (95% CI: 5.5, 7.4) months in the TPC arm. The Applicant's analysis in patients without *ESR1* mutation included 20 patients whose *ESR1* mutation status was failed or missing (13 from the imlunestrant arm and 7 from the TPC arm). The FDA review team conducted an exploratory analysis of investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-not detected (*ESR1m*-nd) population by excluding these 20 patients whose *ESR1* mutation status was failed or missing (n=385). The HR was 1.04 (95% CI: 0.82, 1.33). The observed median PFS was 5.6 (95% CI: 3.8, 7.3) months in the imlunestrant arm and 5.6 (95% CI: 5.5, 7.4) months in the TPC arm (Table 8.19). No treatment benefit for PFS was observed in the *ESR1m*-nd population.

L1040: EMBER-3 met its primary endpoint of investigator-assessed PFS in the *ESR1m*-detected population but didn't meet its primary endpoint of investigator-assessed PFS in the ITT population. In the *ESR1m*-nd population, no treatment benefit for PFS was observed with treatment of imlunestrant compared with TPC (Figure 8.5 and Figure 8.6).
L1041: - *Non-proportional hazards*
L1042: In the *ESR1m*-detected population, the PFS Kaplan-Meier curves overlapped throughout the first 4 months and started to separate thereafter (Figure 8.4), which indicated that the assumption of exponential distribution was not met. Late separation in the KM curves persisted and led to an observed benefit in the PFS hazard ratio. The Applicant conducted an exploratory analysis of PFS using the restricted mean survival time (RSMT) to further quantify the treatment benefit for PFS (Table 8.13). The estimated RSMT at 19.4 months was 7.9 months (95% CI: 6.8, 9.1) in the imlunestrant arm vs 5.4 months (95% CI: 4.6, 6.2) in the TPC arm [difference 2.6 months (1.2.-3.9)]. It is noted that RMST results could change if a different cutoff timepoint was chosen; therefore, the RMST analysis is considered to be supportive only.
L1043: ## • *Discordance between PFS per investigator assessment and PFS per BIRC assessment*
L1044: The review team assessed the discordance rate of PFS assessments between investigator and BIRC (Table 8.20). In the ITT population, more patients in the TPC arm were censored by BIRC but considered as having a PFS event by investigator than patients in the imlunestrant arm (31.5% in the TPC arm vs 26.9% in the imlunestrant arm).
L1045: **Table 8.20: Discordance Analysis for PFS per Investigator Assessment vs PFS per BIRC Assessment**

L1047: (Source: FDA's analysis)
L1048: To evaluate whether the observed discordance suggested an investigator bias favoring the imlunestrant arm in the investigator-assessed PFS analysis, the Applicant calculated the early discordance rate (EDR) and the late discordance rate (LDR) for each arm. EDR was the frequency of investigator-assessed progressions before central review as a proportion of all investigator progressions. LDR was the frequency of investigator-assessed progressions after central review as a proportion of all discrepancies. A negative differential discordance for the EDR and/or a positive differential discordance for the LDR between the experimental arm and the control arm beyond a threshold would suggest a possible bias in the investigator assessments
L1049: favoring the experimental arm. Threshold values ranging from 0.075 to 0.1 were recommended based on simulation studies in Amit et al. 2011.

Late discrepancy rate	0.351	0.339	0.012
L1052: (Source: Adapted from CSR Table JZLC.5.5 and Table JZLC.5.13)
L1053: In both the ITT population and the ESR1m-detected population, neither EDR rate nor LDR rate crossed the threshold value of 0.075, thus they do not suggest an investigator assessment bias in favor of imlunestrant arm in these populations (Table 8.21).
L1054: ## - *Impact of early censoring in PFS analysis*
L1055: For the comparison of imlunestrant vs TPC in the ESR1m population, more patients in the TPC arm were censored early in the PFS analysis compared to those in the imlunestrant arm: 12 patients (10.2%) in the TPC arm were censored within 4 months after randomization compared to 7 patients (5.1%) in the imlunestrant arm. Per FDA's request, the Applicant conducted a tipping point analysis for PFS (Figure 8.7). The results suggest that even if the early censored patients in the TPC arm were to have at least 75% lower risk of PFS compared to the other chemotherapy arm patients, the 96% CI of the estimated PFS HR still won't cross 1. Based on the tipping point analysis, early censoring in the TPC arm were unlikely to change the statistical significance conclusion for PFS in the ESR1m-detected population.
L1056: **Figure 8.7: Tipping-point Analysis for Early Censoring on Investigator-assessed PFS in the ESR1m-detected Population (Imlunestrant versus TPC)**
L1057: (Source: Applicant's response to FDA's Information Request)
L1058: ## • *Impact of palliative radiation therapy*
L1059: The palliative radiation therapy was permitted in EMBER-3. In a response to an FDA's information request, the Applicant stated that sixteen patients (9 from the imlunestrant arm and 7 from the TPC arm) received on-therapy palliative radiation in the *ESR1m*-detected population. Per FDA's information request, the Applicant conducted sensitivity analyses to evaluate the impact of receiving palliative radiation therapy. The sensitivity analyses results are summarized in Table 8.22. The sensitivity analyses showed consistent results with the primary PFS findings.
L1060: **Table 8.22: Sensitivity Analyses of Investigator-assessed PFS to Assess the Impact of Palliative Radiation Therapy in the *ESR1m*-detected Population (Imlunestrant versus TPC)**

L1063: ## - *ESR1 mutation status based on Guardant 360 ctDNA assay*
L1064: Per EMBER3 study schema in Protocol (d), *ESR1* mutation status was planned to be centrally determined in plasma by Guardant 360 ctDNA assay from a blood draw at baseline. Among 256 patients in the *ESR1m*-detected population for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and TPC arm, all patients were tested with *ESR1* mutation by Guardant 360 ctDNA assay except two patients in the imlunestrant arm who were tested with *ESR1* mutation by another device in China. After excluding these 2 patients from the *ESR1m*-detected population, the hazard ratio for the investigator-assessed PFS was 0.635 (95% CI: 0.478, 0.844). The results are consistent with the primary findings for the investigator-assessed PFS for the comparison of imlunestrant arm and TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L1065: ## - *Subgroup analyses of Investigator-assessed PFS*
L1066: The subgroup analyses of investigator-assessed PFS are considered exploratory, and no formal statistical inference may be drawn. The FDA agrees that generally no apparent outliers were observed in the subgroup analyses of investigator-assessed PFS, as shown in **Figure 8.8**.
L1067: **Figure 8.8: Investigator-assessed PFS Subgroup Analyses in the *ESR1m*-detected Population (Imlunestrant versus TPC)**
L1068: (Source: FDA analysis)
L1069: The FDA review team also conducted subgroup analyses of investigator-assessed PFS by ethnic subgroups. In the Hispanic/Latino subgroup (n=48), the HR was 0.42 (95% CI: 0.22, 0.83), in the Non-Hispanic/Latino subgroup (n=181), the HR was 0.61 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.85), in patients whose ethnicity was not reported or missing (n=27), the HR was 1.20 (95% CI: 0.51, 2.72). Although the subgroup of patients whose ethnicity was not reported or missing had a hazard ratio point estimate greater than 1, this subgroup has a very small sample size, ethnicity is not a stratification factor at randomization and the hazard ratio confidence interval included 1. Therefore, this subgroup result does not raise concerns about the overall efficacy findings and should be interpreted with caution.

L1103: ## Time-to-chemotherapy, progression-free survival-2, and chemotherapy-free survival
L1104: Time to chemotherapy, PFS2, and CFS showed a treatment effect that favored imlunestrant with HRs of 0.672 (95% CI: 0.472, 0.957), 0.600 (95% CI: 0.418, 0.861), and 0.651 (95% CI: 0.474, 0.894) respectively.
L1105: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1106: The totality of efficacy data from these secondary or exploratory endpoints demonstrates consistency with the benefit observed for the primary endpoint in the *ESR1m*-detected population.
L1107: Based on the Sponsor's assessment, no deaths were attributed to imlunestrant toxicity. The numerical data primarily reflect deaths secondary to disease progression.
L1108: Although OS data are immature, results demonstrate no trend toward detriment for patients treated with imlunestrant. ORR data favored imlunestrant and the low rate of response across
L1109: both arms was reflective of responses observed with ET monotherapy in patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or MBC previously treated with endocrine therapy.
L1110: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1111: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of OS Interim Analysis 1 (IA1) results for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm vs TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population. There were 80 deaths observed in the *ESR1m*-detected population (52% information fraction of the OS final analysis in the *ESR1m*-detected population). The updated alpha allocated for interim and final analyses of OS in the *ESR1m*-detected population is 5.5 \times 10^{-6} (2-sided). The p-value for the OS IA1 for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm vs the TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population did not cross the efficacy boundary of 2.2 \times 10^{-10} (2-sided). There was no potential detriment in overall survival in the *ESR1m*-detected population at IA1.
L1112: Landmark analyses for time-to-event endpoints are considered exploratory since specific time points are used as opposed to assessing the entire survival distribution. Therefore, any differences between treatment arms at these landmarks must be interpreted with caution.
L1113: The OS curves for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population overlapped for the first 3 months, favored to the TPC arm from about 3 to 7 months, and began to separate in favor of the imlunestrant arm from Month 7 onward. The Applicant provided a table showing the number of OS events by time interval, and it was noted that the number of events that occurred before Month 7 was small.

The median OS was not reached in either arm.

L1133: ## - *Impact of withdrawal of consent in the OS analysis*
L1134: In the OS analysis for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, more patients in the imlunestrant arm were censored due to withdrawal of consent or being lost to follow-up compared to those in the TPC arm (10.8% vs 5.9%). The FDA requested that the Applicant use public registries to retrieve survival data for patients who were censored for OS due to withdrawal of consent or being lost to follow-up and update the OS results. The Applicant stated that they were “working with a vendor to identify and retrieve the survival status of patients who were censored for OS while following local laws and regulations which vary by country.” The Applicant also stated that future OS analyses would include retrieved OS information. In a response to FDA’s follow-up Information Request regarding conducting an updated IA1 OS analysis by including retrieved OS information, the Applicant stated that as of March 2025, survival data for only 2 patients have been retrieved, thus an updated IA1 OS analysis would be premature.
L1135: Per the FDA’s request, the Applicant conducted a tipping point analysis to evaluate the impact of withdrawal of consent or being lost to follow-up in the OS analysis. In the tipping-point analysis, 15 patients who were censored for OS due to withdrawal of consent or lost to follow-up in the imlunestrant arm were assumed to have an increased hazard rate compared with the remaining patients in this arm. The results show that the point estimates of the HR were below 1 across all scenarios and the 95% CI of the HR (assumed nominal significance level) only included 1 when
L1136: patients who withdrew consent or were lost to follow-up in the imlunestrant arm were assumed to have an increase of hazard by 200% to 220% (Figure 8.13).
L1137: **Figure 8.13: Tipping-point Analysis for Withdrawal of Consent on OS in the *ESR1m* Population (Imlunestrant versus TPC)**
L1138: (Source: Applicant's response to FDA's Information Request)
L1139: The Applicant also conducted additional sensitivity analyses to evaluate the impact of the withdrawal of consent or being lost to follow-up in the OS analysis. The sensitivity analyses results are generally consistent with the primary interim OS analysis results (Table 8.27).

L1143: ## • *Descriptive analysis of OS in the ESR1m-detected population with additional follow-up*
L1144: The Applicant submitted SAP Addendum v1 in a meeting request dated November 14, 2024 (Sequence Number 0165). SAP Addendum v1 was amended after data unblinding. In SAP Addendum v1, the Applicant proposed to remove the anchoring of the OS testing on the OS endpoint for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm versus the TPC arm in the ITT population, and to update the event-driven OS interims to time-driven interims for the following OS endpoints: OS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population, and OS for the comparison of the imlun+abema arm and the imlunestrant arm in the ITT population. The FDA advised the Applicant not to change the remaining formal OS tests between the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population as they were made after data unblinding and could be data driven. Additionally, the FDA stated that changing from event-driven to calendar-based analyses would likely lead to inadequately powered analyses. The FDA warned the Applicant that analyses conducted under SAP Amendment v1 would be considered post-hoc and descriptive only.
L1145: Despite FDA's warning, the Applicant proceeded with SAP Addendum v1 and conducted updated OS analyses with a DCO date of January 30, 2025. Topline results of updated OS analyses based on the DCO date of January 30, 2025 were provided and discussed
L1146: (b) (4)
L1147: The FDA considered these OS analyses post-hoc and descriptive only. The descriptive analyses of OS with a DCO date of January 30, 2025 showed that the hazard ratio was 0.60 (95% CI: 0.41, 0.88) for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1m*-detected population. The observed median OS was 34.4 (95% CI: 25.0, NE) months in the imlunestrant arm and 22.7 (95% CI: 18.4, 28.9) months in the TPC arm. The descriptive OS analysis results are consistent with the OS results conducted at IA1 with a DCO date of June 24, 2024.
L1148: ## • *Secondary endpoints*
L1149: For the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm, the multiplicity plan accounted for PFS in the *ESR1m*-detected population, PFS in the ITT population, OS in the *ESR1m*-detected population, and OS in the ITT population. There was no multiplicity plan for other secondary endpoints for this comparison. The p-value for the ORR analysis in the *ESR1m*-detected population shown in Table 8.21 is considered nominal p-value only. The FDA noted that the ORRs in both the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm were generally low in the ITT population, the *ESR1m*-detected population, and the *ESR1m*-nd population. In the *ESR1m*-detected population, patients with measurable disease at baseline had an ORR of 14.3% (95% CI: 7.8, 20.8) in the imlunestrant arm and 7.7% (95% CI: 2.2, 13.2) in the TPC arm. In the ITT population, patients with measurable disease at baseline had an ORR of 12.2% (95% CI: 8.2, 16.2) in the imlunestrant arm and 8.4% (95% CI: 4.9, 11.8) in the TPC arm. In the *ESR1m*-nd population, patients with measurable disease at baseline had an ORR of 10.6% (95% CI: 5.5,
L1150: 15.6) in the imlunestrant arm and 8.3% (95% CI: 4.0, 12.7) in the TPC arm. CBR is not an endpoint used by the FDA for regulatory decision making, because it includes stable disease, which may not represent meaningful clinical benefit from the drug, making it less stringent than ORR which only counts actual tumor shrinkage.
L1151: Secondary endpoints of time-to chemotherapy, PFS2, and chemotherapy-free survival were not endpoints that the FDA considers in regulatory decision making. These endpoints lack standardized definitions across trials and are confounded by physician and patient treatment choices unrelated to drug efficacy and serve as indirect measures rather than direct assessments of how the drug affects cancer progression or patient survival.

L1167: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or Exploratory COA (PRO) Endpoints
L1168: ## Data:
L1169: Compliance for PRO measures at baseline ranged from 87.2% to 88.5% for the **imlunestrant** arm and 87.7% for the **SOC** arm.
L1170: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1171: The majority of patients receiving fulvestrant IM experience reactions, including pain. Imlunestrant eliminates this by virtue of oral administration. Patient-reported injection site pain, swelling, or redness was assessed among the **ESR1m**-detected patients on the **SOC** arm that received fulvestrant IM using a single PRO-CTCAE item (yes/no). Of the patients on the **SOC** arm who received fulvestrant IM, 72.3% reported injection site pain, swelling, or redness at any time while on treatment, with roughly 30% reporting these symptoms during the first week of each cycle.
L1172: Imlunestrant did not demonstrate an advantage with regard to the worsening of pain. Among the **ESR1m**-detected populations, the secondary PRO endpoint was time to sustained worsening of “worst pain” measured by the Worst Pain numeric rating scale from randomization until the date of the first increase (\ge 2 points) in the weekly average of the worst pain score with confirmation in the next consecutive week. At data cutoff, a total of 41 patients reported a sustained worsening of worst pain score, including 23 patients (17.4%) in the **imlunestrant** arm and 18 patients (16.1%) in the **SOC** arm. Overall, there were few events, high rates of censoring, and no difference between arms (stratified HR = 0.980 [95% CI: 0.527, 1.822], stratified log-rank test p = 0.9531).
L1173: Patients who received imlunestrant maintained or improved both their physical function and overall QOL, while those on SOC experienced a worsening in physical function and overall QOL. The Physical Functioning and GHS/QOL domain scores of the EORTC-QLQ-C30 were evaluated by a longitudinal MMRM using the change from baseline. The change from baseline for all visits while on treatment was included in the model. The analysis included all cycles through which at least 25% of patients in each arm had an assessment. Within the **ESR1m**-detected population, the mean physical function scores from baseline through cycle 7 were maintained or improved for patients in the **imlunestrant** arm, while the mean physical function scores worsened from baseline for the **SOC** arm. The least-square mean change difference between arms was positive in value at all time points ranging from 2.54 to 6.17, favoring the **imlunestrant** arm.
L1174: Similarly, within the **ESR1m**-detected population, the mean GHS/QOL scores from baseline through cycle 7 were maintained or improved for patients in the **imlunestrant** arm, while the mean GHS/QOL scores worsened from baseline for the **SOC** arm. The least-square mean change difference between arms was &gt;9.5 points at cycles 5 and 7, favoring the **imlunestrant** arm.
L1175: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1176: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's interpretation of patient-reported outcome results. After cycle 3 day 1 many patients were no longer on therapy because of disease progression, treatment discontinuation, and death and were therefore not expected to complete PROs, making an on-treatment assessment of the patient experience challenging. However, of those patients who were expected to complete an assessment, completion rates remained high (&gt;80%) for those still on trial therapy through cycle 11 day 1.
L1177: While route of administration does inform the specific associated adverse events, focusing on one item such as injection site pain, swelling, or redness is not indicative of the totality of patient experience with a specific therapy. FDA does note that the Applicant collected weekly PRO-CTCAE diarrhea (frequency) using PRO-CTCAE, and frequency of diarrhea was similar between Arm A and B at most timepoints.
L1178: Time to event patient-reported outcome results are also challenging to interpret, especially when intercurrent events which may preclude capture of PROs and high censoring. Therefore, FDA cannot draw conclusions from the PRO results. FDA does not agree that there is no observed difference between arms, nor can FDA conclude that there was relative improvement or maintenance of QOL given the limitations noted regarding high rate of attrition and censoring.
L1179: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1180: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1181: Not applicable.
L1182: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1183: Not applicable.
L1184: ## **8.1.3 Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1185: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1186: The FDA's conclusions regarding the efficacy of imlunestrant for the treatment of patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative, *ESR1*-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer were based on the results from EMBER-3, a randomized, active-controlled, open-label, multicenter trial comparing imlunestrant versus treatment of physician's choice (TPC: fulvestrant or exemestane). The primary endpoints were investigator assessed PFS in the *ESR1*m population and the ITT
L1187: population (*ESR1m* and *ESR1m-not detected*). The secondary endpoints were OS in the *ESR1m* and ITT populations.
L1188: The PFS endpoint was met in the *ESR1m* population (median PFS 7.4 months versus 5.5 months, HR 0.62, 95% CI: 0.46-0.82; p-value 0.0008). (b) (4) was removed (b) (4) before the trial started enrolling. While exploratory analyses by individual *ESR1* mutations may suggest differential efficacy for imlunestrant, the evidence does not support a detriment for patients with specific *ESR1* mutations and therefore the review team recommends granting approval consistent with the proposed CDx. The PFS endpoint in the ITT population did not cross the prespecified significance alpha level.
L1189: EMBER-3 was an active-controlled trial with a replacement design. For such trials even a modest improvement in an efficacy endpoint may support a favorable regulatory action provided the control arm is active, and the investigational treatment is not more toxic than the control. At the time that the study was designed single agent endocrine therapy was an acceptable option for second line therapy. Subsequently, it has been observed that fulvestrant monotherapy underperforms after progression on a CDK4/6i therapy.
L1190: The secondary OS endpoint in the *ESR1m* population did not reach its prespecified significance alpha level. However, there was a trend favoring the imlunestrant arm in the *ESR1m* population (HR 0.91, 95% CI: 0.56-1.47). OS could not be tested in the ITT population since PFS failed to meet its prespecified threshold. The exploratory endpoint of objective response rate also favored imlunestrant (14% versus 8%). A postmarketing commitment (PMC) has been issued by the FDA and agreed to by the Applicant to obtain the next prespecified interim and final OS results for the *ESR1m* population
L1191: Finally, the approval of elacestrant for patients with an *ESR1m* and emerging data from other oral SERDs, summarized in Section 2.2, suggest that the benefit of these therapies is greater for patients with an *ESR1m*.
L1192: The FDA concluded that clinical benefit was demonstrated for imlunestrant in the *ESR1m* population. Although the median PFS improvement was modest it should be interpreted in the context of a replacement design. The PFS results were robust across multiple sensitivity analyses and supported by no potential trend toward OS detriment. The FDA revised the indicated population to patients with evidence of disease progression after endocrine therapy based on the population included in the trial.
L1193: While male patients were eligible to enroll on EMBER-3, there were no male patients in the *ESR1m* population. The FDA included male patients in the indication based on extrapolation from the data in female patients and the biologic rationale that there were no expected efficacy or
L1194: safety differences between male and female patients with an *ESR1m* (FDA Guidance: Male Breast Cancer, 2020).

L1221: ## 8.2 Review of Safety
L1222: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1223: The safety population of EMBER-3 included all participants randomly assigned to study treatment who took at least 1 dose of study treatment (N = 859) with 327 patients assigned to Arm A and 324 patients assigned to Arm B. The Safety Population for Arms A and B is henceforth called the Overall Safety Population. The *ESR1m*-detected safety population in Arm A and Arm B (N=254) was a subset of the overall safety population.
L1224: The safety profile of **imlunestrant** monotherapy was generally favorable and similar to **SOC**. The imlunestrant safety profile was manageable through routine monitoring as evidenced by low rates of dose reduction or treatment discontinuation due to AEs.
L1225: The safety profile of the *ESR1m*-detected safety population was similar to that of the overall safety population.
L1226: The safety profile of **imlunestrant** monotherapy was consistent with the mechanism of action of SERDs, and events commonly experienced by patients with ABC.
L1227: - The incidence of most TEAEs was similar between treatment arms and the majority were low grade (Grade 1 or 2).
- The most frequently reported TEAEs in the **imlunestrant** arm were fatigue, diarrhea, and nausea, which were easily detectable based on clinical signs and symptoms. The majority of these events were observed early in treatment. A higher incidence of low grade diarrhea and fatigue in the **imlunestrant** arm was not considered clinically relevant.
L1228: - The incidence of SAEs and deaths due to AEs were similar across treatment arms. The incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs showed a slightly lower incidence in **imlunestrant** arm (17.1%) compared with **SOC** (20.7%).
- Overall, TEAEs were manageable with supportive therapies and/or dose adjustments (withheld or reductions).
- The frequency of discontinuations or dose adjustments due to AEs was low across both arms.
L1229: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1230: The FDA safety analyses were based on the FDA's independent review of data from the EMBER-3 (J2J-OX-JZLC) pivotal trial. The FDA independent analyses were based on 651 patients who received at least one dose of study treatment in Arm A (imlunestrant) or Arm B (treatment of physician's choice, TPC) of the trial, including 327 patients who received imlunestrant and 324 patients who received TPC (fulvestrant-292 patients, or exemestane-32 patients). The FDA assessed safety in all patients in Arm A and Arm B, as well as in patients with *ESR1*m-detected status in these two arms. *ESR1*m-detected status for the FDA review of safety was defined as in the review of efficacy (section 8.1.1). The FDA agrees that the safety profile of the *ESR1*m-detected safety population was similar to the safety profile of the overall safety population.
L1231: Most patients in EMBER-3 experienced at least one treatment emergent adverse event (TEAE), and all grade TEAEs occurred at a similar frequency in the imlunestrant arm (83%) and the TPC arm (84%). The most common (\ge 10\%) TEAEs, including lab abnormalities, in patients who received imlunestrant included hemoglobin decreased, musculoskeletal pain, calcium decreased, neutrophils decreased, fatigue, diarrhea, nausea, increased ALT, increased AST, triglycerides increased, platelets decreased, cholesterol increased, abdominal pain, and constipation. Grade \ge 3 TEAEs were slightly higher in the TPC arm (19%) compared to the imlunestrant arm (15%). The most common (\ge 1\%) clinically relevant grade 3 TEAEs in patients who received imlunestrant were musculoskeletal pain, anemia, and neutropenia. The FDA agrees with the Applicant that most TEAEs in both the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm were Grade 1-2. Serious adverse events (SAEs) were similar between patients who received imlunestrant (10%) and patients who received TPC (11%). The most common SAE (\ge 1\%) was pleural effusion and occurred only in patients who received imlunestrant (1.2%). The most common SAE (\ge 1\%) in the TPC group (1.5%) was fracture.
L1232: The incidence of TEAEs leading to dosage interruption was higher in patients who received imlunestrant (10%) compared to patients who received TPC (7%), and the most common (\ge 1\%) TEAE leading to dosage interruption in patients receiving imlunestrant was vomiting (1.5%).
L1233: The incidence of TEAEs leading to drug discontinuation was higher in patients receiving imlunestrant (4.3%) compared to those receiving TPC (1.4%). Dose reductions were low (2.4%) in patients receiving imlunestrant and did not occur in patients receiving TPC.
L1234: Important safety signals for imlunestrant included GI TEAEs (diarrhea, constipation, nausea, and vomiting). Diarrhea, constipation, nausea, and vomiting all occurred in a higher proportion of patients who received imlunestrant compared to TPC: 22% vs. 12%, 10% vs. 6%, 17% vs. 13%, and 9% vs. 4.9%, respectively. The frequency of fatigue was also higher in patients who received imlunestrant (23%) vs. TPC (13%). Dyslipidemia, including hypertriglyceridemia and hypercholesterolemia, was seen in patients in EMBER-3. The incidence of hypertriglyceridemia and hypercholesterolemia were similar across patients who received imlunestrant (21% and 10%, respectively) and patients who received TPC (22% and 12%, respectively). Musculoskeletal pain occurred at similar frequency in patients who received imlunestrant (30%) vs. TPC (30%). The only TEAE with notable higher frequency in patients receiving TPC was injection site reactions, which occurred in 6% of patients who received fulvestrant. For further details, refer to the FDA's assessment in Section 8.2.4.
L1235: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's statement that imlunestrant monotherapy was generally favorable and similar to TPC. As noted, GI TEAEs occurred in a greater proportion of patients who received imlunestrant compared to TPC. Although most TEAEs were Grade 1-2, the FDA notes that even certain Grade 1-2 TEAEs, e.g., nausea or diarrhea, can adversely impact a patient's quality of life. Fatigue was also experienced by a higher proportion of patients who received imlunestrant compared to those who received TPC.
L1236: The safety profile of imlunestrant is manageable with labeling for the indicated patient population with *ESR1*m-detected status.
L1237: ## 8.2.1 Safety Review Approach
L1238: ## Data:
L1239: The safety assessment of imlunestrant for the treatment of patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or MBC previously treated with ET was primarily derived from the Phase 3 registrational study, EMBER-3. Two Phase 1 studies, EMBER and EMBER-2, were also evaluated for safety and provided supporting data including the 400 mg QD dosage.
L1240: Across studies, 408 patients with ER+ HER2- ABC received at least 1 dose of 400 mg imlunestrant QD dose. Patients treated with 400 mg imlunestrant monotherapy included
L1241: - 327 with ABC in EMBER-3
- 51 with ABC in EMBER, and
- 30 with EBC in EMBER-2.
L1242: The overall safety population for the comparison of **imlunestrant** treatment arm (Arm A) versus the **SOC** treatment arm (Arm B) in EMBER-3 includes 651 patients who initiated treatment:
L1243: - 327 patients with **imlunestrant**, and
- 324 patients with investigator's choice of **SOC ET fulvestrant** (n = 292) or **exemestane** (n = 32).
L1244: The **ESR1m**-detected safety population for the comparison of imlunestrant treatment arm (Arm A) versus the **SOC** treatment arm (Arm B) in EMBER-3 includes 254 patients who initiated treatment:
L1245: - 137 patients with imlunestrant, and
- 117 patients with investigator's choice of SOC ET fulvestrant (n=111) or exemestane (n=6)
L1246: The safety population included all participants randomly assigned to study treatment and who took at least 1 dose of study treatment.
L1247: Supportive analysis sets provide a side-by-side presentation of data from the respective cohorts from EMBER-3 and EMBER who received imlunestrant 400 mg QD. This presentation format facilitates an evaluation of the safety profile across studies.
L1248: The pooled analysis sets of EMBER-3 and EMBER increase the size of the datasets for patients receiving imlunestrant 400 mg QD and increases the robustness of the safety assessment. EMBER-2 data supplement the understanding of the safety profile in ET-naive patients.
L1249: Results of the supportive and the pooled analyses are not presented or discussed within the text unless the results differ notably from the results of the primary analysis sets, or if they offer additional insights in characterizing specific safety parameters. The results of the supportive and pooled analyses for EMBER-3 and EMBER are available in Module 2.7.4.7 Summary of Clinical Safety Appendix. These analyses are based on the safety population, as a review of **ESR1m** status data did not indicate a clinically meaningful difference between the safety profile between **ESR1m** status subgroups.
L1250: Safety endpoints included AEs, SAEs, deaths, and clinical laboratory abnormalities per NCI CTCAE v5.0, vital signs, ECGs, and ECOG PS.
L1251: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1252: The clinical development of imlunestrant evaluated a series of important scientific and clinical questions with the goal of understanding the benefits and risks of imlunestrant therapy in the intended patient population, as assessed relative to fulvestrant and exemestane.
L1253: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1254: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of the safety assessment in EMBER-3. The FDA's primary analysis of safety focused on the 651 patients who received at least one dose of treatment in the EMBER-3 trial, including 327 who received imlunestrant and 324 who received treatment of physician's choice (TPC) – fulvestrant or exemestane. There were 254 patients with *ESR1*m-detected status in the EMBER-3 safety population, 137 who received imlunestrant and 117 who received TPC. There were 397 patients with *ESR1*m-not detected (no mutation detected, no ctDNA detected, or test result missing) in the EMBER-3 safety population, 190 who received imlunestrant and 207 who received TPC.
L1255: The FDA's safety review of the EMBER-3 trial is based on data submitted with an initial cutoff date of June 24, 2024. The 120-day safety update did not reveal any new safety signals.
L1256: The FDA did not independently review the safety data from the pooled EMBER-3 and EMBER studies and did not pool data from EMBER or EMBER-2 with data from EMBER-3 as the EMBER and EMBER-2 trials included a different patient population from the intended indication. EMBER included patients with more heterogeneous prior therapies and EMBER-2 was a perioperative study that included patients who were endocrine therapy naïve. However, the FDA agrees in general that the safety data from patients in the EMBER trial with ER+/HER2-advanced breast cancer were considered supportive.
L1257: The FDA's safety analysis for EMBER-3 focused on deaths and TEAEs including SAEs, TEAEs leading to trial drug discontinuation, dose reduction, or dose interruption, grade 3-4 TEAEs, and all-grade TEAEs. The FDA also reviewed laboratory data. The Applicant identified transaminase increases as an AE of special interest. FDA conducted additional assessments of dyslipidemia, which the FDA identified as an AE of special interest. Dyslipidemia, including both hypercholesterolemia and hypertriglyceridemia, did not occur at greater frequency in patients who received imlunestrant vs those who received TPC. There was no grade 3-4 TEAEs for hyperlipidemia or hypercholesterolemia, no deaths or SAEs due to dyslipidemia, and the use of concomitant medications for the management of dyslipidemia did not differ significantly across the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm. For these reasons dyslipidemia was not included in Section 5 of the USPI (Warnings and Precautions). However, FDA added information regarding monitoring for dyslipidemia in the Patient Counseling Section of the USPI as it was noted in a clinically significant percentage of patients and has also been noted for other approved SERDs. Additionally, EMBER-3 had an active control TPC arm (fulvestrant or exemestane), and although the incidences of dyslipidemia were similar in both arms, FDA identified dyslipidemia as an important AE that should be conveyed to providers and patients. Additional details
L1258: regarding AEs of special interest are included in the Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues.
L1259: For assessment of TEAEs, the FDA grouped related preferred terms as follows:
L1260: - Abdominal pain: Abdominal pain, Abdominal pain lower, Abdominal pain upper, Abdominal tenderness
- Diarrhea: Colitis, Diarrhea, and Enterocolitis
- Musculoskeletal pain: Arthralgia, Arthritis, Back pain, Bone pain, Musculoskeletal chest pain, Musculoskeletal pain, Myalgia, Neck pain, Pain in extremity, Spinal pain, and Cancer pain
- Stomatitis: Mouth ulceration, Mucosal inflammation, and Stomatitis
- Vomiting: Hematemesis, and Vomiting
- Fatigue: Fatigue, and Asthenia

L1276: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L1277: ## Data:
L1278: Please see **Table 8.11** for the relevant patient demographics and **Table 8.12** for baseline disease characteristics. Demographic and baseline characteristics of patients were adequately reflective of ER+, HER2- ABC, and were generally balanced across treatment arms. Baseline characteristics for age, sex, ECOG, region, menopausal status, presence of visceral metastases, bone-only disease, endocrine resistance, and prior CDK4/6i use were balanced across treatment arms for ITT and *ESR1*-m-detected populations.
L1279: A minimal difference in the demographics and disease characteristics of patients in the ITT population compared to the overall safety population was observed.
L1280: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1281: EMBER-3 enrolled a population that was reflective of these patients, allowing for appropriate characterization of the safety profile.
L1282: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1283: Table 8.10 and Table 8.11 referenced by the Applicant present demographics, baseline characteristics, and disease characteristics of the intention-to-treat (ITT) population not the safety population. The ITT population (N=661) included 331 patients in the imlunestrant arm and 330 patients in the treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm. The safety population (N=651) included 327 patients in the imlunestrant arm and 324 patients in the TPC arm. The ITT population included 138 patients with *ESR1*-m-detected status in the imlunestrant arm and 118 in the TPC arm. The safety population included 137 patients with *ESR1*-m-detected status in the imlunestrant arm and 117 in the TPC arm. The FDA assessed the demographics of the overall safety population and patients with *ESR1*-m-detected status within that population. In general, FDA agrees that baseline characteristics and disease characteristics were balanced in the safety population.
L1284: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant that the enrolled population allowed for characterization of the safety profile of imlunestrant, was generally reflective of ER+/HER2- metastatic breast cancer and was generally balanced across the two arms. However, FDA notes that there were no male patients with ESR1/m-detected status in the safety population and few Black or African-American patients were enrolled in the safety population relative to the prevalence of ER+/HER2- advance breast cancer in the US. No patients with ECOG \ge 2 were enrolled in EMBER-3.
L1285: ## Adequacy of the safety database
L1286: ## Data:

L1288: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1289: The size of the safety database for imlunestrant was sufficient to adequately characterize the safety profile. EMBER-3 was well designed with appropriate endpoints and a robust safety review process. The safety profile of imlunestrant was studied in a representative population for which the sponsor is seeking an indication. Safety findings are noted in Section 8.2.4. The Sponsor considers these safety risks to be manageable through labeling and standard clinical practice.
L1290: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1291: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the size of the safety database was adequate for assessment of the safety of imlunestrant. The FDA's independent analysis of safety relied on data from the EMBER-3 pivotal trial, as previously stated. FDA generally agrees that the safety population in the trial is representative of the population for which the Applicant is seeking an indication, with any caveats noted throughout this safety review, where applicable. In the FDA's review of safety, important safety signals for imlunestrant included diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, musculoskeletal pain, and fatigue. The FDA considers these safety risks to be manageable through labeling.

L1300: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1301: ## Data:
L1302: ## Consolidated terms
L1303: Preferred terms identified by medical evaluation as clinically identical or synonymous were grouped together under a single consolidated term to minimize the excess granularity of MedDRA preferred terms and allow meaningful interpretation of data. Consolidated terms are italicized. CTCAE v5.0 was used to assess safety and tolerability.
L1304: ## Evaluation of AESI
L1305: The safety evaluation plan also included an analysis of AESIs (Section 8.2.5.1). All analyses evaluated for AESIs were for treatment-emergent events.
L1306: AESIs were determined to be events that were
L1307: - of clinical significance
- potentially associated with other SERD agents, or
- observed in preclinical evaluation or earlier clinical studies of imlunestrant, although in some instances a causal association with imlunestrant could not be clearly established.
L1308: The only AESI for imlunestrant is transaminase increased.
L1309: The Sponsor reviewed deaths due to AEs, SAEs, and TEAEs to identify any significant safety concerns.
L1310: TEAEs were also presented by subgroups based on the values collected at baseline (that is, the last nonmissing measurement collected on or before the study drug's first dose date/time).
L1311: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1312: Routine clinical tests were conducted as directed by the Schedule of Activities in the EMBER-3 protocol. A central laboratory analyzed clinical chemistry, fasting lipid panel, PK, and biomarker samples. Local laboratories were used to analyze hematology, coagulation, and hormone levels. The study monitors evaluated the local laboratories' use of internal and external quality control, quality assurance, and accreditation schemes.
L1313: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1314: EMBER-3 was designed with routine scheduled visits. Tests were appropriate for monitoring safety at the designated intervals. The testing standards were suitable for the indicated population, and the assessments performed were appropriate.
L1315: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1316: In general FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment for categorization of adverse events.
L1317: FDA categorization of adverse events used preferred terms (PTs) to group terms and further consolidate synonymous adverse events, as outlined in section 8.2.1. The FDA PTs for abdominal pain, vomiting, diarrhea, and musculoskeletal pain were slightly different than the ones used by the Applicant as previously noted in this review. The FDA PT for abdominal pain included hepatic pain, the PT for vomiting included hematemesis, the PT for diarrhea included enterocolitis and colitis, and the PT for musculoskeletal pain included cancer pain and back pain. These differences (b) (4) of the adverse events reflecting these PT which the Applicant agreed to. The following safety sections in this Assessment Aid detail FDA safety findings for deaths, TEAEs, SAEs, Grade 3-4 AEs, and adverse events of special interest.
L1318: ## 8.2.4 Safety Results
L1319: ## Deaths
L1320: ## **Data:**
L1321: Overall, the majority of patient deaths on study and during long-term follow-up were due to disease progression in both the overall and the *ESRm*-detected safety population. The frequencies of deaths due to any cause on treatment or up to 30 days after discontinuation from study treatment were low across all arms (Table 8.29).
L1322: At the time of data cutoff, there were 62 deaths in the **imlunestrant** arm and 90 deaths in the **SOC** arm. The frequency of death due to AEs on treatment or up to 30 days after discontinuation from study treatment was low in both the overall safety population [**imlunestrant** arm (3.7%), **SOC** arm (4.9%)] and *ESR1*-m-detected safety population [**imlunestrant** arm (4.4%), **SOC** arm (6.0%)]. All patients who died either while receiving imlunestrant or during short-term follow-up have CSR narrative summaries submitted for review.
L1323: In EMBER, no deaths due to TEAEs were reported in patients with ABC treated with imlunestrant 400 mg.
L1324: No clear patterns were identified that would indicate a common pathophysiology associated with imlunestrant.
