L1331: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1332: The majority of deaths were due to study disease and were higher in the SOC arm. The frequency of deaths due to TEAEs was similar across treatment arms in both the overall and ESR1m-detected safety populations with the majority of patients in the **imlunestrant** arm having significant relevant medical history or risk factors leading to the fatal events.
L1333: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1334: The FDA's independent analysis of deaths in the EMBER-3 trial is generally aligned with the Applicant's position. However, the FDA's review of deaths for patients on treatment or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation only identified 8 (2.5%), not 16 (4.9%), deaths in the treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm in the ITT population, and 4 (3.4%), not 7 (6.0%), deaths in the TPC arm in the ESR1m-detected population. The difference in the number of deaths resulted from a difference in the Applicant's definition of "on therapy or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation" compared to the standard FDA definition. The Applicant's definition was based on a **study discontinuation date** which varied from the time of the last day treatment was received compared to the FDA definition which is based solely on the last day treatment was received. The Applicant reported using the same definition for deaths "on therapy or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation" for both the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm.
L1335: FDA agrees that most deaths in both treatment arms were due to disease progression. The difference in definition of deaths "on therapy or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation"
L1336: also led to a difference between the Applicant's and the FDA's assessment of the number of deaths due to TEAEs in the TPC arm as noted below. For the TPC arm, per the Applicant's definition, there were 6 deaths due to AEs within 30 days vs. using the FDA definition, there were 3 deaths within 30 days of the last treatment dose on the TPC arm. For the imlunestrant arm, there were 5 deaths by both the Applicant and FDA definition.
L1337: Below is a summary of the narratives for the 5 deaths associated with TEAEs reported in patients who received imlunestrant in EMBER-3, that occurred on therapy or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation. Of note, a sixth death occurred in a patient who received imlunestrant within 30 days of treatment discontinuation. However, this death was due to septic shock due to confirmed dengue fever, and based on the review of the narrative, it is less likely that imlunestrant contributed to this death.
L1338: ## 1. Patient (b)(6):
L1339: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-detected
L1340: This 39-year-old Asian female in India with baseline anemia and thrombocytopenia, and metastases in the liver and bones, died on day 107 from right ventricular failure, two weeks after the last dose of imlunestrant. The patient's baseline anemia and thrombocytopenia worsened while on treatment with imlunestrant and on day 29 imlunestrant was held for 5 days. The patient was also treated with romiplostim. Imlunestrant was re-started at a lower dose on day 35. However, on day 92 the patient again experienced worsened anemia and thrombocytopenia and imlunestrant was held and not re-started. Five days later the patient went to the hospital with fatigue, breathlessness, cough, and symptoms of ongoing cardiac failure. She was admitted for severe thrombocytopenia and received a platelet transfusion. The severe thrombocytopenia was not considered related to imlunestrant by the investigator. An echocardiogram suggested moderate pulmonary hypertension considered to be secondary to right cardiac failure. FDA requested results of the echocardiogram and cardiac evaluation; however, the Applicant stated that echocardiogram results were not provided and a cardiology evaluation was not done. A bone marrow biopsy was done and confirmed progression of disease in the bone marrow. The patient was discharged 9 days after this admission, and on that same day she experienced breathlessness and fatigue. She ultimately died the following day. The cytopenias noted were likely secondary to bone marrow involvement of the tumor and this may have contributed to the ultimate cardiac failure. Chronic anemia may have led to the development of pulmonary HTN. The right ventricular failure was assessed as related to disease progression and there is insufficient evidence to suggest a causal association between the patient's right ventricular failure and imlunestrant. FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1341: ## 2. Patient (b)(6):
L1342: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-detected
L1343: This 64-year-old white female patient in Mexico with baseline anemia and elevated uric acid, and metastatic disease to the bones and thyroid gland, died of upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage on day 142, one day after the last dose of imlunestrant. She had no dose omissions or dose reductions reported. The patient had sudden, massive hematemesis on day 142 and died on the way to the hospital. She continued to have anemia and increased blood uric acid throughout the study and was started on allopurinol on day 65. Thirteen days prior to starting imlunestrant the patient's PTT was abnormal (51.8 seconds), though her INR was 1.12. Three days prior to starting the study drug her PTT was 38 seconds and her INR was 1.12. There were no other known risk factors for upper gastrointestinal bleeding, however, she was taking tramadol and paracetamol before and throughout the study for treatment of ongoing hip and back pain. She did not have known helicobacter pylori infection, gastroduodenal ulcer disease or severe esophagitis/gastritis/duodenitis or esophagogastric varices. Imaging done approximately 1 month before her death did not reveal disease progression. No autopsy was performed, and no endoscopy had been performed. The upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage is thought by the Applicant not to be related to imlunestrant. It is difficult to assess the cause of upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage due to confounding factors and complicated medical course and FDA cannot rule whether imlunestrant contributed to the upper gastrointestinal hemorrhage event.
L1344: ## 3. Patient (b) (6):
L1345: Tumor mutation status: ESR1/m-not detected
L1346: This 69-year-old American Indian/Alaska Native female in Mexico with metastases to bone and a history of hypertension, gout, and renal atrophy, died of an acute myocardial infarction on day 288. The last dose of study drug was on day 281. She had no dose omissions or dose reductions reported. The patient had experienced intense chest pain on day 267 and was diagnosed with a myocardial infarction. She underwent catheterization and percutaneous coronary intervention at that time for single vessel 70% injury. The patient was discharged from the hospital 3 days after these interventions. She had also received prior treatment with epirubicin and docetaxel for metastatic breast cancer. The acute myocardial infarction is thought not to be related to imlunestrant. The patient had known risk factors for a cardiac event. FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1347: ## 4. Patient (b) (6):
L1348: Tumor mutation status: ESR1/m-not detected
L1349: This 64-year-old American Indian/Alaskan Native female in Mexico with metastases in the liver and history of diabetes mellitus type 2, hypertension, and anemia, died on day 77 from hypovolemic shock. The last dose of imlunestrant was received on day 57. On day 14 she reported gastroesophageal reflux and was treated with omeprazole, sucralfate, and cisapride. On day 54 her imaging studies showed progression of disease in the liver and on day 57 her baseline anemia worsened. She was also noted to have increased blood urea nitrogen, uric acid, and aspartate aminotransferase as well as decrease in albumin on that day. She had no dose omissions
L1350: or dose reductions reported. On day 75 she experienced gastrointestinal bleeding of unknown cause and died on day 77 of hypovolemic shock reportedly secondary to digestive tract bleeding. The gastrointestinal bleeding is thought not to be related to imlunestrant. FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment and cannot exclude that imlunestrant therapy did not contribute to death due to confounding factors.
L1351: ## 5. Patient (b) (6):
L1352: **Tumor mutation status:** *ESR1m-* not detected
L1353: This 72-year-old white female in the United States with metastases in the lung and a history of hypertension, diabetes mellitus 2, hypercholesterolemia, and pulmonary embolism on apixaban died on day 54 of cardiac arrest. The last dose of imlunestrant was on day 54 and she had no dose omissions or reductions. On day 54 she reported heart racing, vomiting and profuse sweating. She died in the ambulance on the way to the hospital within 30 minutes of the onset of her symptoms. Imaging done two days prior revealed progression of disease in multiple bone lesions. The cardiac arrest was thought not to be related to imlunestrant by the investigator and the Applicant. The FDA considers that there is insufficient information to determine if the study treatment contributed to death but cannot exclude the possibility.
L1354: Below is a summary of the death narratives for the 6 deaths identified by the Applicant as related to TEAEs that occurred in patients in the TPC arm of the EMBER-3 trial while on therapy or within 30 days of discontinuation. As stated above, the Applicant had a different definition for deaths "while on therapy or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation." Based on the FDA's definition and review, there were only 3 deaths in the TPC arm. Patient (b) (6) died 33 days after last treatment, patient (b) (6) died 52 days after last treatment, and patient (b) (6) died 46 days after last treatment.
L1355: ## 1. Patient (b) (6):
L1356: **Tumor mutation status:** Not evaluable
L1357: This 68-year-old white female from Mexico with metastases to lung, liver, and bone, and a history of hypertension and diabetes mellitus type 2 complicated by diabetic retinopathy, died of elevated calcium levels on day 62. The patient had previously been treated with cyclophosphamide, epirubicin, docetaxel, and anastrozole. On day 28 she was advised by the investigator to present to a health system because she had been noted to have clinical deterioration and poor performance status. She had also stopped attending scheduled activities and was non-compliant with denosumab treatment. When she presented to a health system for care she was told her calcium was significantly elevated, however, no laboratory results or additional details are available. She received her last dose of fulvestrant on day 29 and on day 62 the patient was admitted to the hospital due to elevated calcium levels; she died the same day. The investigator stated that paraneoplastic syndrome could not be ruled out and that this death was not considered related to study drug. FDA agrees with this assessment. Of note, this death
L1358: occurred after the FDA 30-day definition (33 days after last dose of therapy).
L1359: ## 2. Patient (b) (6):
L1360: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-detected
L1361: This 78-year-old White female from Greece with metastases to liver and bone and history of diabetes mellitus, hyperlipidemia, hyperthyroidism, and chronic kidney disease died on day 165 after a 10-day hospitalization due to chronic kidney disease. No other information or details regarding the fatal event were provided. She received her last dose of fulvestrant on day 113 and had been noted to have chronic kidney disease after signing consent but prior to receiving the first dose of study drug. The investigator did not consider the event related to study drug. FDA agrees with this assessment. Of note, this death occurred after the FDA 30-day definition (52 days after last dose of therapy).
L1362: ## 3. Patient (b) (6):
L1363: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-not detected
L1364: This 59-year-old Asian female from India with metastases to liver and no known hypertension, diabetes mellitus, or cardiac disorder died on day 15 after collection of blood samples from cardiac arrest. This was a witnessed cardiac arrest after which cardiopulmonary resuscitation was started and the patient was admitted to the intensive care unit. Cardiac activity resumed; however, the patient experienced a second cardiac arrest in the intensive care unit. Cardiopulmonary resuscitation was started and the patient was placed on a ventilator. She deteriorated and two days later was comatose. On day 18 the family opted for no further escalation of treatment and comfort care. On day 26 after one fulvestrant dose the patient had another cardiac arrest and resuscitation was not successful. The investigator's opinion is that the cardiac arrest was not related to the study drug. FDA generally agrees with this assessment.
L1365: ## 4. Patient (b) (6):
L1366: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-not detected
L1367: This 47-year-old American Indian/Alaskan Native female from Mexico with metastases to liver and bone died on day 103 of unknown cause. Her last dose of fulvestrant was on day 57 and she was discontinued from the study on day 85 due to progression of disease in her bone metastases. The patient died at home and it was not possible to acquire more information. In the opinion of the investigator the death was not related to study drug. FDA agrees with this assessment. Of note, this death occurred after the FDA 30-day definition (46 days after last dose of therapy).
L1368: ## 5. Patient (b) (6):
L1369: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-detected
L1370: This 73-year-old White female from Argentina with history of hypertension, chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, and obesity died on day 204 due to an abdominal infection. She received her last dose of fulvestrant on day 176. On day 38 she was hospitalized for pneumonia and on day 42 she experienced atrial fibrillation, both of which were treated with antibiotics analgesics,
L1371: hydrocortisone, and enoxaparin. She was discharged from the hospital on day 46 and had recovered from the pneumonia. On day 195 she presented to the hospital with abdominal pain and dyspnea. She was diagnosed with an abdominal infection secondary to an intestinal perforation identified on tomography. She was admitted to the hospital and had surgery to correct a perforation of diverticulum in the sigmoid colon. She was also treated with antibiotics as she developed an abscess. The investigator assessed this death not to be related to study drug. FDA agrees with this assessment.
L1372: ## 6. Patient (b)(6):
L1373: Tumor mutation status: *ESR1m*-not detected
L1374: This 76-year-old White female from France with metastatic breast cancer and history of cardiomegaly, hypertension, chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, and ongoing alcohol use died on study day 497 due to cardiac arrest. On day 411 the patient had cardiac decompensation that lasted approximately 2 weeks. She received the last dose of fulvestrant on day 479 and on day 491 was hospitalized due to ileal occlusion. She underwent surgery one day after hospitalization, but showed signs of respiratory distress two days after the surgery. On day 497 the patient was found in cardiac arrest and the patient died that same day despite cardiopulmonary resuscitation. The investigator did not consider the cardiac arrest related to the study drug. FDA agrees with this assessment.
L1375: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1376: ## Data:
L1377: The incidence of TE-SAEs was similar across treatment arms (**imlunestrant** arm [10.4%] compared to the **SOC** arm [11.4%]) (**Table 8.30**). TE-SAE incidence was also similar in the *ESR1m*-detected safety population across the treatment arms (**imlunestrant** arm [11.7%] compared to the **SOC** arm [14.5%]). The most common TE-SAEs occurring in more than 2 patients in each arm in the overall safety population were *pleural effusion* (1.2%) in the **imlunestrant** arm, and *fracture* (1.5%) and *pneumonia* (0.9%) in the **SOC** arm. The pleural effusions occurred in patients with either a history of pleural effusion, disease progression, or concurrent sepsis. Six patients (1.8%) in each arm had fatal TE-SAEs, including a fatal septic shock captured as due to study disease as per **Table 8.30**. Narratives for fatal TE-SAEs are provided in the CSR.

L1385: The frequency of TE-SAEs in patients with ABC given imlunestrant 400 mg in EMBER was low and similar to that in EMBER-3. Each SAE was reported only once in EMBER. No additional safety findings were identified.
L1386: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1387: The frequencies of TE-SAEs were similar across treatment arms, occurring in 34 (10.4%) of patients who received **imlunestrant** and 37 (11.4%) of patients who received SOC in the overall safety population. The frequencies are similar for the *ESR1m*-detected safety populations as compared to the overall safety population in each arm. The only TE-SAE with an incidence of
L1388: &gt;1% in patients who received **imlunestrant** was pleural effusion (1.2%). There were no SAEs of pleural effusion in patients who received **SOC**.
L1389: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1390: The FDA conducted an independent review of SAEs and agrees with the Applicant that frequencies of TE-SAEs were similar across both treatment arms, in both the ITT and the ESR/m-detected population, and that the only SAE occurring at \ge 1\% in the imlunestrant arm was pleural effusion (1.2%) in EMBER-3. FDA reviewed the narratives for the 4 patients in the imlunestrant arm who experienced pleural effusion and agrees that these occurred in patients with prior pleural effusion, disease progression, or concurrent sepsis. FDA did not independently assess the frequency of TE-SAEs in patients with advanced breast cancer given imlunestrant 400 mg in the EMBER trial but generally agrees with the Applicant's findings that no new SAE signals were seen.
L1391: ## **Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects**
L1392: ## **Data:**
L1393: The incidence of treatment discontinuations due to TEAEs (including deaths on therapy due to TEAEs) was low and similar between the **imlunestrant** arm (4.3%) and the **SOC** arm (1.2%) in the overall safety population. The majority of discontinuations were due to SAEs (9, 2.8% in **imlunestrant** arm and 4, 1.2% in the **SOC** arm) including fatal events (5, 1.5% and 4, 1.2%, respectively). The most frequent event, and the only event occurring in more than 1 patient leading to discontinuation in the **imlunestrant** arm, was alanine aminotransferase (ALT) increased (3, 0.9%).
L1394: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1395: The frequency of treatment discontinuations due to AEs was low in both treatment arms with no individual TEAE leading to discontinuation in \ge 1\% of the participants.
L1396: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1397: The FDA conducted an independent review of TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation in EMBER-3 agrees with the frequencies reported by the Applicant. However, FDA disagrees that incidence of discontinuations due to TEAEs was similar between the imlunestrant arm and the standard of care arm. More patients in the imlunestrant arm discontinued treatment due to a TEAE compared to the treatment of physician's choice arm (4.3% vs. 1.2%, respectively). FDA otherwise agrees with the Applicant's assessment of discontinuations.
L1398: ## Dose Interruptions, Delays, and/or Reductions Due to Adverse Effects
L1399: ## Data:
L1400: The incidence of dose withheld/delay was similar between the **imlunestrant** arm (34, 10.4%) and the **SOC** arm (23, 7.1%) in the overall safety population. The most common TEAEs leading to dose withheld for imlunestrant were vomiting (5, 1.5%), aspartate aminotransferase (AST) increased (3, 0.9%), and COVID-19 (3, 0.9%).
L1401: The dose reductions due to TEAE were low and occurred in 8 of 327 (2.4%) patients in the **imlunestrant** arm in the overall safety population. The only TEAE leading to dose reduction occurring in more than 1 patient was AST increased (2, 0.6%). No dose reductions in the **SOC** arm were reported.
L1402: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1403: Overall, the incidence of dose adjustments due to TEAEs was low and similar in the **imlunestrant** arm compared to the **SOC** arm (10.4% versus 7.1%).
L1404: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1405: The FDA conducted an independent assessment of TEAEs leading to dose withheld/delayed. FDA disagrees that the incidence of dose adjustments due to TEAEs was similar in the imlunestrant arm compared to the treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm. More patients in the imlunestrant arm experienced dose withheld/delay than patients in the TPC arm (10.4% vs 7.1%, respectively). Diarrhea, neutrophil count decreased, anemia, and alanine aminotransferase increased were additional TEAEs that led to dose withheld/delayed in \ge 1 patient in the imlunestrant arm (2, 0.6%, each).
L1406: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on dose reductions.
L1407: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1408: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1409: Significant AEs are reported and described in SAEs preceding this section, in the following "Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions" paragraph, and in Section 8.2.5.1.
L1410: Refer to **Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects** and **Dose Interruptions, Delays, and/or Reductions Due to Adverse Effects** for discussion of TEAEs leading to
L1411: discontinuation of study treatment and TEAEs leading to any dose adjustments of study treatment, respectively.
L1412: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1413: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Refer to the next section "Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions" and section 8.2.5.1 for further details.
L1414: ## Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1415: ## **Data:**
L1416: In the overall safety population, the incidence of TEAEs were similar across both treatment arms. Within the *ESR/m*-detected safety population a higher incidence of any-grade TEAEs was noted in the **imlunestrant** arm than the SOC (**imlunestrant** arm: 89.8% vs SOC: 79.5%).
L1417: As the incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, TE-*SAEs*, deaths due to AEs, were all similar within the *ESR/m*-detected safety population (*imlunestrant* vs SOC) and between *ESR/m*-detected and overall safety populations for both arms, the TEAEs incidence difference was not considered clinically meaningful. The most common any-grade TEAEs in the **imlunestrant** arm were *Fatigue*, *Diarrhea*, and *Nausea*.

L1434: Note: Consolidated terms are *italicized*.
L1435: Source: Table JZLC.5.32 and Table JZLC.8.228
L1436: ## Any Grade Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
L1437: The safety profile of imlunestrant monotherapy was consistent with the mechanism of action of SERDs and events commonly experienced by patients with ABC.
L1438: Incidence of any grade TEAEs was similar across treatment arms. The majority of TEAEs were Grade 1 or 2.
L1439: The most common any grade TEAEs in the **imlunestrant** arm were *fatigue*, diarrhea, and nausea, with *fatigue* and diarrhea occurring over 5% more frequently in the **imlunestrant** arm compared to the **SOC** arm.
L1440: Diarrhea was a frequently reported TEAE for the **imlunestrant** arm (21.4%). The majority of events were Grade 1 (60, 18.3%), occurred early in treatment (median time to first onset was 30 days), and did not lead to any discontinuations or dose reductions in either arm. The majority of patients experienced only 1 episode of diarrhea.
L1441: Nausea was a frequently reported TEAE for the **imlunestrant** arm (17.1%). The majority of events were Grade 1 (44, 13.5%), occurred during the first month of treatment (36, 11%), median time to onset (19.5 days) and did not lead to any discontinuations, with 1 dose reduction. The majority of patients experienced only 1 episode of nausea.
L1442: The most common any grade TEAEs in the **SOC** arm were arthralgia, *fatigue*, and nausea.
L1443: *Injection site reaction* was the only any grade TEAE to occur 5% more frequently in the **SOC** arm compared to the **imlunestrant** arm (8.0% versus 0%).
L1444: ## Grade \ge 3 Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
L1445: The incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was similar in the **imlunestrant** arm (17.1%) and the **SOC** arm (20.7%). These events were predominantly Grade 3 (14.1% versus 16.7%), with lower but similar incidences for Grade 4 (1.2% versus 2.2%) and Grade 5 (1.8% versus 1.9%) TEAEs for **imlunestrant** and **SOC** arm, respectively. Grade 5 TEAEs are discussed further in the CSR.
L1446: The most common Grade \ge 3 TEAEs in both the **imlunestrant** and **SOC** arms were associated with laboratory investigations, which were reported with similar incidences in both arms. The most common Grade \ge 3 TEAEs for imlunestrant occurring &gt;1% were *anemia* (2.1%), *neutropenia* (2.1%), and gamma-glutamyltransferase increased (1.5%). The most common Grade \ge 3 TEAEs for the **SOC** arm occurring &gt;1% were *anemia* (2.8%), neutropenia (1.9%), gamma-glutamyltransferase increased (1.9%), and *thrombocytopenia* (1.2%). There were no clinically meaningful differences in Grade \ge 3 TEAE incidences noted between treatment arms.
L1447: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1448: - In the overall safety population, the incidence of TEAEs were similar across both treatment arms. Within the *ESR/m*-detected safety population, a higher incidence of any-grade TEAEs was noted in the **imlunestrant** arm as compared to **SOC** arm.
L1449: - The incidence of any grade and Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was similar between both arms, with fatigue, diarrhea, and nausea reported as the most frequently experienced TEAEs in the **imlunestrant** arm.
- The incidence of TE-SAEs and deaths due to AEs were similar across treatment arms.
- The imlunestrant safety profile was manageable through routine monitoring and standard clinical practice as evidenced by low rates of dose adjustments or treatment discontinuation due to AEs.
L1450: The safety profile in the *ESR1-m*-detected safety population was consistent with the overall safety population.
L1451: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1452: The FDA conducted an independent assessment of TEAEs in the EMBER-3 trial. The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the safety profile for patients with *ESR1-m* detected status was similar to the safety profile for all patients in the EMBER-3 trial. FDA also agrees with the frequencies of all-grade TEAEs, grade \ge 3 TEAEs, SAEs, TEAEs leading to dosage interruption, dose reduction, and drug discontinuation presented in the Applicants Overview of TEAEs table (Table 8.26). Based on FDA's assessment of TEAEs leading to death while on treatment or within 30 days of last treatment, FDA disagrees there were 6 deaths in the treatment of physician's choice (TPC) arm as 3 of the 6 deaths occurred at time points greater than 30 days since last treatment was received. See Section 8.2.4 for more details.
L1453: The FDA's analysis of all-grade and grade \ge 3 TEAEs that occurred at a frequency \ge 10\% is shown in **Table 8.33** below and this information is also included in Section 6 of the imlunestrant USPI. Though all-grade anemia occurred at a frequency of \ge 10\%, it was not included in **Table 8.33** as hemoglobin decreased collected as a laboratory abnormality occurred at a higher frequency and was included in Table 4 of the USPI for imlunestrant. In FDA's review, musculoskeletal pain (which included the preferred terms arthralgia and back pain), was also a common all-grade (30%) and grade \ge 3 (3.7%) TEAE in the imlunestrant arm. The all-grade incidence of musculoskeletal pain was similar in the TPC arm (29%), but the incidence of grade \ge 3 musculoskeletal pain was lower in the TPC arm (1.9%).
L1454: While diarrhea did not lead to any discontinuations or dose reductions, there were 2 cases of diarrhea that led to dose withheld/delayed. Further information about dosage interruption, dose reduction, and drug discontinuation can be found in the corresponding sections above.
L1455: The FDA agrees that fatigue and diarrhea occurred over 5% more frequently in the imlunestrant arm compared to the TPC arm.
L1456: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of injection site reaction in patients who received fulvestrant and also notes that no patients who received imlunestrant experienced injection site reaction.
L1457: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the incidence of any grade TEAEs and Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was similar across treatment arms. The majority of TEAEs were Grade 1 or 2. FDA disagrees with the Applicant that the safety profile of imlunestrant monotherapy was consistent with the mechanism of action of SERDs and events commonly experienced by patients with HR+/HER2-advanced breast cancer. Since imlunestrant is a new molecular entity (NME) this is the first assessment of this drug's safety database and all safety findings are new. The FDA agree with the Applicant that the safety profile of imlunestrant was manageable through routine monitoring.

L1462: ## Laboratory Findings
L1463: ## Data:
L1464: Findings from analysis of laboratory data for serum hematology and chemistry were generally similar between the **imlunestrant** arm and the **SOC** arm in the overall and the **ESR1m-detected**
L1465: safety population. The percentage of patients with reported treatment-emergent laboratory abnormalities was consistent with, but generally higher than, the percentage of patients with laboratory abnormalities reported as TEAEs, as only those deemed clinically relevant were to be reported as AEs.
L1466: Treatment-emergent laboratory abnormalities by the maximum postbaseline CTCAE grade for AST/ALT increased are summarized in Table 8.34.

L1473: Following analysis of laboratory values at baseline and worst CTCAE grade, postbaseline hematology and chemistry laboratory data indicated no additional safety findings as compared to TEAE data. Details regarding TEAEs of elevated transaminases are provided in the AESI section (Section 8.2.5.1).
L1474: In the ESR1m-detected safety population there was a higher frequency of elevated ALT and AST in the imlunestrant arm compared to SOC (ALT: 44.6% vs 31.2%; AST: 37.6% vs 32.4%). The frequency of Grade 3 or 4 ALT / AST elevations was low in both arms.
L1475: In addition, the incidence of any grade ALT/AST elevation in the imlunestrant arm was higher in the *ESR/m*-detected safety population compared to the overall safety population (ALT: 44.6% vs 32.8%; AST: 37.6% vs 31.8%). However, the Grade-3 or 4 ALT/AST elevations were similar.
L1476: Serum lipids were evaluated during the EMBER-3 study due to the known mechanism of action and effects of ET on lipid levels. A similarly increased incidence of treatment-emergent maximum postbaseline serum lipids was noted across treatment arms in both the overall and the *ESR/m*-detected safety population.
L1477: In the overall safety population, the incidence of any grade high cholesterol in the **imlunestrant** arm compared to **SOC** arm was 10.2% versus 12.3%, respectively. In the *ESR/m*-detected safety population, the incidence of any grade high cholesterol in the **imlunestrant** arm compared to **SOC** arm was similar at 10.5% versus 9.9%, respectively and consistent with the overall safety population.
L1478: In the overall safety population, the incidence of any grade high triglycerides in the **imlunestrant** arm compared to **SOC** arm was 21.0% versus 22.0%, respectively. In the *ESR/m*-detected safety population the incidence of any grade high triglycerides in the **imlunestrant** arm compared to **SOC** arm was 18.6% versus 20.0%, respectively and was similar to the overall safety population. Of note, the increases in cholesterol that were observed were driven by increases in high-density lipoprotein rather than low-density lipoprotein in both the treatment arms.
L1479: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1480: Serum hematology and chemistry findings were generally similar across treatment arms and were consistent with the TEAEs reported. No additional safety findings were noted.
L1481: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1482: The FDA conducted an independent safety analysis of laboratory abnormalities in the EMBER-3 trial based on laboratory values that worsened from baseline and disagrees with the Applicant's description of AST/ALT increase as summarized in the Table 8.28 above as the Applicant was not using baseline values in all cases to calculate the change in lab value (Grade worsened compared to baseline). FDA sent additional requests for information to the Applicant to recalculate the change in grade from baseline and independently verified these values.
L1483: The FDA laboratory analysis is shown in **Table 8.35** below, which is also included in section 6 of the imlunestrant USPI. FDA disagrees with the Applicant that the laboratory data indicated no additional safety findings compared to the TEAE data as these reflect more accurately the incidence of abnormalities potentially related to therapy regardless of attribution. FDA found hemoglobin decreased, neutrophil decreased, platelet decreased, triglycerides increased, cholesterol increased, calcium decreased, aspartate aminotransferase (AST) increased, and alanine aminotransferase (ALT) increased to be relevant safety findings identified in review of
L1484: the laboratory data for the EMBER-3 trial. Though these laboratory abnormalities were experienced at similar frequencies in both arms, they occurred at high frequencies and are clinically meaningful. Further information about AST increased and ALT increased is found in **Section 8.2.5** of this review.

L1495: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1496: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1497: Single local ECGs were obtained after baseline for patients in the **imlunestrant** arm and were taken on Day 1 of Cycles 1, 2, and 4, and at short-term follow-up. In addition to TEAEs, all patients were randomly assigned to the **imlunestrant** arm had QTc interval documented after 10 August 2022.
L1498: Analyses of ECGs did not reveal any safety concerns relative to the analysis of TEAE data.
L1499: Vital signs and ECG analyses observed in EMBER-3 were consistent with the respective patient population in EMBER.
L1500: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1501: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that ECG analysis did not reveal any additional safety issues in the EMBER-3 trial. No clinically meaningful change (\ge 20 msec mean increase in QTc interval) was noted. FDA did not review the ECG data for patients in the EMBER trial. Refer to FDA QT Study Review dated 5/20/2025 for additional information.
L1502: ## QT
L1503: ## Data:
L1504: ## *Imlunestrant monotherapy*
L1505: - Of the 327 patients treated with imlunestrant monotherapy, 3 patients experienced a TEAE of ECG QT prolonged and 1 patient experienced a TEAE of syncope. These events were of low grade (Grade 1 [n = 3] and Grade 2 [n = 1]) and were not serious.
- Of the 3 patients who experienced a TEAE of ECG QTc prolongation, 2 patients had prolonged QTc interval at baseline. In the additional patient, the event occurred 1 month after the discontinuation of imlunestrant.
L1506: - Grade 1 TEAE of syncope was reported in a patient who had discontinued the study drug 29 days prior to the event. Of note, a Grade 2 event of general health deterioration was reported 23 days prior to the TEAE of syncope.
- No dose reduction, dose hold, or dose discontinuations were reported due to these events.
L1507: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1508: No increased incidence of AEs that signal a potential for proarrhythmic effects (ICH E14 Section 4) were noted.
L1509: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1510: The Interdisciplinary Review Team for Cardiac Safety (QT-IRT) at the FDA conducted a review of QT and concluded that imlunestrant does not demonstrate mean QTc interval prolongation \ge 20 msec, though it cannot be concluded that imlunestrant has no impact on QTc because there was no positive control in studies performed. They also concluded that available data for imlunestrant do not support concluding a low likelihood of proarrhythmic risk due to delayed repolarization. See the QT Study Review dated 5/20/2025 for additional information.

L1512: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1513: Not applicable.
L1514: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1515: Not applicable.
L1516: ## 8.2.5 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1517: ## 8.2.5.1 Adverse Events of Special Interest
L1518: ## Data:
L1519: Based on considerations of preclinical toxicology and safety data observed during healthy volunteer early phase studies and ongoing clinical trials, transaminase increases are deemed an AESI for imlunestrant. After evaluation of the EMBER-3 data, there were no cases that met Hy's Law criteria.
L1520: The incidence of elevated transaminase events in any grade and Grade \ge 3 was similar between the **imlunestrant** arm and the **SOC** arm (15.6% versus 14.8% any grade, respectively; and Grade \ge 3 was 1.2% for both (Table 8.36).
L1521: In the *ESR/m*-detected safety population the incidence of TE-elevated transaminase events was similar between treatment arms, any grade (imlunestrant: 14.6% versus SOC: 12%) and Grade \ge 3 (imlunestrant: 0.7 % versus SOC: 1.7%). The majority of transaminase elevations were low grade, did not require dose adjustments, and led to few discontinuations in both the overall (1.2%) and *ESR/m*-detected safety population (1.5%) (Table 8.37).
L1522: This allowed the majority of patients who experienced these events to continue on therapy (92.2% **imlunestrant** arm versus all patients in **SOC** arm). Only 1 case of hepatotoxicity, in a patient with liver metastases at baseline, was serious.
L1523: The most frequently reported preferred terms of transaminase elevations were ALT and AST increased, which had similar incidences in both treatment arms. The events in both arms occurred early in treatment (median time to first onset any grade TEAE was 58 days for **imlunestrant** and 42.5 days for **SOC**)

L1533: Consistent with the reported incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs of ALT and AST increased, the
L1534: incidence of Grade \ge 3 laboratory evaluations of ALT and AST increased was also low. The CSR provides a summary of abnormal hepatic laboratory results in the **imlunestrant** arm and the **SOC** arm.
L1535: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1536: The AESI of transaminase increase are monitorable and manageable with few patients discontinuing or dose reducing, allowing most patients who experience these events to continue therapy.
L1537: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1538: The Applicant designated elevated transaminases as an AE of special interest and the FDA designated dyslipidemia as an AE of special interest. The FDA conducted independent safety analyses focused on elevated transaminases and dyslipidemia.
L1539: ## Elevated Transaminases
L1540: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position, the information presented by the Applicant in **Table 8.37** above, and that the most frequently reported preferred terms of transaminase elevations were ALT and AST increased, which occurred in similar frequency in both arms and were predominantly low grade. An independent review by FDA of cases that met Hy's Law criteria revealed no cases. There were three cases of patients with AST or ALT &gt; 3 \times ULN or total bilirubin elevation &gt; 2 \times ULN that were reviewed by FDA, and all patients had liver metastases and/or abnormal liver enzymes at baseline. Due to confounding, it is difficult to conclude whether changes were directly related to treatment with imlunestrant.
L1541: Of note, AST or ALT increased were among the most common causes of dose withheld/delay, AST increased was a common cause for dose reduction, and ALT increased was the only cause for discontinuation in more than 1 patient in the imlunestrant arm. See relevant sections above on Dropouts/Discontinuations/Dose Interruptions for further details. AST and ALT increased are included in section 6 of the USPI for imlunestrant. The FDA agrees with the Applicant that elevated transaminases can be monitored and managed in patients taking imlunestrant with appropriate labeling.
L1542: ## Dyslipidemia – Hypertriglyceridemia and hypercholesterolemia
L1543: Since dyslipidemia has been associated with other endocrine therapies used to treat ER+/HER2-breast cancer, such as aromatase inhibitors, selective estrogen receptor modulators, and selective estrogen receptor degraders, the FDA conducted additional analyses to assess TEAEs of hypertriglyceridemia and hypercholesterolemia more comprehensively in patients who received imlunestrant in the EMBER-3 trial.
L1544: Hypertriglyceridemia and hypercholesterolemia occurred in 21% and 10% of patients who received imlunestrant in the EMBER-3 trial, and in 22% and 12% of patients who received TPC. Grade \ge 3 triglycerides increased occurred in 0 patients in the imlunestrant arm and in 1.2% of patients in the TPC arm. Grade \ge 3 cholesterol increased occurred in 0 patients in both the imlunestrant and TPC arm. There were no dose adjustments, dose holds, or treatment discontinuations due to hypertriglyceridemia or hypercholesterolemia.
L1545: FDA reviewed the monitoring and management of lipid panel labs as described in the protocol for the EMBER-3 trial, the use of concomitant medications for the management of dyslipidemia, and protocol deviations associated with missing lipid panel labs. No significant aberrancies or differences between the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm were identified. FDA also requested an analysis of frequency of triglycerides increased and cholesterol increased by geographic region from the Applicant. This analysis revealed no significant differences in the frequency of all grade or Grade \ge 3 triglycerides increased or cholesterol increased across North America/Western Europe, East Asia, or Other; although, these analyses are limited due to small sample size and exploratory nature of the analysis.
L1546: Based on the totality of information reviewed, FDA did not include triglycerides increased and cholesterol increased in the Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI for imlunestrant, but these were included in the safety sections of the USPI. Prescribers are recommended to monitor the lipid panel at baseline and periodically throughout treatment with imlunestrant.
L1547: ## 8.2.6 Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1548: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1549: Patient-reported outcomes have been previously discussed in Section 8.1.2.
L1550: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1551: See FDA Assessment of patient-reported outcomes in Section 8.1.2.
L1552: ## 8.2.7 Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1553: ## Data:
L1554: Not applicable.
L1555: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1556: Supportive analyses were performed to assess the safety and tolerability of imlunestrant across selected subgroups (for example, age group, race, region, and selected baseline disease characteristics). Overall, the safety profile within each subgroup per treatment arm was consistent with the overall safety population. Although numerical differences were noted in the subpopulations by age/region, these differences are expected in the patient population and are not considered to be clinically significant.
L1557: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1558: The FDA performed an independent analysis of safety based on age \le 65 years and &gt;65 years. The FDA agrees there were no clinically significant differences in the safety profile of imlunestrant based on age using an age cutoff of 65 years. There were too few patients &gt;75 years to assess for differences in safety using an age cutoff of 75 years. There were numerical differences in all grade TEAEs and Grade \ge 3 by region, but FDA agrees with the Applicant these differences do not appear clinically significant.
L1559: While there were no clinically significant differences in the safety profile across race groups in the analysis conducted by FDA, no definitive conclusions can be drawn given there were few Black or African American patients in the safety population of the EMBER-3 trial relative to the prevalence of ER+/HER2- advanced breast cancer in this population in the US. There were only 5 male participants in the EMBER-3 trial, which limited assessment of differences in safety based on sex, but did not alter FDA's overall conclusions about the safety profile of imlunestrant. FDA did not conduct an analysis of safety based on baseline characteristics. The FDA examined safety in the ESR1m-detected subgroup and the entire EMBER-3 trial safety population and did not note major differences in safety.

The potential risks regarding the use of imlunestrant in patients of reproductive potential, use during pregnancy and use during lactation will be addressed in product labeling.

L1597: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1598: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1599: Potential safety concerns beyond the risks conveyed in the proposed labeling are not expected. Routine pharmacovigilance practices will be used to monitor for unexpected events.
L1600: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1601: The safety assessment for imlunestrant for this NDA review is primarily based on the 327 patients treated in the imlunestrant arm of the EMBER-3 trial. The FDA will continue to monitor post-marketing reports and safety reports submitted after approval.
L1602: ## 8.2.11 Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1603: ## Data
L1604: A pooled safety analysis for patients with ER+, HER2- ABC in EMBER-3 and EMBER receiving the 400-mg dose as monotherapy was reviewed. The pooled analysis sets increase the size of the datasets for patients receiving imlunestrant 400 mg daily and increase the robustness of the safety assessment. Review of the pooled data did not reveal any additional safety findings.
L1605: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1606: Results of analyses performed for individual safety parameters using the analysis sets described were reviewed, and they revealed an overall consistency in results between the primary analysis sets and the supportive and the pooled analysis sets.
L1607: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1608: The FDA safety assessment for imlunestrant considered the data from the EMBER trial as supportive and determined the safety data from EMBER-3 was adequate for the NDA review as it represented the indicated population. The safety profile of imlunestrant monotherapy is based on review of data from the overall safety population in the EMBER-3 trial, including both patients with *ESR1m*-detected and *ESR1m*-not detected status. The overall safety population in the EMBER-3 trial consisted of 651 patients (327 in the imlunestrant arm, and 254 of these with *ESR1m*-detected status). As no differences were identified between the safety profile of imlunestrant in patients with *ESR1m*-detected and *ESR1m*-not detected status, the safety review was reported for the overall safety population.
L1609: The safety profile for imlunestrant monotherapy was consistent with the anticipated safety concerns for selective estrogen receptor degraders (SERDs). The most common TEAEs included fatigue, diarrhea, nausea, and musculoskeletal pain. The majority of TEAEs were low grade, but clinically significant as they can impact quality of life. The frequency of fatigue and diarrhea was numerically higher in the imlunestrant arm compared to the TPC arm. For further details refer to Section 8.2.4. Elevated transaminases and dyslipidemia were AEs of special interest, with elevated transaminases including the preferred terms ALT increased and AST increased. ALT
L1610: increased and AST increased were among the most common causes for dose delays, interruptions, and discontinuations in the imlunestrant arm. For additional FDA conclusions regarding safety, refer to Section 8.2.
L1611: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1612: ## 8.3 Statistical Issues
L1613: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1614: The randomization for EMBER-3 was stratified on the following factors: prior CDK4/6i therapy, visceral metastases, and region. The *ESR1* mutation status was not a stratification factor at the time of randomization. Although the key measured demographics and baseline disease characteristics are generally balanced between treatment arms, there is still a risk of potential imbalance between treatment arms for unmeasured baseline characteristics.
L1615: EMBER-3 did pre-specify formal testing of treatment benefit of PFS and OS in the *ESR1*-m-detected population. EMBER-3 demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in investigator-assessed PFS in the *ESR1*-m-detected population. These results were robust to several sensitivity analyses, including a restricted mean survival time analysis. EMBER3 did not meet its primary endpoint of investigator-assessed PFS in the ITT population and no benefit was demonstrated in the *ESR1*-m-nd population.
L1616: At the time of the primary PFS analyses, the IA1 for the key secondary endpoint of OS for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1*-m-detected population did not reach statistical significance. However, no trend toward a survival detriment was observed in the *ESR1*-m-detected population. Therefore, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed indication for patients with a detected *ESR1*-m.
L1617: OS curves for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1*-m-detected population overlapped for the first 3 months, favored to the TPC arm from about 3 to 7 months, and began to separate in favor of the imlunestrant arm from Month 7 onward. It was noted that the number of OS events that occurred before Month 7 was small. Although OS curves began to separate from Month 7 in favor of the imlunestrant arm, the censoring for OS was heavy starting from Month 8 since many patients were administratively censored, therefore, there is still uncertainty in the OS result and further review of the preplanned remaining interim and final OS analyses for the comparison of the imlunestrant arm and the TPC arm in the *ESR1*-m-detected population with more mature data is important for both efficacy and safety. The Applicant committed to submit SAP version 5 pre-specified interim and final OS results as a PMC.
L1618: ## 8.4 Conclusions and Recommendations
L1619: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1620: EMBER-3 was a randomized, active-controlled, open-label, multicenter trial that enrolled 661 patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer to receive either imlunestrant monotherapy or TPC. The trial demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in investigator-assessed PFS in the *ESR1m* population. This finding was further supported by a favorable trend in OS HR in the *ESR1m* population. The trial did not meet the prespecified efficacy threshold for the ITT population and exploratory analyses suggested that there was not benefit in the *ESR1m-nd* population.
L1621: Imlunestrant was associated with increased fatigue, gastrointestinal adverse events, and elevated transaminases compared to treatment of physician's choice.
L1622: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assessment that an oral route of administration is inherently superior to other routes of administration. In this instance patients are required to take imlunestrant in a fasted state. Additionally, orally administered drugs may be associated with increased gastrointestinal toxicity.
L1623: The review team recommends regular approval for imlunestrant for the following indication:
L1624: *Imlunestrant (INLURIYO) is indicated for the treatment of adults with ER-positive, HER2-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.*

L1729: ## 19.4 OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1730: ## 19.4.1 Population PK Analysis
L1731: ## 19.4.1.1 Executive Summary
L1732: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1733: The popPK analyses assessed the effect of covariates on PK parameters of imlunestrant. Notably,
L1734: imlunestrant disposition was not affected by multiple covariates including drug formulation, presence of abemaciclib, age, ethnicity, race, serum albumin levels, mild-to-moderate renal impairment, and mild hepatic impairment.
L1735: Body weight showed statistical significance but limited clinical impact, with exposure differences of 26-40% across the weight range. The simulation results from the final model demonstrated that 87% of patients receiving the proposed 400 mg once daily dose are expected to achieve minimum concentrations exceeding biologically effective thresholds throughout the dosing interval.
L1736: The Applicant's dataset and analyses were reviewed and verified by the pharmacometrics reviewers. The final model was deemed adequate to characterize the PK profile of imlunestrant in healthy participants and patients with ER+ breast cancer and endometrial endometroid cancer (EEC). The Applicant's popPK analysis is acceptable.

L1776: ## 19.4.2 Exposure-Response Analysis
L1777: ## 19.4.2.1 ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L1778: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1779: The Applicant conducted an exposure-response (E-R) analysis for efficacy using data from Study J2J-OX-JZLC (EMBER-3). Initially, the Applicant concluded that higher imlunestrant exposures were associated with longer progression-free survival (PFS). This conclusion was found to be incorrect as the Applicant identified a mistake in unit conversion during the review. After correcting the errors and updating the exposure-PFS analysis, no significant exposure-PFS relationship was identified. The corrected analysis is acceptable.
L1780: In general, the E-R relationship for efficacy was considered not fully characterized because the analysis included data from only one dose level (400 mg QD) evaluated in the pivotal EMBER-3 study. Labeling language in Section 12.2 (Pharmacodynamics) was updated to reflect this limitation.
L1781: ## 19.4.2.2 ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L1782: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1783: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Characterize the relationship between imlunestrant systemic exposure and selected efficacy outcomes (PFS and CBR) in patients with ER+, HER2- locally advanced or MBC
Study Included	JZLC (EMBER-3)
Endpoint	Primary: PFS
Secondary: CBR
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	527
Population Characteristics (Table 19.1)	General	See Table 19.1
Pediatrics (if any)	Not applicable
Dose(s) Included	400 mg QD
L1784: Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Average concentration, C_{avg} (range: 18.1 – 389.8 ng/mL)
Covariates Evaluated	ESR1 mutation status, Presence of abemaciclib, Prior CDK4/6 therapy, Number of prior lines of treatment, Presence of visceral metastases
Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Model Structure	PFS: Time-to-event model with concentration effects on baseline hazard and Gompertz shape parameter
CBR: logistic regression model with concentration effects on CBR	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 19.3, Table 19.4	See below
Model Evaluation	Figure 19.5	See below
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Significant factors leading to increase CBR included:
- Presence of abemaciclib
- Prior CDK4/6 therapy - Presence of visceral metastases	FDA disagrees with the positive relationship between imlunestrant exposure and PFS. During the review process, the Applicant identified an error in the description of the exposure–PFS analysis presented in the EMBER-3 population PK/PD report. Specifically, an incorrect unit conversion in the dataset used for the exposure–PFS analysis was discovered, which altered the interpretation of the results. The previously reported positive relationship between exposure and PFS
L1785: based on EMBER-3 data was incorrect; no exposure-PFS relationship was observed. In other words, variations in imlunestrant exposure at the 400 mg once-daily dose in the EMBER-3 trial did not affect PFS.
Simulation for Specific Population	Figure 19.5	Updated based on Applicant's addendum analysis submitted on May 5, 2025
Visualization of E-R relationships	Figure 19.6	Updated based on Applicant's addendum analysis submitted on May 5, 2025
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	Exposures up to 2-fold below that of 400 mg are still expected to have some therapeutic benefit	See below
Labeling Language	Description	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
12.2 Pharmacodynamics	Not applicable	FDA modified language in this section and the Applicant accepted the modifications.

L1818: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1819: The Applicant updated the exposure-PFS analyses in their addendum PK/PD report submitted on May 5th, 2025. (b) (4)
L1820: They are updated with Table 19.3 (addendum), Figure 19.5 (addendum) and Figure 19.6 (addendum) below, respectively. FDA agrees with the corrected exposure-PFS analysis in the Applicant's addendum PK/PD report. No significant relationship between imlunestrant exposure and PFS was identified within the therapeutic exposure range at the 400 mg QD dosage.
L1821: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusion from the corrected analysis that there is no
L1822: significant relationship between imlunestrant exposure and PFS. The initial statement—that patients with 2-fold higher exposures experienced approximately 5 months longer median PFS compared to the mean exposure at 400 mg once daily, while patients with 2-fold lower exposures had approximately 2 months shorter median PFS—(b)(4) and should be discarded based on the corrected exposure-PFS analysis presented in the addendum PK/PD report.

L1838: As noted above, the exposure-response relationship for efficacy should be interpreted with caution as this relationship was characterized from a single dose level (400 mg QD) in the pivotal EMBER-3 study, which limits the ability to fully characterize the E-R relationship across a broader range of therapeutic exposures.
L1839: ## 19.4.2.3 ER (safety) Executive Summary
L1840: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1841: The Applicant conducted E-R analysis for safety of imlunestrant by evaluating the relationship between drug exposure and adverse events in patients with estrogen receptor positive (ER+) breast cancer and EEC.
L1842: The safety analysis included 473 patients from monotherapy arms of Studies EMBER and EMBER-3. Four key safety endpoints of interest were evaluated: serious adverse events (SAEs), nausea, diarrhea, and fatigue. These endpoints were selected based on their frequency and clinical importance.
L1843: There were no significant relationships between imlunestrant exposure and selected safety endpoints. The Applicant's E-R analysis for safety is acceptable.
L1844: ## 19.4.2.4 ER (safety) Assessment Summary

L1860: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1861: No significant relationships between imlunestrant exposure and serious TEAEs were identified. Higher exposure to imlunestrant was associated with lower incidence of other AEs of interest (nausea, diarrhea, and fatigue) in the E-R analysis. Such reverse E-R relationships for safety
L1862: should be interpreted with caution. In Study EMBER-2, higher doses of imlunestrant were associated with higher rates of GI adverse events which conflicts with the E-R finding.
L1863: ## 19.4.2.5 ER Review Issues
L1864: The FDA's Assessment:
L1865: See **Section 19.4.2.2**.
L1866: ## 19.4.2.6 Reviewer's Independent Analysis
L1867: The FDA's Assessment:
L1868: Not applicable.
L1869: ## 19.4.2.7 Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L1870: The Applicant's Position:
L1871: The proposed imlunestrant dose of 400 mg QD is associated with meaningful benefit and an acceptable safety profile. A 2.5-fold increase in exposure from 400 mg QD is not expected to have a major impact on safety. Dose reductions for tolerability from 400 mg QD to 200 mg QD are expected to maintain sufficient benefit to warrant continued treatment. Overall, the totality of data from EMBER-3 demonstrates a favorable benefit-risk profile for Imlunestrant 400mg QD and a reduced dose of 200mg QD when needed to maintain tolerability.
L1872: The FDA's Assessment:
L1873: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's initial statement that there was a significant relationship between imlunestrant exposure and PFS due to the error in the initial analysis as described above. There were no clear E-R relationships for efficacy or safety of imlunestrant. However, the proposed dosage of 400 mg QD is supported by the totality of data including the E-R analyses. The Applicant's position regarding overall benefit-risk is stated in **Section 8.4**. See FDA's Assessment in **Section 1** and **Section 8.4**.

L1897: ## *Assessment Conclusions*
L1898: The Applicant has sufficiently validated bioanalytical methods for determination of imlunestrant concentration in human plasma by HPLC with MS/MS detection (8415876) as well as in K2EDTA plasma by LC-MS/MS (400008-213865-PMV_1).
L1899: The precision and accuracy of quality control (QC) were within the prespecified acceptance limits. The Applicant validated the selectivity, matrix effect, hemolysis effect, lipemic effect, dilution linearity, bench-top and process stability, and freeze-thaw stability. The long term stability was established for concentration control was 1393 days at -70°C and 960 days at -70°C for methods 8415876 and 400008-213865-PMV_1, respectively.

L1916: Figure 5.1 displays two graphs illustrating the anti-tumor activity of imlunestrant in the MCF-fluc brain orthotopic model.
(A) Total Flux [p/s] vs Days: This graph shows the total flux of luciferase activity over 56 days. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 10^6 to 10^{11} p/s. The X-axis represents Days (0 to 56). A vertical dashed line indicates the Dosing period (approximately Days 7 to 35). Four groups are compared:
Vehicle Control, QD x 28, PO (Black circles)
LY3484356, 10 mpk, QD x 28, PO (Green squares)
LY3484356, 30 mpk, QD x 28, PO (Blue triangles)
LY3484356, 60 mpk, QD x 28, PO (Red inverted triangles)
The Vehicle Control group shows the highest flux, increasing sharply after Day 7. The LY3484356 groups show significantly lower flux, with the 60 mpk dose appearing to maintain the lowest flux levels after the dosing period.
(B) Percent Survival vs Days Post Implant: This graph shows the percentage of surviving animals over 56 days post-implant. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 125% (Percent Survival). The X-axis represents Days Post Implant (0 to 56). A vertical dashed line indicates the Dosing period (approximately Days 7 to 35). Four groups are compared:
Vehicle Control, QD x 28, PO (Black line)
LY3484356, 10 mpk, QD x 28, PO (Green line)
LY3484356, 30 mpk, QD x 28, PO (Blue line)
LY3484356, 60 mpk, QD x 28, PO (Red line)
The Vehicle Control group shows the lowest survival rate, dropping to approximately 10% by Day 56. The LY3484356 groups show significantly improved survival rates. The 60 mpk dose group shows the highest survival rate, reaching approximately 90% by Day 56.
L1917: Graph showing Bodyweight (g) (Sex: Male) versus Day(s) Relative to Start Date (0 to 200). The Y-axis ranges from 300 to 750 g. Five dose groups are plotted: 1.0 mg/kg/day (blue squares), 2.10 mg/kg/day (orange stars), 3.30 mg/kg/day (green triangles), 4.60 mg/kg/day (red circles), and 5.150 mg/kg/day (purple diamonds). All groups show a steady increase in bodyweight, stabilizing around 550-600 g by Day 160. The 1.0 mg/kg/day group shows the highest bodyweight, peaking around 680 g.

All groups show a steady increase in bodyweight, stabilizing around 400-440 g by Day 160. The 1.0 mg/kg/day group shows the highest bodyweight, peaking around 440 g.

L1921: Flowchart illustrating the trial design:
Eligibility
ER+/HER2- breast cancer
Locally advanced or metastatic
Prior treatment with an AI, alone or in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitorPrior treatment with a CDK4/6 inhibitor expected if this treatment is approved and can be reimbursed

No other prior therapy for advanced diseaseNo prior SERD/chemotherapy/inhibitor of PI3K/mTOR pathway

Measurable or nonmeasurable bone only disease
Archival tumor tissue will be collected at baseline
Patients were stratified by:
previous treatment with any CDK4/6i
presence of visceral metastases
region
Randomization (R 1:1:1) leads to three arms:
Arm A: Imlunestrant 400 mg PO QD
Arm B: Investigator's choice endocrine therapy (Fulvestrant or Exemestane) Per labelled dose
Arm C: Imlunestrant 400 mg PO QD + Abemaciclib 150 mg PO BID
Enrollment to Arm C Starts with Amendment A.

The connections are labeled with probabilities: H1 to H4 (1, dotted line), H1 to H3 (0.1), H1 to H2 (0.7), H2 to H3 (0.3 - epsilon), H2 to H6 (0.9 - epsilon), H3 to H6 (1, dotted line).

L1933: Figure 8.13: Tipping-point Analysis for Withdrawal of Consent on OS in the ESR1m Population (Imlunestrant versus TPC). The graph plots OS HR with 95% CI (Y-axis, ranging from 0.4 to 1.0) against Assumed % Hazard Increase in Imlunestrant Arm (X-axis, ranging from 0 to 220). The HR Estimate (solid line with dots) starts around 0.55 at 0% hazard increase and increases slightly to about 0.65 at 220% hazard increase. The 95% CI (dashed lines) starts around 0.8 to 1.0 at 0% hazard increase and increases slightly to about 0.9 to 1.0 at 220% hazard increase. A horizontal dashed line at HR=1.0 is also shown.
L1934: Figure 19.1 displays four plots assessing the goodness-of-fit for the Imlunestrant popPK final model.
The top row shows scatter plots of the dependent variable (DV, imlunestrant concentration in ng/mL) versus predicted concentrations. The left plot compares DV (ng/mL) against PRED (population predicted imlunestrant concentration in ng/mL). The right plot compares DV (ng/mL) against IPRED (individual predicted imlunestrant concentration in ng/mL). Both plots show data points clustered around the 45-degree line (red loess fit), indicating a good fit.
The bottom row shows scatter plots of the conditional weighted residual (CWRES) versus time or predicted concentration. The left plot shows CWRES versus PRED (ng/mL). The right plot shows CWRES versus Time from first dose (h). Both residual plots show CWRES values tightly clustered around the horizontal line at 0.0, indicating that the model adequately captures the variability in the data.
L1935: Figure 19.2 displays three panels showing the visual predictive check (VPC) of the popPK model for imlunestrant concentration (ng/mL) versus time from dose (h) for three populations: Healthy Participants, mBC, and EEC. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 3 to 300 ng/mL. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 192 hours. Observed data is represented by solid black lines (median) and dotted black lines (5th and 95th percentiles). The model-predicted 95% confidence interval is shown by shaded blue areas, representing the 5th, 50th (median), and 95th percentiles. The Healthy Participants panel shows a rapid decline in concentration. The mBC and EEC panels show more complex, fluctuating concentration profiles, particularly around 48 hours.
L1936: Figure 19.3 is a line graph showing simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) profiles of imlunestrant concentration over time (day) for three different body weights (WT). The Y-axis represents Predicted imlunestrant concentration (ng/mL), ranging from 0 to 500. The X-axis represents Time (day), ranging from 1 to 14. The legend indicates three profiles:
WT = 48.5 kg (Blue line)
WT = 68.2 kg (Orange line)
WT = 100.4 kg (Black line)
The solid lines represent the median prediction, and the shaded/hatched areas represent the 90th prediction intervals based on interindividual variability.
L1937: Figure 19.4: Simulated imlunestrant PK profiles at 400 mg QD for patients and healthy participants in the pooled analysis population. The graph plots Predicted imlunestrant concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 400) against Time (day) on the X-axis (1 to 14). Three profiles are shown: Healthy (orange), mBC (black), and EEC (blue). Solid lines represent the median prediction, and shaded/hatched areas represent 90th prediction intervals based on interindividual variability. All profiles show a cyclical pattern of concentration, peaking around day 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, and 14, and troughing around day 2, 5, 7, 9, 11, and 13. The EEC profile generally maintains the highest concentration, followed by mBC, and then Healthy.
L1938: Four Kaplan-Meier plots showing Progression-Free Survival (PFS) versus Time (months) for two groups (0 and 1). The plots compare observed data (lines) with 95% prediction intervals (shaded areas) of simulated data. The legend indicates Group 0 (red/pink) and Group 1 (teal/blue).
L1939: Figure 19.6: Logistic regression model for CBR. The plot shows the Probability of CBR (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Average imlunestrant concentration (ng/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 400). The solid black line and grey shaded area represent the logistic regression and 95% CIs of the predicted probability of CBR. Black open squares represent individual observed CBR in imlunestrant-treated patients. Black circles with error bars show the observed CBR and 95% CI for each exposure quartile. A box plot below the main graph shows the 25th, 50th, and 75th percentiles of predicted average imlunestrant concentration for a 400-mg dose, with whiskers representing 1.5 times the interquartile range.
L1940: Figure 19.7 displays four Visual Predictive Check (VPC) graphs for the time-to-event model of Progression-Free Survival (PFS). The Y-axis represents Progression-Free Survival (%) and the X-axis represents Time (months). The lines represent the Kaplan-Meier plots of the observed data, and the shaded areas represent the 95% prediction intervals of the simulated data.
Top Left Graph: Average concentrations below/above median. Shows a single Kaplan-Meier curve (blue) decreasing from 100% survival at 0 months to approximately 20% survival at 28 months.
Top Right Graph: Presence/absence of Abemaciclib. Compares survival for patients receiving Abemaciclib (Yes, orange) versus those who did not (No, blue). Both curves start at 100% survival. The Abemaciclib group shows slightly better survival, reaching approximately 20% survival at 28 months, compared to approximately 25% survival for the No group.
Bottom Left Graph: Presence/absence of Visceral Metastases. Compares survival for patients with Visceral Metastases (Yes, orange) versus those without (No, blue). Both curves start at 100% survival. The No group shows slightly better survival, reaching approximately 25% survival at 28 months, compared to approximately 20% survival for the Yes group.
Bottom Right Graph: Prior CDK4/6i Therapy. Compares survival for patients with Prior CDK4/6i Therapy (Yes, orange) versus those without (No, blue). Both curves start at 100% survival. The No group shows slightly better survival, reaching approximately 25% survival at 28 months, compared to approximately 20% survival for the Yes group.
L1941: Scatter plot showing Progression-Free Survival (PFS) in months (Y-axis, 0 to 30) versus Average concentration in ng/mL (X-axis, 0 to 400). The plot displays numerous black dots representing observations. A blue line indicates the linear regression, and a grey shaded area represents the 95% confidence interval around the regression line.
Abbreviation: PFS = progression-free survival.
Note: Black dots represent observations, blue line is linear regression line as visual aid and grey area is 95% confidence interval around linear regression.
L1942: Figure 19.9 displays four exposure-response (E-R) plots showing the relationship between average imlunestrant concentration (ng/mL) on the x-axis and the probability of a Treatment-Emergent Adverse Event (TEAE) on the y-axis (ranging from 0.00 to 1.00). The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid, corresponding to four safety endpoints: Nausea (Top Left), Diarrhea (Top Right), Fatigue (Bottom Left), and Serious TEAEs (Bottom Right).
In all four plots, the solid black line represents the logistic regression curve, and the grey shaded area represents the 95% Confidence Interval (CI). Black open squares represent the individual observed TEAEs in imlunestrant-treated patients. Black circles with black error bars represent the observed response rate and 95% CI for each exposure quartile, plotted against the average imlunestrant concentration. Box plots below the x-axis show the distribution of predicted average imlunestrant concentration for a 400-mg dose, with whiskers extending 1.5 times the interquartile range.
The plots for Nausea, Diarrhea, and Fatigue show a decreasing trend in the probability of TEAE as average imlunestrant concentration increases, suggesting a reverse E-R relationship for these events. The plot for Serious TEAEs shows a flat probability curve, indicating no significant relationship between exposure and serious TEAEs.
